## Part XVIII. Fredholm theory in polyfolds (HWZ Chapter 18, §§18.1–18.6)

Chapter 18 (Springer 2021, pp. 843–861; 2017 numbers in parentheses) carries the sc-Fredholm theory of Chapters 12–15 to a strong polyfold bundle $P : E \to C$ over a GCT $C$ with a polyfold construction (Chapter 17). The method is uniform: a notion on $E$ or $C$ is the corresponding notion on an sc-smooth model $W_{\bar\Psi} \to X_\Psi$ of a covering family of strong bundle uniformizers, read through the equivalences $\bar\Gamma : W_{\bar\Psi} \to E$ and $\Gamma : X_\Psi \to C$, and a theorem shows that the choice of the model does not matter. §18.1 defines section functors and sc-Fredholm section functors (Definition 18.1.1, Remark 18.1.2 (2017: Definition 18.1, Remark 18.1)), the linearization and the set of linearizations (Definitions 18.1.3, 18.1.4 (2017: 18.2, 18.3)), proves the implicit function theorem (Theorem 18.1.5, Remark 18.1.6 (2017: Theorem 18.1, Remark 18.2)) and treats auxiliary norms and their reflexive forms (Definitions 18.1.7, 18.1.9–18.1.11, Lemma 18.1.8, Theorem 18.1.12 (2017: Definitions 18.4–18.7, Lemma 18.1, Theorem 18.2)). §18.2 treats compactness (Definitions 18.2.1, 18.2.3, 18.2.4, Remarks 18.2.5, 18.2.7, Theorems 18.2.2, 18.2.6 (2017: Definitions 18.8–18.10, Remarks 18.3, 18.4, Theorems 18.3, 18.4)). §18.3 defines sc⁺-multisection functors and structurability (Definitions 18.3.1, 18.3.3, Remark 18.3.2, Theorems 18.3.5, 18.3.6 (2017: Definitions 18.11, 18.12, Remark 18.5, Theorems 18.5, 18.6); Remark 18.3.4 is new in the 2021 edition). §18.4 constructs them and extends them from the boundary (Definition 18.4.1, Remark 18.4.2, Proposition 18.4.3, Theorem 18.4.4 (2017: Definition 18.13, Remark 18.6, Proposition 18.1, Theorem 18.7)). §18.5 defines the orientation category $O_f$ and orientations of sc-Fredholm section functors (Definitions 18.5.1, 18.5.2, 18.5.4, Remarks 18.5.3, 18.5.5, Proposition 18.5.6 (2017: Definitions 18.14–18.16, Remarks 18.7, 18.8, Proposition 18.2)). §18.6 states the perturbation theorem (Theorem 18.6.1 (2017: Theorem 18.8)) and Theorem 15.3.9 in the polyfold context.

This Part follows the book's order. §§XVIII.1–XVIII.11 treat §18.1: section functors, their local representatives, the model section and functors on $E$ (§XVIII.1), Definition 18.1.1 and Remark 18.1.2 (§XVIII.2), the linearization (§XVIII.3), $\mathrm{Lin}(f, \alpha)$ (§XVIII.4), (18.2) and $\varphi_*$ (§XVIII.5), the full subcategory $C_U$ (§XVIII.6), good position to the partial cone (§XVIII.7), Theorem 18.1.5 with Remark 18.1.6 (§XVIII.8), auxiliary norms and Lemma 18.1.8 (§XVIII.9), reflexive fibres and reflexive auxiliary norms (§XVIII.10) and Theorem 18.1.12 (§XVIII.11). §§XVIII.12–XVIII.14 treat §18.2: the solution set and Theorem 18.2.2 (§XVIII.12), controlling compactness and Remark 18.2.5 (§XVIII.13), Theorem 18.2.6 and Remark 18.2.7 (§XVIII.14). §§XVIII.15–XVIII.17 treat §18.3: sc⁺-multisection functors and the pointwise norm (§XVIII.15), structurability, sums, products and Theorem 18.3.5 (§XVIII.16), and Theorem 18.3.6 (§XVIII.17). §§XVIII.18–XVIII.20 treat §18.4: partitions of unity and bump functions (§XVIII.18), Proposition 18.4.3 (§XVIII.19) and Theorem 18.4.4 (§XVIII.20). §§XVIII.21–XVIII.23 treat §18.5: $O_f$ and Definition 18.5.1 (§XVIII.21), the continuity property (§XVIII.22), and orientations with Proposition 18.5.6 (§XVIII.23). §§XVIII.24–XVIII.26 treat §18.6: Theorem 18.6.1 (§XVIII.24), its orientation clause (§XVIII.25) and Theorem 15.3.9 in the polyfold context (§XVIII.26). Then come the witness bundles (§XVIII.27), the hypotheses and their witnesses (§XVIII.28), what is not formalized (§XVIII.29) and what the formalization taught us (§XVIII.30).

**What is proved, and what is not.** All six sections of Chapter 18 are formalized, except the sentence of §18.2 that a solution object is smooth (as a statement on objects), the category $\widehat{Gr}_F(C)$ of p. 856, the "moreover tame" of Theorem 15.3.9 in the polyfold context, and the two exercises at the end of the chapter.

*Formalized as printed.* Section functors and their local representatives (§XVIII.1). Definition 18.1.1 and Remark 18.1.2 (a), (b) (§XVIII.2). (18.2) and the functoriality of $\varphi_*$ (§XVIII.5). The full subcategory $C_U$ with its polyfold construction (§XVIII.6). Remark 18.1.6 (§XVIII.8). Definitions 18.1.9 and 18.1.10 with the paragraph after Definition 18.1.9, and the paragraph before Theorem 18.1.12 (§§XVIII.10, XVIII.11). Definitions 18.2.1, 18.2.3 with the observation after it, and 18.2.4 (§§XVIII.12, XVIII.13). All of §18.3: Definitions 18.3.1, 18.3.3, Remarks 18.3.2, 18.3.4, the pointwise norm $N(\Lambda)$, sums and products, Theorem 18.3.5 (§§XVIII.15, XVIII.16). Definition 18.4.1, Remark 18.4.2 and Proposition 18.4.3 with the construction before it (§§XVIII.18, XVIII.19). The section functors of $\sigma$ and their obstruction from isotropy, the push-forward $\bar\Psi_*$ with the germ identity, and Definition 18.5.4 (§§XVIII.21–XVIII.23). Where the text calls a statement an exercise, easy, or left to the reader, it is proved.

*Restated.* The model section and the change of model, which the text uses throughout without stating them (§XVIII.1). Definition 18.1.4 with $s$ a local sc⁺-section near $q_0$, as the text says, and "$f_{\bar\Psi} - s$" read with $s(q_0) = f_{\bar\Psi}(q_0)$ (§XVIII.4). Good position to the partial cone, stated on $T_\alpha C$ and shown equivalent to the model condition of Chapter 3 (§XVIII.7). The solution category $S_f$ as the set of zeros, with its orbit space in $|C|$ (§XVIII.12). Theorem 18.3.6, with the sc-smooth strong bundle proper covering functor of §17.8 read through models, as the text indicates on p. 842 (§XVIII.17). Definition 18.5.2, with $\bar\Gamma^* o$ read as $\bar\Psi^* o$ and "related by continuation" as Definition 6.6.12 (§XVIII.22).

*Stronger.* Remark 18.5.3, both reductions as equivalences (§XVIII.22); Proposition 18.5.6 (3) as an equivalence (§XVIII.23).

*Explicit.* Remark 18.1.2 (c), whose direction "⇒" uses $\bar F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ (§XVIII.2). Definition 18.1.3, stated on level $1$, where the linearization of a representative is defined, with its independence of $\bar\Psi$ proved (§XVIII.3). Theorem 18.1.5 with the germ data of $f$ in a tame chart, conclusion (1) on $C_U$ and conclusion (2) at the zeros in the model (§XVIII.8). Definition 18.1.7 read with the continuity of Definition 12.2.1 (§XVIII.9). Definition 18.1.11, its two forms shown equivalent, and its comparison with Definition 18.1.10 for reflexive fibres (§XVIII.10). Theorem 18.1.12, with the norm over $\partial C$ in (3) given through a model, as Theorem 12.2.13 takes it (§XVIII.11). Theorem 18.2.2 with $\bar F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ (§XVIII.12). Remark 18.2.5, from the lower semicontinuity of a reflexive auxiliary norm, without local compactness (§XVIII.13). Theorem 18.2.6 (§XVIII.14). Theorem 18.4.4, with "admits sc-smooth partitions of unity" read on the object M-polyfold of a model, the way Theorem 14.1.9 uses it, in a model and free of the model (§XVIII.20). The orientation category $O_f$, read through the determinant of Chapter 12 in a reference model, over a tame $C$ with sc-smooth bump functions as in Theorem 6.6.17 (§XVIII.21). Remark 18.5.5 with a topology on the orientation category of the reference model as in Theorem 6.6.17 (§XVIII.23). The paragraph before Theorem 18.6.1 and Theorem 18.6.1, with "sc-smooth section functor" read as sc-Fredholm, as the preamble of §18.6 says, and the germ data in a tame chart; (3) and (4) are shown independent of the model (§XVIII.24). The orientation clause of Theorem 18.6.1, with the Fredholm index on the support as a hypothesis, tacit in the text through Theorem 15.4.3 (§XVIII.25). Theorem 15.3.9 in the polyfold context, without the "moreover tame", as in Chapter 15 (§XVIII.26).

*Narrower.* Lemma 18.1.8: with the continuity of Definition 12.2.1 in Definition 18.1.7 it holds as an equivalence; for Definition 18.1.7 as printed the direction "⇒" does not hold as we read it (§XVIII.9). The printed clauses do not imply that $|N|$ is continuous on $|E_{0,1}|$, and a norm on the reflection bundle rescaled by a discontinuous function of the base satisfies them but is not an auxiliary norm in the model. The text's own phrase about importing the notion from the ep-groupoid context supports the reading with continuity, which is the one the chapter uses.

*Commentary.* Remark 18.2.7 is commentary; its two steps are Theorems 18.1.12 (3) and 18.2.6 (§XVIII.14). *Tools and witnesses.* The functors on $E$ read as invariant functions (§XVIII.1) and the two witness bundles (§XVIII.27).

*Not formalized.* The sentence before Definition 18.2.1 that a solution object is smooth, as a statement on objects (its step on levels is Remark 18.1.2 (b), §XVIII.2); the category $\widehat{Gr}_F(C)$ of p. 856; the "moreover tame" of Theorem 15.3.9 in the polyfold context; the exercises on Theorems 5.3.12 and 5.3.15.

*Points* (§XVIII.29). G.235–G.256. Settled: G.235–G.243, G.245, G.246, G.248, G.250–G.254, G.256. Nothing to formalize: G.244, G.247, G.249 (invitations and comments) and G.255 (exercises).

**The decisions that shape the chapter.**

(1) *Everything through sc-smooth models.* Every notion of the chapter is a notion of Chapters 12–15 for the strong bundle $W_{\bar\Psi} \to X_\Psi$ of a covering family, transported along $\bar\Gamma$. Each comes with the theorem that it holds in one model iff it holds in every model, proved with the comparison generalized strong bundle isomorphisms $[\bar f_{c,c'}]$ of Chapter 17 and with the principle that a section functor, or an invariant function, of a strong bundle over an ep-groupoid is determined by what it does on orbits. Where the text says "the specific model does not matter", this theorem is the content.

(2) *Functors to sets are invariant functions.* A functor $N : E \to [0, \infty]$ or $\Lambda : E \to \mathbb Q^+$ is a functor into a discrete category, hence a function on objects constant along morphisms; this is the reading of Chapters 12–13, used for auxiliary norms and multisection functors alike (§XVIII.1). Where the text prints $N : C \to [0, \infty]$ (Lemma 18.1.8, Definition 18.2.4, Theorem 18.2.6) or $|C_{0,1}|$ (Definition 18.1.7 (3)), we read $E$.

(3) *Standing readings.* $\bar F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ for every object, the bundle form of Chapter 17's $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$; the germ data of the representatives in a tame chart, the standing reading of Chapters 3 to 15, wherever a theorem of Chapter 15 or Theorem 6.6.17 is invoked (Theorems 18.1.5 and 18.6.1, §18.5); "sc-smooth" in Theorem 18.6.1 as sc-Fredholm, from the preamble of §18.6.

(4) *Auxiliary norms with continuity.* Definition 18.1.7 is read with the continuity of $|N|$ on $|E_{0,1}|$ of Definition 12.2.1, which Lemma 18.1.8 needs; the printed form is formalized too, and the comparison is exact (§XVIII.9).

(5) *Orientations through a reference model.* The two orientations at an object $\alpha$ are those of the determinant line of Chapter 12 of $f_{\bar\Psi}$ at the point over $\alpha$, for a reference uniformizer, and the push-forward along morphisms goes through that model. Theorem 6.6.17, which makes the determinant and its continuation available, needs a tame base with sc-smooth bump functions; §18.5 takes these hypotheses (§XVIII.21).

(6) *Added hypotheses are named and witnessed.* Where the text's argument reduces to a theorem of Chapters 12–15 that carries a hypothesis the polyfold statement does not print, the hypothesis is added, named and witnessed (§XVIII.28): the transported sc⁺-sections of the global form of Definition 18.1.4, partitions of unity on the object M-polyfold in Theorems 18.4.4 and 15.3.9, the index on the support in the orientation clause of Theorem 18.6.1, and the §18.5 hypotheses of Theorem 6.6.17. Two witness bundles carry the examples: the reflection bundle over $\mathbb Z/2 \ltimes \mathbb R$, with isotropy acting non-trivially on the fibre and $\partial C = \emptyset$, and the half-space bundle over $\mathbb Z/2 \ltimes ([0, \infty) \times \mathbb R)$, with boundary and a corner with isotropy $\mathbb Z/2$ (§XVIII.27).

*The half-space bundle.*
- `Chapter18/HalfSpaceBundleConstruction`, `Chapter18/HalfSpaceWitnesses`, `Chapter18/HalfSpaceBoundaryData`: the strong polyfold bundle construction with $\partial C \ne \emptyset$ and isotropy $\mathbb Z/2$, and the boundary witnesses of §§18.1–18.6 (§XVIII.27 and throughout).

Throughout, $C$ is a GCT with a polyfold construction $S = (F, \mathbf M)$, $P : E \to C$ the bundle given by $\mu : C \to \mathrm{Ban}$, and $Q = (\bar F, \mathbf M)$ a strong polyfold bundle construction on it (Chapter 17). A strong bundle uniformizer $\bar\Psi \in \bar F(\alpha)$ is a strong bundle $p : K \to O$ with a functor $\bar\Psi : G \ltimes K \to E$ covering a uniformizer $\Psi : G \ltimes O \to C$ with $\Psi(q_0) = \alpha$; for a family $\bar{\boldsymbol\Psi}$ the sc-smooth model is $W_{\bar\Psi} \to X_\Psi$ with $\bar\Gamma$ and $\Gamma$, and for a covering family both are equivalences. Two covering families $c, c'$ are compared by the generalized strong bundle isomorphism $[\bar f_{c, c'}] : W_c \to W_{c'}$ with $|\bar\Gamma_{c'}| \circ |\bar f_{c,c'}| = |\bar\Gamma_c|$ (Chapter 17).

### XVIII.1 Section functors, local representatives and the model section (2017: §18.1), pp. 843–844

Throughout, $C$ is a GCT with a polyfold construction $S = (F, \mathbf M)$ (Part XVII), $\mu : C \to \mathrm{Ban}$ defines the bundle $P : E \to C$, and $Q = (\bar F, \mathbf M)$ is a strong polyfold bundle construction on $E$ (Definition 17.7.4). A strong bundle uniformizer $\bar\Psi \in \bar F(\alpha)$ covers a uniformizer $\Psi : G \ltimes O \to C$ with $\Psi(q_0) = \alpha$, and is a strong bundle $p : K \to O$ with a functor $\bar\Psi : G \ltimes K \to E$ which on each fibre is a Banach space isomorphism $K_q \to P^{-1}(\Psi(q))$. For a family $\bar{\boldsymbol\Psi}$ of strong bundle uniformizers, $W_{\bar\Psi} \to X_\Psi$ is the strong bundle model of Part XVII, with the functors $\Gamma : X_\Psi \to C$ and $\bar\Gamma : W_{\bar\Psi} \to E$; for a covering family (a family whose base family covers $|C|$) both are equivalences.

**Statement** (same). *Section functors* (pp. 843–844) ⟦Chapter18/SectionFunctors · SectionFunctor⟧: a section functor of $P : E \to C$ is an object map $\alpha \mapsto f(\alpha) \in P^{-1}(\alpha)$ with $f(t\varphi) = \mu(\varphi)(f(s\varphi))$ for every morphism $\varphi$. It is a functor $f : C \to E$ ⟦Chapter18/SectionFunctors · SectionFunctor.toGroupoidFunctor⟧, sending $\varphi$ to $(\varphi, f(s\varphi))$ ⟦Chapter18/SectionFunctors · SectionFunctor.morMap⟧, with $P \circ f = \mathrm{Id}$ on objects and on morphisms ⟦Chapter18/SectionFunctors · SectionFunctor.proj_obj_toGroupoidFunctor⟧ ⟦Chapter18/SectionFunctors · SectionFunctor.proj_mor_toGroupoidFunctor⟧, and $f(\alpha') = \mu(\varphi)(f(\alpha))$ for $\varphi : \alpha \to \alpha'$ ⟦Chapter18/SectionFunctors · SectionFunctor.toFun_eq_act⟧. *The local representative* (p. 844) ⟦Chapter18/SectionFunctors · StrongBundleUniformizerData.localRep⟧: for $\bar\Psi$ covering $\Psi$, $f_{\bar\Psi} : O \to K$ is a section of $p$ ⟦Chapter18/SectionFunctors · StrongBundleUniformizerData.p_localRep⟧ with $\bar\Psi \circ f_{\bar\Psi} = f \circ \Psi$ ⟦Chapter18/SectionFunctors · StrongBundleUniformizerData.obj_localRep⟧, it is $G$-equivariant ⟦Chapter18/SectionFunctors · StrongBundleUniformizerData.localRep_smul⟧, and the representatives of two uniformizers are related by the transition maps: $f_{\bar\Psi'}(q') = T(a, f_{\bar\Psi}(q))$ for $a = (q, \varphi, q') \in \mathbf M(\Psi, \Psi')$ ⟦Chapter18/SectionFunctors · StrongBundleUniformizerData.localRep_transAct⟧.

*Reading.* A functor $f : C \to E$ with $P \circ f = \mathrm{Id}$ is determined by its object map, since $f(\varphi)$ must be $(\varphi, f(s\varphi))$; the structure records the object map and the condition that this rule is functorial. $f_{\bar\Psi}(q)$ is the unique $k \in K_q$ with $\bar\Psi(k) = f(\Psi(q))$ ⟦Chapter18/SectionFunctors · StrongBundleUniformizerData.localRep_eq_iff⟧, which exists because $\bar\Psi$ is a Banach space isomorphism on the fibre (Definition 17.7.2 (2)).

**Proof that $f$ is a functor.** $s(f(\varphi)) = f(s\varphi)$ by definition, and $t(f(\varphi)) = f(t\varphi)$ is the functoriality condition. For composable $\varphi, \psi$, the morphisms $f(\varphi)$, $f(\psi)$ are composable in $E$ because $f(s\varphi) = f(t\psi)$, and the composite of $E$ is $(\varphi\psi, f(s\psi))$ with $s(\varphi\psi) = s\psi$; this is $f(\varphi\psi)$. The unit: $f(1_\alpha) = (1_\alpha, f(\alpha))$, the unit of $E$ at $f(\alpha)$, since $P(f(\alpha)) = \alpha$.

**Proof of equivariance and of the transition law.** Both go through the uniqueness of $f_{\bar\Psi}(q)$. *Equivariance.* $\bar\Psi(g \ast f_{\bar\Psi}(q))$ is $\mu(\Psi(g, q))$ applied to $\bar\Psi(f_{\bar\Psi}(q)) = f(\Psi(q))$, because $\bar\Psi$ is a functor on $G \ltimes K$ over $\Psi$; by functoriality of $f$ along the morphism $\Psi(g, q) : \Psi(q) \to \Psi(g \ast q)$ this is $f(\Psi(g \ast q))$. So $g \ast f_{\bar\Psi}(q)$ is the point of $K_{g \ast q}$ that $\bar\Psi$ sends to $f(\Psi(g \ast q))$, which is $f_{\bar\Psi}(g \ast q)$. *Transition law.* $\bar\Psi'(T(a, k)) = \mu(\varphi)(\bar\Psi(k))$ for $a = (q, \varphi, q')$ ⟦Chapter17/StrongBundleStructures · StrongBundleUniformizerData.obj_transAct⟧; with $k = f_{\bar\Psi}(q)$ this is $\mu(\varphi)(f(\Psi(q))) = f(\Psi'(q'))$, so $T(a, f_{\bar\Psi}(q)) = f_{\bar\Psi'}(q')$.

**Statement** (restated). *The model section* (p. 844) ⟦Chapter18/SectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.modelSection⟧: on the model of a family $\bar{\boldsymbol\Psi} = (\bar\Psi_\lambda)$, $f^{\bar\Psi}(\lambda, q) = (\lambda, f_{\bar\Psi_\lambda}(q))$. It is a section of $W_{\bar\Psi} \to X_\Psi$ ⟦Chapter18/SectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isSection_modelSection⟧ with $\bar\Gamma \circ f^{\bar\Psi} = f \circ \Gamma$ ⟦Chapter18/SectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.bundleModelFunctor_obj_modelSection⟧, the only such section ⟦Chapter18/SectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.modelSection_eq_of⟧; it is a section functor of the strong bundle $W_{\bar\Psi} \to X_\Psi$ over an ep-groupoid in the sense of Definition 8.3.4 ⟦Chapter18/SectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isFunctorialSection_modelSection⟧; and $|f| \circ |\Gamma| = |\bar\Gamma| \circ |f^{\bar\Psi}|$ ⟦Chapter18/SectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.orbitMap_modelSection⟧, the orbit identity of (18.3). Along the inclusion $W_{\bar\Psi} \to W_{\bar\Phi}$ of a subfamily whose base family covers, the model sections correspond by pull-back and push-forward ⟦Chapter18/SectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.pullback_modelSection⟧ ⟦Chapter18/SectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.pushforward_modelSection⟧. Along the comparison generalized strong bundle isomorphism $[\bar f_{c, c'}] : W_c \to W_{c'}$ of two covering families (Part XVII) ⟦Chapter17/BundleModelComparison · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.bundleComparison⟧, for sc-smooth $f$, $[\bar f_{c,c'}]_* f^c = f^{c'}$ ⟦Chapter18/ModelSectionPushforward · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.pushforward_bundleComparison_modelSection⟧ and $[\bar f_{c,c'}]^* f^{c'} = f^c$ ⟦Chapter18/ModelSectionPushforward · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.pullback_bundleComparison_modelSection⟧. The text uses the model section and its independence of the model throughout the chapter without stating them; they are stated and proved here.

**Proof that $f^{\bar\Psi}$ is a section functor.** *Step 1, uniqueness.* $\bar\Gamma$ is injective on the pairs (base point, image) ⟦Chapter17/BundleModel · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.bundleModelObj_injective⟧: two points of $W_{\bar\Psi}$ over the same point of $X_\Psi$ with the same image in $E$ coincide, because each $\bar\Psi_\lambda$ is injective on fibres. This gives $f^{\bar\Psi} = s$ for every section $s$ with $\bar\Gamma \circ s = f \circ \Gamma$. *Step 2, the functorial condition.* For a morphism $\varphi$ of $X_\Psi$ one must show $f^{\bar\Psi}(t\varphi) = \mu_W(\varphi, f^{\bar\Psi}(s\varphi))$. Both sides lie over $t\varphi$, so by Step 1 it suffices to compare their images under $\bar\Gamma$. The left side goes to $f(\Gamma(t\varphi))$. The right side goes to $\mu(\Gamma\varphi)$ applied to $\bar\Gamma(f^{\bar\Psi}(s\varphi)) = f(\Gamma(s\varphi))$, since $\bar\Gamma$ is a functor; by functoriality of $f$ along $\Gamma\varphi$ this is $f(t(\Gamma\varphi)) = f(\Gamma(t\varphi))$. *Step 3, the orbit identity* is $\bar\Gamma \circ f^{\bar\Psi} = f \circ \Gamma$ passed to orbits.

**Proof of the pull-back along inclusions.** For $\bar{\boldsymbol\Psi}' = \bar{\boldsymbol\Phi} \circ \sigma$, the inclusion $i : W_{\bar\Psi'} \to W_{\bar\Phi}$ sends $f^{\bar\Psi'}(x)$ to $f^{\bar\Phi}(i(x))$ by definition ⟦Chapter18/SectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.modelSection_comp_incl⟧. When the base family of $\bar{\boldsymbol\Psi}'$ covers, $i$ is a strong bundle equivalence ⟦Chapter17/BundleModelComparison · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.equivalences_bundleIncl⟧, and the pull-back of a section along a strong bundle equivalence is characterized pointwise by this relation, so $i^* f^{\bar\Phi} = f^{\bar\Psi'}$. The push-forward is the inverse of the pull-back on section functors (Part X), which gives $i_* f^{\bar\Psi'} = f^{\bar\Phi}$.

**Proof of $[\bar f_{c,c'}]_* f^c = f^{c'}$.** *Step 1.* $f^c$ is an sc-smooth section functor of $W_c \to X_c$, because $f$ is sc-smooth (Remark 18.1.2 (a), §XVIII.2) ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.remark_18_1_2_a_model_smooth⟧, so the push-forward of Theorem 10.5.4 (Part X) along the generalized isomorphism ⟦Chapter17/BundleModelComparison · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isGeneralizedIso_bundleComparison⟧ is defined, and is a section functor of $W_{c'} \to X_{c'}$ ⟦Chapter10/StrongBundleLocalizationSections · GenSBMap.theorem_10_5_4⟧. *Step 2, the orbit identity of the push-forward.* For $x' \in X_{c'}$ there is $x \in X_c$ with $|\bar\Gamma_{c'}|(|([\bar f]_* f^c)(x')|) = |\bar\Gamma_c|(|f^c(x)|)$ ⟦Chapter18/ModelSectionPushforward · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.orbitMap_pushforward_bundleComparison⟧: one takes $x$ from a representing diagram of $[\bar f]$, and the orbit maps of the diagram compose to the orbit map of $[\bar f]$, which is $|\bar\Gamma_{c'}|^{-1} \circ |\bar\Gamma_c|$ ⟦Chapter17/BundleModelComparison · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.orbitMap_bundleComparison⟧. *Step 3, the base points.* Projecting the identity of Step 2 to $C$ ⟦Chapter18/ModelSectionPushforward · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.π_base_eq_of_π_eq⟧ gives $|\Gamma_{c'}(x')| = |\Gamma_c(x)|$, and then $|f(\Gamma_{c'}(x'))| = |f(\Gamma_c(x))|$ ⟦Chapter18/ModelSectionPushforward · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.π_toFun_eq_of_π_eq⟧. With the orbit identity of $f^c$ and of $f^{c'}$ this gives $|\bar\Gamma_{c'}|(|f^{c'}(x')|) = |\bar\Gamma_{c'}|(|([\bar f]_* f^c)(x')|)$. *Step 4.* $\bar\Gamma_{c'}$ is full ⟦Chapter17/BundleModel · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.bundleModelFunctor_full⟧, so its orbit map is injective ⟦Chapter10/Equivalence · injective_orbitMap_of_full⟧, and $f^{c'}(x')$, $([\bar f]_* f^c)(x')$ have the same orbit. *Step 5.* Two section functors of a strong bundle over an ep-groupoid with the same orbits at every point are equal ⟦Chapter10/StrongBundleLocalizationSections · EpStrongBundle.eq_of_forall_π_eq⟧. The pull-back form follows, since $[\bar f]^*$ is the inverse of $[\bar f]_*$ (Theorem 10.5.4).

**Statement** (extra). *Functors on $E$ and their transfer* (§§18.1–18.6, pp. 846–861). A function $N : E \to \beta$ is *morphism-invariant* if $N(t a) = N(s a)$ for every morphism $a$ of $E$ ⟦Chapter18/ModelTransfer · IsMorInvariant⟧; it is then constant on isomorphism classes ⟦Chapter18/ModelTransfer · IsMorInvariant.eq_of_isomorphic⟧ and induces $|N| : |E| \to \beta$ ⟦Chapter18/ModelTransfer · orbitFun⟧. Its pull-back $N \circ \bar\Gamma$ ⟦Chapter18/ModelTransfer · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.bundlePull⟧ is invariant along the morphisms of $W_{\bar\Psi}$ ⟦Chapter18/ModelTransfer · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.bundlePull_μ⟧ ⟦Chapter18/ModelTransfer · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.bundlePull_eq_of_isomorphic⟧, with $|N \circ \bar\Gamma| = |N| \circ |\bar\Gamma|$ ⟦Chapter18/ModelTransfer · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.bundleOrbitFun⟧ ⟦Chapter18/ModelTransfer · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.orbitFun_bundlePull⟧, and for two covering families $|N \circ \bar\Gamma_c| = |N \circ \bar\Gamma_{c'}| \circ |\bar f_{c,c'}|$ ⟦Chapter18/ModelTransfer · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.bundlePull_comparison⟧. For a covering family, $|\bar\Gamma|$ is a bijection $|W_{\bar\Psi}| \cong |E|$ ⟦Chapter18/ModelTransfer · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.bundleOrbitEquiv⟧, and an invariant function $N'$ on $W_{\bar\Psi}$ pushes forward to an invariant function on $E$ ⟦Chapter18/ModelTransfer · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.bundlePush⟧ ⟦Chapter18/ModelTransfer · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isMorInvariant_bundlePush⟧; pull-back and push-forward are inverse ⟦Chapter18/ModelTransfer · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.bundlePull_bundlePush⟧ ⟦Chapter18/ModelTransfer · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.bundlePush_bundlePull⟧.

*Reading.* The text's functors $N : E \to [0, \infty]$ (auxiliary norms, §XVIII.9) and $\Lambda : E \to \mathbb Q^+$ (multisection functors, §XVIII.15) are functors into discrete categories, which have only identities; such a functor is a function constant along morphisms. This is the reading of Part III's ep-groupoid chapters, used for every such functor of the chapter.

**Proofs.** Invariance of $N \circ \bar\Gamma$: $\bar\Gamma$ sends a morphism of $W_{\bar\Psi}$ to a morphism of $E$ with the images of its source and target, and $N$ is constant along it. Independence of the model: $|N \circ \bar\Gamma_c| = |N| \circ |\bar\Gamma_c|$ and $|\bar\Gamma_c| = |\bar\Gamma_{c'}| \circ |\bar f_{c,c'}|$ (Part XVII). The bijection $|\bar\Gamma|$ is the homeomorphism of orbit spaces of a covering family ⟦Chapter17/BundleModel · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isHomeomorph_bundleModelOrbitMap⟧. The push-forward sends $e$ to $N'(w)$ for any $w$ with $|\bar\Gamma(w)| = |e|$; invariance along $a$ holds because $s a$ and $t a$ have the same orbit. $\mathrm{pull} \circ \mathrm{push} = \mathrm{Id}$ is $|\bar\Gamma|^{-1} \circ |\bar\Gamma| = \mathrm{Id}$; for $\mathrm{push} \circ \mathrm{pull} = \mathrm{Id}$, given $e$ choose $w$ with $|\bar\Gamma(w)| = |e|$ and use that $N$ is constant on the isomorphism class of $e$.

### XVIII.2 Definition 18.1.1 and Remark 18.1.2 (2017: Definition 18.1, Remark 18.1), p. 844

**Statement** (same). *Definition 18.1.1* ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsScFredholmSectionFunctor⟧: $f$ is an sc-Fredholm section functor if for every object $\alpha$ and every $\bar\Psi \in \bar F(\alpha)$ the representative $f_{\bar\Psi}$ is an sc-Fredholm section of $K \to O$ (Definition 3.1.16). Equivalently, $f_{\bar\Psi}$ is sc-Fredholm for every strong bundle uniformizer of the construction ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isScFredholmSectionFunctor_iff_unif⟧. With the same pattern: $f$ is *sc-smooth* if every $f_{\bar\Psi}$ is ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsScSmoothSectionFunctor⟧, implied by sc-Fredholm ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsScFredholmSectionFunctor.isScSmoothSectionFunctor⟧; *regularizing* if $q \in O_m$ and $f_{\bar\Psi}(q) \in K_{m, m+1}$ imply $q \in O_{m+1}$ ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsRegularizing⟧; and, the tame form, every $f_{\bar\Psi}$ is a tame sc-Fredholm section in the sense of Definition 3.6.5 ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsScFredholmSectionFunctorTame⟧, which implies Definition 18.1.1 ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsScFredholmSectionFunctorTame.toIsScFredholmSectionFunctor⟧. The tame form, the germ data in a tame chart, is a separate definition and enters only where the theorems of Part III's chapters need it (Theorems 18.1.5 and 18.6.1, §18.5). The standing reading $\bar F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ for every object is ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.HasStrongBundleUniformizers⟧, the bundle form of Part XVII's $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$.

**The tool: sections of a disjoint union.** The model $W_{\bar\Psi} \to X_\Psi$ is, as a strong bundle, the disjoint union of the $K_\lambda \to O_\lambda$, and $f^{\bar\Psi}$ is the section $(\lambda, q) \mapsto (\lambda, f_{\bar\Psi_\lambda}(q))$ ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.modelSection_eq_sigmaSection⟧ ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctors · StrongBundle.sigmaSection⟧. *Step 1.* The inclusion of one piece $K_i \to \bigsqcup K_\lambda$ is a strong bundle isomorphism onto its image, on levels $0$ and on the $1$-level bundle ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctors · StrongBundle.isStrongBundleIsoOn_sigmaMk⟧ ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctors · StrongBundle.sigmaMkLocalIso⟧. *Step 2.* On the piece $i$ the section of the union is $f_{\bar\Psi_i}$ transported by this isomorphism, and conversely. *Step 3.* Sc-smoothness is checked piece by piece ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctors · StrongBundle.isScSmoothSection_sigma_iff⟧. Sc-Fredholm germs move along local strong bundle isomorphisms ⟦Chapter5/FredholmPullbackIso · StrongBundle.LocalIso.isScFredholmGerm⟧ (Part V), so a germ of the union at $(i, x)$ is one of $f_{\bar\Psi_i}$ at $x$ ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctors · StrongBundle.isScFredholmGerm_sigma_iff⟧; the regularizing property moves the same way ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctors · StrongBundle.regularizing_sigmaSection⟧. Since Definition 3.1.16 is local ⟦Chapter5/FredholmPullbackIso · isScFredholmSection_of_forall⟧, a section of the union is sc-Fredholm iff every piece is ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctors · StrongBundle.isScFredholmSection_sigma_iff⟧, and the same holds for the tame form ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctors · StrongBundle.isScFredholmSectionTame_sigma_iff⟧. Hence, in a model, $f^{\bar\Psi}$ is an sc-smooth, an sc-Fredholm or a tame sc-Fredholm section functor (Definition 8.3.4) iff every $f_{\bar\Psi_\lambda}$ is sc-smooth, sc-Fredholm, or tame sc-Fredholm ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isScSmoothSectionFunctor_model_iff⟧ ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isScFredholmSectionFunctor_model_iff⟧ ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isScFredholmSectionTame_model_iff⟧; the functorial condition of Definition 8.3.4 is §XVIII.1.

**Statement** (same). *Remark 18.1.2 (a)* ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.remark_18_1_2_a⟧: for a covering family $c$, $f$ is sc-Fredholm iff each $f_{\bar\Psi_\lambda}$, $\bar\Psi_\lambda$ in $c$, is; the same for sc-smooth ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.remark_18_1_2_a_smooth⟧ and for the tame form ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.remark_18_1_2_a_tame⟧. In the model: $f^{c}$ is an sc-Fredholm section functor of $W_c \to X_c$ iff $f$ is sc-Fredholm ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.remark_18_1_2_a_model⟧, with the sc-smooth and tame forms ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.remark_18_1_2_a_model_smooth⟧ ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.remark_18_1_2_a_model_tame⟧; so the property holds in one model iff in every model ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isScFredholm_model_iff_model⟧. The text calls (a) an easy exercise (G.235, settled).

**Proof of the exercise.** Let the representatives of $c$ be sc-Fredholm and let $\bar\Psi'$ be any strong bundle uniformizer. *Step 1.* Adjoin $\bar\Psi'$ to $c$ ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.adjoin⟧. The base family of $c$ already covers, so the inclusion $W_c \to W_{c \sqcup \{\bar\Psi'\}}$ is a strong bundle equivalence ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.equivalences_adjoin⟧, and the push-forward of $f^c$ along it is $f^{c \sqcup \{\bar\Psi'\}}$ ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.modelSection_adjoin⟧ (§XVIII.1). *Step 2.* $f^c$ is an sc-Fredholm section functor by the disjoint-union tool. Theorem 10.4.5 (Part X) carries sc-Fredholm section functors along strong bundle equivalences ⟦Chapter10/FredholmEquivalence · theorem_10_4_5_fred⟧, so $f^{c \sqcup \{\bar\Psi'\}}$ is sc-Fredholm. *Step 3.* $f_{\bar\Psi'}$ is the piece of $f^{c \sqcup \{\bar\Psi'\}}$ at the adjoined index, hence sc-Fredholm ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isScFredholmSection_of_covering⟧. The sc-smooth form uses Theorem 10.4.5 for sc-smooth section functors ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isScSmoothSection_of_covering⟧, and the tame form the push-forward of tame sc-Fredholm sections along an equivalence (Part X) ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isScFredholmSectionTame_of_covering⟧. The converse direction of (a) is immediate: the members of $c$ are strong bundle uniformizers of the construction. The model forms combine (a) with the disjoint-union tool.

**Statement** (same). *Remark 18.1.2 (b)* ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.remark_18_1_2_b⟧: an sc-Fredholm section functor is regularizing; in the levels $C_m$ of Part XVII, if $\alpha \in C_m$ and $f_{\bar\Psi}(q) \in K_{m, m+1}$ for a point $q$ of a strong bundle uniformizer with $\Psi(q) = \alpha$, then $\alpha \in C_{m+1}$ ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.remark_18_1_2_b_levels⟧.

*Reading.* "$\alpha \in C_m$" is the regularity of Part XVII (p. 765); "$f(\alpha) \in E_{m, m+1}$" is read in a strong bundle uniformizer at $\alpha$, as $f_{\bar\Psi}(q) \in K_{m, m+1}$.

**Proof.** Every $f_{\bar\Psi}$ is sc-Fredholm, and an sc-Fredholm section is regularizing (Definition 3.1.16). For the level form, $\alpha \in C_m$ iff $q \in O_m$ for any uniformizer point $q$ over an object isomorphic to $\alpha$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldLevels · PolyfoldConstruction.hasRegularity_iff⟧, so the statement on $C$ is the statement on $O$.

**Statement** (explicit). *Remark 18.1.2 (c)* ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.remark_18_1_2_c⟧: if $\bar F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ for every $\alpha$, then $f$ is sc-Fredholm iff it is sc-smooth, regularizing, and has *Fredholm germs* ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.HasFredholmGerms⟧: over every smooth point $|\alpha|$ of $|C|$ there are $\alpha'$ with $|\alpha'| = |\alpha|$, $\bar\Psi \in \bar F(\alpha')$ and $\bar q$ with $\Psi(\bar q) = \alpha'$ such that $(f_{\bar\Psi}, \bar q)$ is an sc-Fredholm germ (Definition 3.1.11). The direction "⇐" holds without $\bar F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isScFredholmSectionFunctor_of_germs⟧.

*Reading.* The germ condition is read at a representative of each smooth orbit through a uniformizer at that representative; for "⇒" an object over the orbit with a strong bundle uniformizer is needed, which is the standing reading $\bar F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$.

**Proof of the germ transfer** ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isScFredholmGerm_of_isomorphic⟧. Let $(f_{\bar\Psi_0}, q_0)$ be an sc-Fredholm germ and let $\varphi : \Psi_0(q_0) \to \Psi'(q')$ be a morphism of $C$. *Step 1.* Form the model of the two-element family $\{\bar\Psi_0, \bar\Psi'\}$. The triple $\sigma = (q_0, \varphi, q')$ is a morphism of its ep-groupoid from the point $q_0$ of the first piece to the point $q'$ of the second. *Step 2.* In a strong bundle over an ep-groupoid, every morphism $\sigma$ acts by a local strong bundle isomorphism $L$ from a neighbourhood of $t\sigma$ to a neighbourhood of $s\sigma$ with $\varphi_L(t \sigma) = s\sigma$, along which every section functor is its own pull-back ⟦Chapter10/FredholmEquivalence · EpStrongBundle.exists_localIso_mor⟧ (Part X). *Step 3.* The model section $f^{\bar\Psi}$ is a section functor (§XVIII.1). Its germ at $(\text{first}, q_0)$ is the germ of $f_{\bar\Psi_0}$ at $q_0$ (disjoint-union tool), hence sc-Fredholm. Moving it along $L$ ⟦Chapter5/FredholmPullbackIso · StrongBundle.LocalIso.isScFredholmGerm⟧ gives an sc-Fredholm germ of $f^{\bar\Psi}$ at $(\text{second}, q')$, which is the germ of $f_{\bar\Psi'}$ at $q'$.

**Proof of (c).** "⇒": sc-smooth and regularizing by Definition 18.1.1 and (b). For a smooth $\alpha$ choose $\bar\Psi \in \bar F(\alpha)$ (here $\bar F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ enters) and $q$ with $\Psi(q) = \alpha$; $q$ is a smooth point of $O$ because the levels of $q$ are those of $\alpha$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldLevels · PolyfoldConstruction.regularity_eq_of_mem⟧, so Definition 3.1.16 gives the germ. "⇐": for $\bar\Psi \in \bar F(\beta)$ and a smooth $q \in O$, $\Psi(q)$ is a smooth object ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isSmoothObj_obj⟧; the germ condition gives $\alpha_0$ in its orbit with a germ of some $f_{\bar\Psi_0}$ at $q_0$ over $\alpha_0$, and the germ transfer carries it to $(f_{\bar\Psi}, q)$, since $\Psi_0(q_0) = \alpha_0 \cong \Psi(q)$. With sc-smoothness and the regularizing property, $f_{\bar\Psi}$ is sc-Fredholm.

**Witnesses.** The reflection bundle ⟦Chapter17/StrongBundleStructuresExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflQ⟧: $C = \mathbb Z/2 \ltimes \mathbb R$, $\mu(x) = \mathbb R$, the generator $-1$ acting by $x \mapsto -x$ and $v \mapsto -v$, with the diagonal section $f(x) = (x, x)$ ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctorsExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflDiag⟧, whose representative is the graph section of the identity ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctorsExamples · ReflectionBundle.localRep_reflDiag⟧. It satisfies Definition 18.1.1 ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctorsExamples · ReflectionBundle.isScFredholmSectionFunctor_reflDiag⟧ and its tame form ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctorsExamples · ReflectionBundle.isScFredholmSectionFunctorTame_reflDiag⟧; (a) on the model with two copies of $\bar\Psi$ ⟦Chapter17/StrongBundleStructuresExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflPair⟧, whose morphisms include the non-trivial $(0, (-1, 0), 0)$ ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctorsExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflDiag_model⟧; $\bar F(x) \ne \emptyset$ ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctorsExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflQ_hasStrongBundleUniformizers⟧; and the germ form of (c) ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctorsExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflDiag_germs⟧. It is not degenerate: $f(x) \ne 0$ for $x \ne 0$, the isotropy $-1$ of $0$ acts non-trivially on the fibre, and the representative vanishes exactly at $0$ ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctorsExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflDiag_nondegenerate⟧. The weakness is $\partial C = \emptyset$; the half-space bundle of §XVIII.4 has boundary.

### XVIII.3 (18.1) and Definition 18.1.3 (2017: Definition 18.2), p. 845: the linearization

**Statement** (explicit). Let $f$ be an sc-smooth section functor, $\alpha$ an object with $f(\alpha) = 0$, with $\alpha \in C^1$ and $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ (the condition under which $T_\alpha C$ carries its linear structure, Part XVII ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · PolyfoldConstruction.TangentObj⟧), and $\bar F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$. *(18.1) for one $\bar\Psi$* ⟦Chapter18/Linearization · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.sectionLin⟧: for $\bar\Psi \in \bar F(\alpha)$ and the level-$1$ point $x$ with $\Psi(x) = \alpha$, $e \mapsto \bar\Psi(x)\bigl(f_{\bar\Psi}'(x)(S_\Psi e)\bigr)$, where $S_\Psi : T_\alpha C \cong T_x O$ is the chart of Proposition 17.2.4 ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · PolyfoldConstruction.tangentChartCLE⟧ and $\bar\Psi(x) : K_x \to \mu(\alpha)$ the fibre isomorphism followed by the identification $\mu(\Psi(x)) = \mu(\alpha)$ ⟦Chapter18/Linearization · fiberCast⟧. In $E$ it reads $(\alpha, f'(\alpha) e) = \bar\Psi(f_{\bar\Psi}'(x)(S_\Psi e))$ ⟦Chapter18/Linearization · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.objMk_sectionLin⟧. It does not depend on $(\bar\Psi, x)$ ⟦Chapter18/Linearization · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.sectionLin_indep⟧ (G.236, settled). *Definition 18.1.3* ⟦Chapter18/Linearization · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.linearization⟧: $f'(\alpha) : T_\alpha C \to \mu(\alpha)$ is (18.1) through a chosen $\bar\Psi_\alpha \in \bar F(\alpha)$ ⟦Chapter18/Linearization · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.refBundleUnif⟧ and its point over $\alpha$ ⟦Chapter18/Linearization · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.refBundlePoint⟧ ⟦Chapter18/Linearization · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.obj_refBundlePoint⟧; it is (18.1) through every $\bar\Psi \in \bar F(\alpha)$ ⟦Chapter18/Linearization · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.definition_18_1_3⟧, which is (18.1) as printed, on the tangent vector $[\alpha, \Psi, h] = S_\Psi^{-1} h$ ⟦Chapter18/Linearization · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.equation_18_1⟧. Moreover $f'(\alpha)$ is onto iff $f_{\bar\Psi}'(x)$ is ⟦Chapter18/ImplicitFunctionTheorem · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.surjective_linearization_iff⟧.

*Readings.* $f_{\bar\Psi}'(x)$ is the linearization of Chapter 3 (Part III) at a zero on level $1$, the intrinsic linearization of a section at a zero read through a strong bundle chart ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationIntrinsic · StrongBundle.linIntr⟧, which does not depend on the chart ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationIntrinsic · StrongBundle.linIntr_chart_independent⟧; the formalization defines it through a chosen chart ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationTransport · StrongBundle.linZeroAt⟧, and it is the one read through every chart ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationTransport · StrongBundle.linZeroAt_eq⟧ and depends only on the section ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationTransport · StrongBundle.linZeroAt_congr⟧. The text states the definitions at smooth objects; they are stated on level $1$, where $f_{\bar\Psi}'(x)$ is defined. Only sc-smoothness of the representatives is used. A zero of $f$ is a zero of every representative over it ⟦Chapter18/Linearization · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isZeroAt_localRep⟧, since $\bar\Psi$ is linear on fibres.

**The transport lemma** ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationTransport · StrongBundle.LocalIso.linIntr_transport⟧. Let $(\phi, \Phi)$ be a local strong bundle isomorphism $Y|U \to Y'|U'$ and $f = \Phi^{-1} \circ f' \circ \phi$ on $U$, with zeros $x$ of $f$ and $\phi(x)$ of $f'$ on level $1$. Then $\Phi(f'(x) h) = f''(\phi(x))(T\phi(x) h)$. *Step 1.* Restrict a chart $c'$ at $\phi(x)$ to $\mathrm{dom}\, c' \cap U'$ and transport it along $(\phi, \Phi)$ to a chart $d$ at $x$; by chart independence both linearizations may be read in $d$ and in the restricted $c'$. *Step 2.* The principal parts of $f$ in $d$ and of $f'$ in $c'$ agree on the retract domain ⟦Chapter5/FredholmPullbackIso · StrongBundle.LocalIso.principal_transportChart⟧, so their sc-derivatives at the base point agree. *Step 3.* The chart vectors of $h$ in $d$ and of $T\phi(x) h$ in $c'$ coincide, because $d$ is $c' \circ \phi$. *Step 4.* The fibre maps of the two charts are injective, so equality of the chart expressions is equality in $Y'$. The chart-free form is ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationTransport · StrongBundle.LocalIso.linZeroAt_transport⟧; zeros correspond ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationTransport · StrongBundle.LocalIso.isZeroAt_of_eqOn⟧.

**The transition law** ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationTransport · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.linZeroAt_transTangent⟧. For $a = (q, \varphi, q') \in \mathbf M(\Psi, \Psi')$ with $q, q'$ on level $1$ and zeros of $f_{\bar\Psi}$, $f_{\bar\Psi'}$: $f_{\bar\Psi'}'(q')(T\sigma(q) h) = T(a, f_{\bar\Psi}'(q) h)$, where $T\sigma(q)$ is the tangent of the transition ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · PolyfoldConstruction.transTangent⟧ and $T(a, \cdot)$ the fibre map of $\bar t$. *Step 1.* Pull $f_{\bar\Psi}$ back to the transition bundle $\mathbf M(\bar\Psi, \bar\Psi') \to \mathbf M(\Psi, \Psi')$: $b \mapsto (b, f_{\bar\Psi}(s b))$ ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationTransport · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.transSection⟧ ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationTransport · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isSection_transSection⟧. On a sheet of $\bar s$ it is $\bar s^{-1} \circ f_{\bar\Psi} \circ s$ ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationTransport · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.eqOn_transSection_src⟧, which makes it sc-smooth ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationTransport · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isScSmoothSection_transSection⟧; on a sheet of $\bar t$ it is $\bar t^{-1} \circ f_{\bar\Psi'} \circ t$ ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationTransport · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.eqOn_transSection_tgt⟧, by the transition law of the representatives (§XVIII.1). *Step 2.* $\bar s$ and $\bar t$ are local strong bundle isomorphisms near $a$ (Definition 17.7.4) ⟦Chapter17/StrongBundleStructures · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.isLocalStrongBundleIso_src⟧. The transport lemma along $\bar s^{-1}$ carries $f_{\bar\Psi}'(q)$ to the linearization of the pulled-back section at $a$, and along $\bar t$ to $f_{\bar\Psi'}'(q')$. *Step 3.* On the intersection of the two sheets, $t \circ (s|)^{-1}$ is a local representative of the transition $\sigma$ near $q$ (an admissible triple), so $T t \circ T(s|)^{-1} = T\sigma(q)$. *Step 4.* $\bar t \circ \bar s^{-1}$ on $K_q$ is $T(a, \cdot)$, read off from the definition of $\bar t$.

**Proof of the independence of $\bar\Psi$.** Take $\bar\Psi_1, \bar\Psi_2 \in \bar F(\alpha)$ with points $x_1, x_2$ over $\alpha$, and the unit transition $a = (x_1, 1_\alpha, x_2)$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · PolyfoldConstruction.unitTr⟧. *Step 1.* The charts of Proposition 17.2.4 are related by $S_{\Psi_2} = T\sigma \circ S_{\Psi_1}$ along $a$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · PolyfoldConstruction.tangentChart_symm_trans⟧. *Step 2.* By the transition law, $f_{\bar\Psi_2}'(x_2)(S_{\Psi_2} e) = T(a, f_{\bar\Psi_1}'(x_1)(S_{\Psi_1} e))$. *Step 3.* Apply $\bar\Psi_2$: $\bar\Psi_2(T(a, k)) = \mu(1_\alpha)(\bar\Psi_1(k)) = \bar\Psi_1(k)$ ⟦Chapter17/StrongBundleStructures · StrongBundleUniformizerData.obj_transAct⟧, so both sides of (18.1) give the same point of $E$, hence the same vector of $\mu(\alpha)$. Definition 18.1.3 and (18.1) as printed follow, the latter from the former with $e = S_\Psi^{-1} h$. *Surjectivity.* $f'(\alpha)$ is $\bar\Psi(x) \circ f_{\bar\Psi}'(x) \circ S_\Psi$ with two isomorphisms around the middle factor, so it is onto iff $f_{\bar\Psi}'(x)$ is. Theorem 18.1.5 uses this to pass from the text's hypothesis to the model.

### XVIII.4 Definition 18.1.4 (2017: Definition 18.3), p. 845: the linearizations Lin(f, α)

**Statement** (restated). *The local form* ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationSetLocal · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.linSetLoc⟧. A *local sc⁺-section near $q_0$* is a section $s$ of $K \to O$ that is an sc⁺-section (Definition 2.6.6) on an open $W \ni q_0$ ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationSetLocal · StrongBundle.IsScPlusSectionOn⟧. For $\bar\Psi \in \bar F(\alpha)$, its point $x$ over $\alpha$ and a local sc⁺-section $s$ on $W$ with $(f_{\bar\Psi} + s)(x) = 0$, put $f'_s(\alpha) e = \bar\Psi(x)\bigl((f_{\bar\Psi} + s)'(x)(S_\Psi e)\bigr)$ ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationSetLocal · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.linOpLoc⟧, the linearization being that of the section $f_{\bar\Psi} + s$, sc-smooth on $W$ ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationSetLocal · StrongBundle.linZeroLoc⟧. $\mathrm{Lin}(f, \alpha)$ is the set of these operators over all $(\bar\Psi, x, s)$; through one $(\bar\Psi, x)$ it is ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationSetLocal · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.linSetLocAt⟧. *Definition 18.1.4, its claims* ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationSetLocal · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.definition_18_1_4_local⟧, for an sc-Fredholm section functor $f$ and with no further hypothesis: $\mathrm{Lin}(f, \alpha)$ contains the set built from global sc⁺-sections ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationSetLocal · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.linSet_subset_linSetLoc⟧; it is the set read through any one $\bar\Psi \in \bar F(\alpha)$ and point $x$ ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationSetLocal · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.linSetLoc_eq_linSetLocAt⟧; all its elements have finite-dimensional kernel and cokernel and one index $\mathrm{ind}(f, \alpha)$ ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationSetLocal · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.exists_index_linSetLoc⟧; and for $\varphi : \alpha \to \alpha'$ with $\bar F(\alpha') \ne \emptyset$, $\varphi_*$ maps $\mathrm{Lin}(f, \alpha)$ into $\mathrm{Lin}(f, \alpha')$ ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationSetLocal · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.linPush_mem_linSetLoc⟧. At a zero, $f'(\alpha) \in \mathrm{Lin}(f, \alpha)$, with $s = 0$ ⟦Chapter18/Linearization · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.linearization_mem_linSet⟧ (G.237, settled).

*The global form* ⟦Chapter18/Linearization · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.linSet⟧ takes $s$ an sc⁺-section on all of $O$ ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationSet · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.linOp⟧ ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationSet · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.linSetAt⟧ ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationSet · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.mem_linSet_iff⟧. Through one $\bar\Psi$, every element has the index of $(f_{\bar\Psi}, x)$ with no hypothesis ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationSet · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.linSetAt_index⟧. The comparison of two uniformizers takes the hypothesis $\mathrm{HasTransportedScPlusSections}$ ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationSet · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.HasTransportedScPlusSections⟧: for $a \in \mathbf M(\Psi, \Psi')$ and an sc⁺-section $s$ of $K \to O$ there is an sc⁺-section $s'$ of $K' \to O'$ with $s'(t b) = T(b, s(s b))$ for $b$ near $a$. Under it the claims hold for the global form ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationSet · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.definition_18_1_4_hyp⟧: independence ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationSet · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.linSetAt_subset_hyp⟧ ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationSet · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.linSet_eq_linSetAt_hyp⟧, one index ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationSet · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.exists_index_linSet_hyp⟧, and $\varphi_*$ ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationSet · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.linPush_mem_linSet_hyp⟧.

*Readings.* We read the text's $s$ as a local sc⁺-section near $q_0$, as the text says, and "$f_{\bar\Psi} - s$ with $s(q_0) = f_{\bar\Psi}(q_0)$" as $f_{\bar\Psi} + s$ with $(f_{\bar\Psi} + s)(q_0) = 0$, the sign convention of Part III's chapters; the two sets are the same, $s \mapsto -s$. "Sc-Fredholm of the same index" is read on level $0$, where the intrinsic operators $T_\alpha C \to \mu(\alpha)$ live: finite-dimensional kernel and cokernel and one common $\dim\ker - \dim\operatorname{coker}$. In a chart these operators are the sc-Fredholm operators $T_z O \to K_z$ of Chapter 3, conjugated by linear isomorphisms; that the elements differ by sc⁺-operators is not stated separately, the common index is proved directly as the local index. With local sections the transport along a transition needs nothing; the hypothesis of the global form is needed only because a section defined near $q_0'$ must be extended to all of $O'$. It is meant for $O'$ with sc-smooth bump functions, which would cut off the transported section; that construction is not formalized. Witness: the reflection bundle, whose transitions are global, $s' = g \ast s \circ g^{-1}$ ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationSetExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflQ_hasTransportedScPlusSections⟧. The local form, which is the text's, takes no hypothesis, so the global form is a supplement.

**The index of one element** ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationSet · StrongBundle.linZeroAt_hasFiniteIndex⟧ ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationSet · StrongBundle.linZeroAt_sectionAdd_index⟧. *Step 1.* For an sc-Fredholm $f$ and an sc⁺-section $s$, $f + s$ is sc-Fredholm (Theorem 3.1.18) ⟦Chapter3/FredholmSectionsG · theorem_3_1_18_preprint⟧, and a zero $x$ of $f + s$ is smooth; its sc-Fredholm germ comes with conjugation data to a basic germ, from which the chart linearization $\mathrm{Lin}(z) : T_z O \to K_z$ is Fredholm of index $\dim P_n - \dim Q$ ⟦Chapter5/IndexLocallyConstantG · StrongBundle.isLinOfIndex_of_conjugationData⟧. This index is the index of $(f, x)$ of Definition 6.2.1, which is unique. *Step 2.* In the chart, $(f + s)'(x) = \Phi_x^{-1} \circ \mathrm{Lin}(z) \circ T\phi$, with $T\phi : T_x X \cong T_z O$ and $\Phi_x : Y_x \cong K_z$, the image of the fibre map being $\rho(z) F$ ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationSet · StrongBundleChart.range_fiberLin_eq_rangeS⟧. *Step 3.* Conjugation by linear isomorphisms keeps finite index and the index ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationSet · hasFiniteIndex_conj⟧. *Step 4.* $f'_s(\alpha)$ is $(f_{\bar\Psi} + s)'(x)$ conjugated once more, by $S_\Psi$ and $\bar\Psi(x)$, which gives the index of one element of the global set read through $\bar\Psi$. For local sections ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationSetLocal · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.linSetLocAt_index⟧: the restriction of a local sc⁺-section on $W$ to $P|W$ is an sc⁺-section ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationSetLocal · StrongBundle.IsScPlusSectionOn.restrictOpen⟧, $f + s$ is sc-smooth on $W$ ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationSetLocal · StrongBundle.IsScSmoothSection.isScSmoothOn_sectionAdd'⟧, the linearization on $W$ is the one of $(f + s)|W$ read through $T_x W \cong T_x X$ ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationSetLocal · StrongBundle.linZeroAtOn⟧ (for an sc-smooth section it is the linearization above ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationSetLocal · StrongBundle.linZeroLoc_eq_linZeroAt⟧), and Steps 1–3 on $P|W$ give the local index of Definition 6.2.1 with local sections ⟦Chapter5/IndexAlongPathsLocal · StrongBundle.IsIndexAtLocal⟧ ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationSetLocal · StrongBundle.linZeroLoc_sectionAdd_index⟧. Every element comes with a local index of $(f_{\bar\Psi}, x)$ ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationSetLocal · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.exists_isIndexAtLocal_of_mem_linSetLocAt⟧, which, being unique, is the same for all of them.

**Transport of a local sc⁺-section along a transition** ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationSetLocal · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.exists_linZeroLoc_transAct⟧. Let $a \in \mathbf M(\Psi, \Psi')$, $x$ over $s(a)$, $y$ over $t(a)$ on level $1$, and $s$ a local sc⁺-section on $W \ni x$ with $(f_{\bar\Psi} + s)(x) = 0$. *Step 1.* A local sc⁺-section moves along a local strong bundle isomorphism $(\phi, \Phi)$: $\Phi \circ s \circ \phi^{-1}$ is a local sc⁺-section on the image ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationSetLocal · StrongBundle.LocalIso.isScPlusSectionOn_transport⟧. Applied to $\bar s^{-1}$ and then to $\bar t$ on sheets near $a$, it gives $\bar t \circ \bar s^{-1} \circ s \circ s \circ t^{-1}$, a local sc⁺-section on an open $W' \ni y$; extended by $0$ off $W'$ ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationSetLocal · extendByZero⟧ ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationSetLocal · StrongBundle.IsScPlusSectionOn.extendByZero⟧ it is a section $s'$ of $K' \to O'$ which is an sc⁺-section on $W'$. *Step 2.* On a neighbourhood $N$ of $a$, $s'(t b) = T(b, s(s b))$, read off from the definitions of $\bar s$ and $\bar t$. Since $T(b, \cdot)$ is linear on fibres ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationSet · StrongBundleUniformizerData.transAct_sectionAdd⟧ and $f_{\bar\Psi'}(t b) = T(b, f_{\bar\Psi}(s b))$, also $(f_{\bar\Psi'} + s')(t b) = T(b, (f_{\bar\Psi} + s)(s b))$ ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationSet · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.sectionAdd_localRep_transAct⟧; at $b = a$ this gives $(f_{\bar\Psi'} + s')(y) = 0$ ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationSet · StrongBundleUniformizerData.transAct_eq_fiberZero⟧. *Step 3.* The transition law of §XVIII.3 for two sections sc-smooth near the points and related by $\bar t$ on $N$ ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationSetLocal · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.linZeroLoc_transAct_of_eqOn⟧, with the transport lemma for linearizations on open sets ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationSetLocal · StrongBundle.LocalIso.linZeroLoc_transport⟧, gives $(f_{\bar\Psi'} + s')'(y)(T\sigma(x) h) = T(a, (f_{\bar\Psi} + s)'(x) h)$. The proof is that of §XVIII.3 with the sheet of $\bar t$ cut down to $N$. No cut-off is needed: $s'$ only has to be an sc⁺-section near $y$. The global analogue, under the hypothesis, is ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationSet · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.exists_linZeroAt_transAct_hyp⟧, through ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationSet · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.linZeroAt_transAct_of_eqOn⟧.

**Proof of the claims.** *Independence of $\bar\Psi$* ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationSetLocal · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.linSetLocAt_subset⟧. For $\bar\Psi_1, \bar\Psi_2 \in \bar F(\alpha)$ with points $x_1, x_2$, transport $s$ along the unit transition $(x_1, 1_\alpha, x_2)$ to $s'$. Then $f'_{s'}(\alpha) = f'_s(\alpha)$, by the computation of §XVIII.3: $S_{\Psi_2} = T\sigma \circ S_{\Psi_1}$, the transported linearization, and $\bar\Psi_2 \circ T(a, \cdot) = \mu(1_\alpha) \circ \bar\Psi_1$. So the set read through $(\bar\Psi_1, x_1)$ lies in the one read through $(\bar\Psi_2, x_2)$, and by symmetry the union over all $(\bar\Psi, x)$ is the set read through any one. *The index* ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationSetLocal · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.exists_index_linSetLoc⟧: if the set is empty there is nothing to prove; otherwise take one element, read through some $(\bar\Psi, x)$, take the local index $i$ of $(f_{\bar\Psi}, x)$ it provides, and use that the whole set is read through $(\bar\Psi, x)$, where every element has index $i$. *$\varphi_*$* ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationSetLocal · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.linPush_mem_linSetLocAt⟧: for $\varphi : \alpha \to \alpha'$, transport $s$ along $(x, \varphi, x')$ ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · PolyfoldConstruction.morTr⟧ to $s'$; writing $e' = T\varphi\, e$, the chart of $T\varphi$ is $S_{\Psi'} \circ T\varphi = T\sigma \circ S_\Psi$ ⟦Chapter18/Linearization · PolyfoldConstruction.tangentChartCLE_tangentMorCLE⟧, the transported linearization gives $T(a, \cdot)$ of $f'_s$, and $\bar\Psi' \circ T(a, \cdot) = \mu(\varphi) \circ \bar\Psi$; so $f'_{s'}(\alpha') = \hat\varphi \circ f'_s(\alpha) \circ T\varphi^{-1} = \varphi_* f'_s(\alpha)$. For the set over all uniformizers, the target is read through a chosen $\bar\Psi' \in \bar F(\alpha')$, which is where $\bar F(\alpha') \ne \emptyset$ enters. *Global sections are local ones* ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationSetLocal · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.linOp_eq_linOpLoc⟧ ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationSetLocal · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.linSetAt_subset_linSetLocAt⟧: an sc⁺-section is a local one on $W = O$ ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationSetLocal · StrongBundle.IsScPlusSection.isScPlusSectionOn⟧, with the same linearization. The global form under the hypothesis has the same proofs, the transport of $s$ being supplied by the hypothesis.

**Witnesses.** On the reflection bundle at the zero $0$ of $f(x) = (x, x)$, whose isotropy acts on the fibre by $v \mapsto -v$: the local form ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationSetLocalExamples · ReflectionBundle.definition_18_1_4_local_reflDiag⟧ and the global form ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationSetExamples · ReflectionBundle.definition_18_1_4_reflDiag⟧, with $\varphi = (-1, 0)$, whose fibre map is $v \mapsto -v$ ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationSetLocalExamples · ReflectionBundle.linPush_reflDiag_neg_local⟧ ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationSetExamples · ReflectionBundle.linPush_reflDiag_neg⟧. On the half-space bundle $\mathbb Z/2 \ltimes ([0, \infty) \times \mathbb R)$ with $f(a, y) = a^2 + y^2 - 1$ ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceWitnesses · HalfSpaceReflection.halfF⟧, at the boundary zero $(0, 1)$: the local form ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationSetLocalExamples · HalfSpaceReflection.definition_18_1_4_local_halfF⟧. Both constructions have global transitions; the local claims are proved for every strong polyfold bundle construction, and the witnesses only show that the statements are not empty.

### XVIII.5 (18.2) and the push-forward φ_* (2017: §18.1), p. 845

**Statement** (same). For $\varphi : \alpha \to \alpha'$, $\hat\varphi = \mu(\varphi) : \mu(\alpha) \to \mu(\alpha')$ ⟦Chapter18/Linearization · morFiber⟧, with $\mu(\varphi)(\alpha, v) = (\alpha', \hat\varphi v)$ in $E$ ⟦Chapter18/Linearization · act_objMk_morFiber⟧, $\hat 1_\alpha = \mathrm{Id}$ ⟦Chapter18/Linearization · morFiber_unit⟧ and $\widehat{\psi\varphi} = \hat\psi\hat\varphi$ ⟦Chapter18/Linearization · morFiber_comp⟧. If $f(\alpha) = 0$ then $f(\alpha') = 0$ ⟦Chapter18/Linearization · SectionFunctor.toFun_eq_zero_of_mor⟧. *(18.2)* ⟦Chapter18/Linearization · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.equation_18_2⟧: for $f(\alpha) = 0$ and $\bar F(\alpha), \bar F(\alpha') \ne \emptyset$, $\mu(\varphi)(f'(\alpha) e) = f'(\alpha')(T\varphi\, e)$, that is, $\hat\varphi \circ f'(\alpha) = f'(\alpha') \circ T\varphi$ ⟦Chapter18/Linearization · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.equation_18_2_comp⟧, with $T\varphi$ the tangent of Part XVII ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · PolyfoldConstruction.tangentMorCLE⟧. *The push-forward* ⟦Chapter18/Linearization · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.linPush⟧: $\varphi_*(L) = \hat\varphi \circ L \circ T\varphi^{-1}$ on all linear maps $T_\alpha C \to \mu(\alpha)$, with $(1_\alpha)_* = \mathrm{Id}$ ⟦Chapter18/Linearization · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.linPush_unit⟧, $(\psi\varphi)_* = \psi_*\varphi_*$ ⟦Chapter18/Linearization · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.linPush_comp⟧ and $\varphi_* f'(\alpha) = f'(\alpha')$ ⟦Chapter18/Linearization · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.linPush_linearization⟧. The text says these hold "clearly" (G.238, settled). That $\varphi_*$ maps $\mathrm{Lin}(f, \alpha)$ into $\mathrm{Lin}(f, \alpha')$ is §XVIII.4.

**Proofs.** *$\hat\varphi$.* $\hat\varphi$ is $\mu(\varphi)$ between the fibres over $s\varphi = \alpha$ and $t\varphi = \alpha'$; the unit and composition laws are those of the functor $\mu$, transported through the identifications of fibres over equal objects. *Zeros along morphisms.* $f(\alpha') = \mu(\varphi)(f(\alpha)) = \mu(\varphi)(\alpha, 0) = (\alpha', 0)$, by functoriality of $f$ and linearity of $\mu(\varphi)$ ⟦Chapter18/ModelTransfer · act_objMk_zero⟧.

*(18.2).* *Step 1.* Read $f'(\alpha)$ through $\bar\Psi_\alpha$ at $x$ and $f'(\alpha')$ through $\bar\Psi_{\alpha'}$ at $x'$ (Definition 18.1.3, §XVIII.3). *Step 2.* The chart of $T\varphi$: $S_{\Psi'}(T\varphi\, e) = T\sigma(S_\Psi e)$ for the transition $\sigma$ of $a = (x, \varphi, x') \in \mathbf M(\Psi, \Psi')$ ⟦Chapter18/Linearization · PolyfoldConstruction.tangentChartCLE_tangentMorCLE⟧. Its proof writes both charts through the reference chart at $\alpha$ and at $\alpha'$ by unit transitions, and composes the transition maps: the composite of the unit transition at $\alpha'$ with the transition of $\varphi$ from the reference point is the transition of $\varphi$ from $x$, by the definition (17.31) of $T\varphi$ and the composition law of transition tangents (Proposition 17.2.5). *Step 3.* By the transition law of §XVIII.3, $f_{\bar\Psi'}'(x')(T\sigma(S_\Psi e)) = T(a, f_{\bar\Psi}'(x)(S_\Psi e))$. *Step 4.* $\bar\Psi'(T(a, k)) = \mu(\varphi)(\bar\Psi(k))$, so the image in $E$ of $f'(\alpha')(T\varphi\, e)$ is $\mu(\varphi)$ of the image of $f'(\alpha) e$. The operator form follows with $\mu(\varphi)(\alpha, v) = (\alpha', \hat\varphi v)$, and $\varphi_* f'(\alpha) = f'(\alpha')$ is the operator form evaluated at $T\varphi^{-1} e'$.

*Functoriality of $\varphi_*$.* $(1_\alpha)_* L = \hat 1_\alpha \circ L \circ T1_\alpha^{-1} = L$, since $\hat 1_\alpha = \mathrm{Id}$ and $T 1_\alpha = \mathrm{Id}$ by Proposition 17.2.5 ⟦Chapter17/TangentVectors · PolyfoldConstruction.proposition_17_2_5⟧. $(\psi\varphi)_* L = \hat\psi\hat\varphi \circ L \circ T\varphi^{-1} T\psi^{-1} = \psi_*(\varphi_* L)$, by the composition law of $\hat{\ }$ and $T(\psi\varphi) = T\psi \circ T\varphi$ (Proposition 17.2.5).

**Witness.** On the reflection bundle, $\varphi = (-1, 0) : 0 \to 0$ has $\hat\varphi = -\mathrm{Id}$, and $\varphi_* f'(0) \in \mathrm{Lin}(f, 0)$ (§XVIII.4); the isotropy acts non-trivially, so $\varphi_*$ is not the identity on the fibre.

### XVIII.6 The full subcategory C_U (2017: §18.1), p. 845

**Statement** (same). Before Theorem 18.1.5 the text introduces, for an open subset $U$ of $|C|$, the full subcategory $C_U$ of the objects $\alpha$ with $|\alpha| \in U$, and Theorem 18.1.5 (1) then speaks of a tame orbifold-type ep⁺-subcategory of $C_U$. So $C_U$ has to be a polyfold in its own right. The formalization builds this structure.

*Definitions.* The objects of $C_U$ are $\pi^{-1}(U)$ ⟦Chapter18/OpenSubcategory · GCT.objOver⟧, and $C_U$ is the full subcategory on them, as a GCT, with $|C_U|$ carrying the topology of $|C|$ ⟦Chapter18/OpenSubcategory · GCT.openSub⟧ (the full subcategory construction of Part XVII ⟦Chapter17/SubpolyfoldStructure · PolyfoldConstruction.subConstruction⟧). A function $\Theta$ on the objects of $C$ restricts to $\Theta|C_U = \Theta \circ (C_U \to C)$ ⟦Chapter18/OpenSubcategory · GCT.restrictTheta⟧; it stays a functor ⟦Chapter18/OpenSubcategory · GCT.isFunctorial_restrictTheta⟧ and keeps the values $0$, $1$ if $\Theta$ does ⟦Chapter18/OpenSubcategory · GCT.isOrbifoldTypeBranched_restrictTheta⟧. For a uniformizer $\Psi : G \ltimes O \to C$, the restriction $\Psi_U : G \ltimes \Psi^{-1}(\pi^{-1}U) \to C_U$ ⟦Chapter18/OpenSubcategory · LocalUniformizer.restrictOver⟧; the polyfold construction $(F_U, M)$ of $C_U$ ⟦Chapter18/OpenSubcategory · PolyfoldConstruction.openSubConstruction⟧ has $F_U(\alpha) = \{\Psi_U \mid \Psi \in F(\alpha)\}$ ⟦Chapter18/OpenSubcategory · PolyfoldConstruction.mem_openSubConstruction_F_iff⟧.

*Results.* (a) For open $U$, $|C_U| \to |C|$ is an open embedding with image $U$, so $|C_U| \cong U$ ⟦Chapter18/OpenSubcategory · GCT.openSubHomeomorph⟧. (b) $(F_U, M)$ is a polyfold construction with uniformizers at every object when $C$ has them ⟦Chapter18/OpenSubcategory · PolyfoldConstruction.hasUniformizers_openSubConstruction⟧, and it is tame when $C$ is ⟦Chapter18/OpenSubcategory · PolyfoldConstruction.isTame_openSubConstruction⟧. (c) A criterion for $\Theta|C_U$ to be a tame branched ep⁺-subcategory of $C_U$ (Definitions 17.5.1 and 17.5.4 (3) for $(F_U, M)$) ⟦Chapter18/OpenSubcategory · PolyfoldConstruction.openSub_isBranchedSubcategory⟧: $\Theta \ge 0$ is a functor, and for every $\Psi \in F(\alpha)$ with $|\alpha| \in U$, $\Theta \circ \Psi$ has at every point of $\Psi^{-1}(\pi^{-1}U)$ in its support a local branching structure with tame sheets.

*Reading.* $C_U$ is read as the full subcategory with the topology of $|C|$, and its polyfold structure as that of Proposition 17.3.6 (Part XVII) for the subpolyfold $\pi^{-1}(U)$: the uniformizers are the restrictions $\Psi_U$ to the open $G$-invariant sets $\Psi^{-1}(\pi^{-1}U)$, and the transition sets $M(\Psi_U, \Psi'_U)$ are the open subsets $s^{-1}(\Psi^{-1}(\pi^{-1}U))$ of $M(\Psi, \Psi')$.

**Proof of (a).** *Step 1.* $\pi^{-1}(U)$ is saturated: for a morphism $\varphi$ with source over $U$, $\pi(t(\varphi)) = \pi(s(\varphi)) \in U$ ⟦Chapter18/OpenSubcategory · GCT.isSaturated_objOver⟧. *Step 2.* The map $|C_U| \to |C|$ sends $|\alpha|$ to $|\alpha|$, so its image is exactly $U$ ⟦Chapter18/OpenSubcategory · GCT.range_subOrbitMap_objOver⟧: a class in the image is the class of an object over $U$, and conversely every $z \in U$ is the class of an object $\alpha$ with $|\alpha| = z \in U$. *Step 3.* Part XVII shows that the orbit map of a full subcategory is an embedding; with an open image it is an open embedding ⟦Chapter18/OpenSubcategory · GCT.isOpenEmbedding_subOrbitMap_objOver⟧, and the embedding followed by the identification of its range with $U$ is the homeomorphism.

**Proof of (b).** *Step 1.* $\Psi^{-1}(\pi^{-1}U)$ is open in $O$, as the preimage of the open $U$ under the continuous $\pi \circ \Psi$ ⟦Chapter18/OpenSubcategory · LocalUniformizer.isOpen_preimage_objOver⟧, hence a sub-M-polyfold of $O$ with the open-subset structure ⟦Chapter18/OpenSubcategory · LocalUniformizer.isSubMPolyfold_preimage_objOver⟧. *Step 2.* So $\pi^{-1}(U)$ is a subpolyfold in the sense of Definition 17.3.1: it is saturated, and its preimage under every uniformizer is a sub-M-polyfold ⟦Chapter18/OpenSubcategory · PolyfoldConstruction.isSubpolyfold_objOver⟧; Proposition 17.3.6 then gives the polyfold construction $(F_U, M)$. *Step 3, uniformizers.* For $\alpha \in C_U$ choose $\Psi \in F(\alpha)$; then $\Psi_U \in F_U(\alpha)$. *Step 4, tameness.* An open subset of a tame M-polyfold, with the sub-M-polyfold structure, is tame (the induced tame structure is inherited by open intersections), so $\Psi_U$ is tame when $\Psi$ is ⟦Chapter18/OpenSubcategory · LocalUniformizer.isTame_restrictOver⟧; since every member of $F_U(\alpha)$ is such a $\Psi_U$, $C_U$ is tame.

**Proof of (c).** *Step 1, the identity as an sc-diffeomorphism.* The open set $\Psi^{-1}(\pi^{-1}U)$ of $O$ and the M-polyfold of $\Psi_U$ are sc-diffeomorphic by the identity; formally the map $O \to \Psi^{-1}(\pi^{-1}U)$ that is the identity on the open set and constant elsewhere ⟦Chapter18/OpenSubcategory · LocalUniformizer.restrictOverRetract⟧, with the inclusion as inverse ⟦Chapter18/OpenSubcategory · LocalUniformizer.isScDiffeoOn_restrictOverRetract⟧. *Step 2, local branching structures restrict.* A local branching structure of $\Theta \circ \Psi$ at a point $q$ of the open set is carried along this sc-diffeomorphism to one of $\Theta|C_U \circ \Psi_U$ at $q$, with sheets $M_i \cap \Psi^{-1}(\pi^{-1}U)$ and the same weights; sheets with a tame induced structure keep it ⟦Chapter18/OpenSubcategory · LocalUniformizer.localBranching_restrict⟧. *Step 3, the restricted uniformizer.* If $\Theta \ge 0$ is a functor and every point of the support in $\Psi^{-1}(\pi^{-1}U)$ carries such a structure, then $\Theta|C_U \circ \Psi_U$ is a branched ep⁺-subgroupoid of $G \ltimes \Psi^{-1}(\pi^{-1}U)$ (non-negativity, functoriality, the support in the smooth points because a sheet through the point lies there, paracompactness of the orbit space of the support, the local branching structures) and it is tame ⟦Chapter18/OpenSubcategory · LocalUniformizer.isBranchedEpSubgroupoid_restrict⟧. *Step 4.* Every member of $F_U(\alpha)$ is a $\Psi_U$ (Step 3 of (b)), so Definitions 17.5.1 and 17.5.4 (3) hold for $(F_U, M)$.

*Witness.* On the reflection polyfold $\mathbb Z/2 \ltimes \mathbb R$, $C_U$ is a tame polyfold with uniformizers for every open $U$ ⟦Chapter18/OpenSubcategoryExamples · ReflectionBundle.openSubConstruction_reflection⟧.

### XVIII.7 Good position to the partial cone (Remark 17.2.18), p. 846

**Statement** (restated). Theorem 18.1.5 asks that $\ker f'(\alpha)$ be in good position to the boundary in the sense of Remark 17.2.18: for a smooth object $\alpha$ the tangent space $T_\alpha C$ carries a partial cone $C_\alpha C$, defined in an sc-smooth model following Definition 2.5.8 and pushed forward, and one can speak of a finite-dimensional subspace in good position to it. The formalization defines the partial cone and the reduced tangent space on $T_\alpha C$, proves that they do not depend on the uniformizer, defines good position to $C_\alpha C$ without norms, and proves that good position of $\ker f'(\alpha)$, together with surjectivity of $f'(\alpha)$, is Part I's good position of the local representative.

*Definitions.* $T_\alpha C$ is the space of Definition 17.2.3 with the charts $S_\Psi : T_\alpha C \cong T_qO$ of Proposition 17.2.4 (Part XVII), for $\Psi \in F(\alpha)$ and $\Psi(q) = \alpha$. The partial cone $C_\alpha C$ is the preimage under the reference chart $S_{\Psi_\alpha}$ of the partial cone $C_{q_\alpha}O_\alpha$ of Definition 2.5.8, read in a chosen chart of $O_\alpha$ and a chosen presentation of its partial quadrant ⟦Chapter18/TangentPartialCone · PolyfoldConstruction.partialCone⟧ ⟦Chapter18/TangentPartialCone · MPolyfold.tangentPartialCone⟧; the reduced tangent space $T^R_\alpha C$ is the preimage of $T^R_{q_\alpha}O_\alpha$ ⟦Chapter18/TangentPartialCone · PolyfoldConstruction.reducedTangent⟧ ⟦Chapter18/TangentPartialCone · MPolyfold.tangentReducedSpace⟧. On a topological vector space $V$, a subspace $N$ is in good position to a set $K$ ⟦Chapter18/GoodPositionIntrinsic · IsInGoodPositionTop⟧ if $N \cap K$ has non-empty interior in $N$ and $N$ has an algebraic complement $P$ with a neighbourhood $W$ of $0$ and a bounded set $B$ such that $n \in K \iff n + p \in K$ whenever $n \in N$, $p \in P$, $p \in sW$ and $n \notin sB$ for some $s > 0$. A subspace $N \subseteq T_\alpha C$ is in good position to $C_\alpha C$ if it is so in this sense ⟦Chapter18/GoodPositionIntrinsic · PolyfoldConstruction.IsInGoodPositionToPartialCone⟧, and the hypothesis of Theorem 18.1.5 is that $\ker f'(\alpha)$ is ⟦Chapter18/GoodPositionIntrinsic · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsKernelInGoodPosition⟧.

*Reading.* $T_\alpha C$ has a topology and a linear structure but no preferred norm, so the text's condition $\|p\| \le \varepsilon \|n\|$ of Definition 3.1.24 is read in the scale-invariant form above. On a normed space the two agree ⟦Chapter18/GoodPositionIntrinsic · isInGoodPositionTop_iff_norm⟧: given $W$ and $B$, take $\delta$ with the $\delta$-ball inside $W$ and $R \ge 1$ with $B$ in the $R$-ball; then $\varepsilon = \delta/(4R)$ works, with $s = \|n\|/(2R)$; conversely, given $\varepsilon$, take $W$ the closed $\varepsilon$-ball and $B$ the open unit ball ⟦Chapter18/GoodPositionIntrinsic · goodComplement_top_iff_norm⟧. "Defined in a model and pushed forward" is read through the charts $S_\Psi$.

**Proof that the cone does not depend on the uniformizer** (Remark 17.2.18) ⟦Chapter18/TangentPartialCone · PolyfoldConstruction.partialCone_eq⟧: for every $\Psi \in F(\alpha)$, the point $q$ over $\alpha$, every chart $\varphi$ of $O$ around $q$ and every presentation $T$ of its partial quadrant, $C_\alpha C = S_\Psi^{-1}(C_qO)$.

*Step 1, the tangent of an sc-diffeomorphism is the identity in a transported chart.* For an sc-diffeomorphism $f$ and a chart $\psi$ around $f(x)$, the chart $\psi \circ f$ around $x$ sees $Tf(x)$ as the identity of the model space: $T\psi(f(x)) \circ Tf(x) = T(\psi \circ f)(x)$ ⟦Chapter18/TangentPartialCone · MPolyfold.fiberCoord_tangentMapOnCLM_transportChart⟧. The proof computes the tangent map in the two charts; $\psi \circ f \circ (\psi \circ f)^{-1}$ is the identity on the image, and the tangent of the identity of a retract is the identity.

*Step 2, sc-diffeomorphisms preserve partial cones.* $Tf(x)v \in C_{f(x)}Y$ iff $v \in C_xX$, in any charts and presentations ⟦Chapter18/TangentPartialCone · MPolyfold.mem_partialConeFiber_tangentMapOnCLM_iff⟧. Part II's independence of the chart and presentation ⟦Chapter2/ReducedTangentIntrinsic · MPolyfold.partialConeFiber_eq⟧ replaces $(\varphi, T)$ by the transported chart $\psi \circ f$ with the presentation $T'$, and Step 1 makes the two coordinate vectors equal. Reduced tangent spaces follow, since $T^R = C \cap (-C)$ and $Tf(x)$ is linear ⟦Chapter18/TangentPartialCone · MPolyfold.mem_reducedTangentFiber_tangentMapOnCLM_iff⟧.

*Step 3, transition morphisms.* For an element $a = (q, \varphi, q')$ of a transition set, $T\sigma(q)$ is the tangent of an sc-diffeomorphism, so it preserves partial cones ⟦Chapter18/TangentPartialCone · PolyfoldConstruction.mem_partialConeFiber_transTangent_iff⟧.

*Step 4.* $S_\Psi = T\sigma(q_\alpha) \circ S_{\Psi_\alpha}$ for $\sigma$ of $(q_\alpha, 1_\alpha, q)$ ⟦Chapter18/TangentPartialCone · PolyfoldConstruction.tangentChartCLE_apply⟧, and Step 3 applied to this transition element gives $C_\alpha C = S_\Psi^{-1}(C_qO)$. The same holds for $T^R_\alpha C$ ⟦Chapter18/TangentPartialCone · PolyfoldConstruction.reducedTangent_eq⟧, through $T^R_\alpha C = C_\alpha C \cap (-C_\alpha C)$ ⟦Chapter18/TangentPartialCone · PolyfoldConstruction.mem_reducedTangent_iff_lineality⟧.

**The codimension and the boundary.** For tame $C$ and smooth $\alpha$, $\dim(T_\alpha C / T^R_\alpha C) = d_C(\alpha)$ ⟦Chapter18/TangentPartialCone · PolyfoldConstruction.finrank_quotient_reducedTangent_eq_degIndex⟧: $S_{\Psi_\alpha}$ maps $T^R_\alpha C$ onto $T^R_{q_\alpha}O_\alpha$, the M-polyfold statement gives codimension $d_{O_\alpha}(q_\alpha)$ in a tame M-polyfold ⟦Chapter18/TangentPartialCone · MPolyfold.finrank_quotient_tangentReducedSpace_eq_degIndex⟧, and Definition 17.1.30 identifies $d_{O_\alpha}(q_\alpha)$ with $d_C(\alpha)$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldLevels · PolyfoldConstruction.definition_17_1_30⟧. Hence on $\partial C$ ($d_C(\alpha) \ge 1$) the cone is proper ⟦Chapter18/TangentPartialCone · PolyfoldConstruction.partialCone_ne_univ⟧: if $C_\alpha C = T_\alpha C$, then $T^R_\alpha C = T_\alpha C$ and the quotient is $0$.

**Good position read through a uniformizer.** For every $\Psi \in F(\alpha)$, $N$ is in good position to $C_\alpha C$ iff $S_\Psi(N)$ is in good position to $C_qO$ ⟦Chapter18/GoodPositionIntrinsic · PolyfoldConstruction.isInGoodPositionToPartialCone_iff⟧, since good position is carried by continuous linear isomorphisms ⟦Chapter18/GoodPositionIntrinsic · isInGoodPositionTop_preimage_iff⟧ (the complement, neighbourhood and bounded set are mapped along). In a chart, good position to $C_xX$ is Part I's good position inside the subspace $T_{\varphi(x)}O$ of the model space ⟦Chapter18/GoodPositionIntrinsic · MPolyfold.isInGoodPositionTop_tangentPartialCone_iff⟧.

**Definition 3.6.6 as printed.** For a tame sc-Fredholm section and a zero $x$ on level $1$, Part I's good position in a tame chart holds iff $f'(x)$ is onto and $\ker f'(x)$ is in good position to $C_xX$ ⟦Chapter18/GoodPositionIntrinsic · StrongBundle.isGoodPositionGermTame_iff_tangent⟧. *Step 1.* In a chart, $f'(x)$ is the chart linearization $\mathrm{Lin}(\varphi(x))$ ⟦Chapter18/GoodPositionIntrinsic · StrongBundle.linAt_fiberCoord⟧, so the chart image of $\ker f'(x)$ is $\ker \mathrm{Lin}(\varphi(x))$ ⟦Chapter18/GoodPositionIntrinsic · StrongBundle.map_ker_linZeroAt⟧, and $f'(x)$ is onto iff the chart linearization is onto the fibre ⟦Chapter18/GoodPositionIntrinsic · StrongBundle.surjective_linZeroAt_iff⟧. *Step 2.* The good position of $\ker f'(x)$ to $C_xX$ is therefore Part I's good position of $\ker\mathrm{Lin}(\varphi(x))$ ⟦Chapter18/GoodPositionIntrinsic · StrongBundle.isInGoodPositionTop_ker_linZeroAt_iff⟧. *Step 3, ⇒.* From Part I's good position one reads off surjectivity, and the kernel condition, after identifying the chart derivative with the germ derivative at $0$ ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationChartsG · StrongBundle.linAt_eq_germDeriv_chart⟧. *Step 4, ⇐.* Part I builds a good-position germ from a surjective linearization whose kernel is in good position ⟦Chapter3/LinearizationChartsG · StrongBundle.isGoodPositionGerm_of_linAt_surj⟧, and the tame germ data make it tame.
