**The hypothesis of Theorem 18.1.5 in a uniformizer** ⟦Chapter18/GoodPositionIntrinsic · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isGoodPositionGermTame_localRep_iff⟧: for every $\bar\Psi \in \bar F(\alpha)$ and $q_0$ with $\Psi(q_0) = \alpha$, $f_{\bar\Psi}$ is in good position at $q_0$ (Definition 3.6.6 in a tame chart) iff $f'(\alpha)$ is onto and $\ker f'(\alpha)$ is in good position to $C_\alpha C$. *Proof.* The previous paragraph for $f_{\bar\Psi}$; $f'(\alpha)$ is onto iff $f'_{\bar\Psi}(q_0)$ is ⟦Chapter18/ImplicitFunctionTheorem · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.surjective_linearization_iff⟧, because $f'(\alpha) = \bar\Psi(q_0) \circ f'_{\bar\Psi}(q_0) \circ S_\Psi$ with both outer maps isomorphisms (Definition 18.1.3); $S_\Psi(\ker f'(\alpha)) = \ker f'_{\bar\Psi}(q_0)$ ⟦Chapter18/GoodPositionIntrinsic · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.map_ker_sectionLin⟧; and good position to $C_\alpha C$ is good position of $S_\Psi(N)$ to $C_{q_0}O$.

**On the boundary, the reduced tangent space is not in good position.** For tame $C$, smooth $\alpha$ with $d_C(\alpha) \ge 1$ and $0 \ne N \subseteq T^R_\alpha C$, $N$ is not in good position to $C_\alpha C$ ⟦Chapter18/GoodPositionIntrinsic · PolyfoldConstruction.not_isInGoodPositionToPartialCone_of_le_reducedTangent⟧. *Proof.* In the Banach space $T_qO$, a convex cone $K$ closed under sums and positive multiples ⟦Chapter18/GoodPositionIntrinsic · PolyfoldConstruction.add_mem_partialCone⟧ ⟦Chapter18/GoodPositionIntrinsic · PolyfoldConstruction.smul_mem_partialCone⟧ with lineality space $L = K \cap (-K) \ne V$ admits no subspace $0 \ne N \le L$ in good position ⟦Chapter18/GoodPositionIntrinsic · not_isInGoodPositionTop_of_le_lineality⟧: a good complement $P$ must contain some $p_0 \notin K$ (otherwise $P \le L$ and $V = N \oplus P \le L$); for $0 \ne n \in N$ and $t = \varepsilon\|n\|/\|p_0\|$, good position gives $n + tp_0 \in K$, and adding $-n \in L \subseteq K$ gives $tp_0 \in K$, so $p_0 \in K$. The cone is proper by `partialCone_ne_univ`, so $L \ne V$.

*Witnesses.* At the corner $(0,0)$ of $\mathbb Z/2 \ltimes [0,\infty) \times \mathbb R$, a boundary object with the non-trivial automorphism $(-1, (0,0))$: the cone contains $T^R_\alpha C$, is proper, and $\dim(T_\alpha C/T^R_\alpha C) \ge 1$ ⟦Chapter18/TangentPartialConeExamples · HalfSpaceReflection.partialCone_corner⟧; the line $\mathbb R(1, 0)$, neither $0$ nor $T_\alpha C$, is in good position to $C_\alpha C$, and the non-zero $T^R_\alpha C$ is not ⟦Chapter18/GoodPositionIntrinsicExamples · HalfSpaceReflection.isInGoodPositionToPartialCone_corner⟧. In coordinates, on $\partial C$ the cone is $\{(a, \eta) \mid a \ge 0\}$, and a non-zero subspace is in good position iff it is not contained in $\{a = 0\}$ ⟦Chapter18/GoodPositionIntrinsicExamples · HalfSpaceReflection.isInGoodPositionToPartialCone_halfS_iff⟧.

### XVIII.8 Theorem 18.1.5 and Remark 18.1.6 (2017: Theorem 18.1, Remark 18.2), p. 846: the implicit function theorem

**Statement** (explicit). *Theorem 18.1.5.* Let $f$ be an sc-Fredholm section functor of a strong polyfold bundle over a tame polyfold, and $\alpha$ a smooth object with $f(\alpha) = 0$, $f'(\alpha) : T_\alpha C \to E_\alpha$ onto and $\ker f'(\alpha)$ in good position to $C_\alpha C$. Then there is an open neighbourhood $U$ of $|\alpha|$ such that (1) $\Theta : C_U \to \{0, 1\}$, $\Theta(\beta) = 1$ iff $f(\beta) = 0$, is a tame orbifold-type ep⁺-subcategory, and (2) at every $\beta$ with $\Theta(\beta) = 1$ the linearization is onto with kernel in good position; by the Note, $|\mathrm{supp}\,\Theta|$ is a smooth orbifold with boundary with corners. The formal statements are three. In a model ⟦Chapter18/ImplicitFunctionTheorem · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.theorem_18_1_5_model⟧; with the text's intrinsic hypothesis ⟦Chapter18/GoodPositionIntrinsic · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.theorem_18_1_5⟧; and on the polyfold $C_U$ ⟦Chapter18/ImplicitFunctionSubcategory · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.theorem_18_1_5_subcategory⟧, with the Note ⟦Chapter18/ImplicitFunctionSubcategory · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.theorem_18_1_5_note⟧. $\Theta$ is the zero indicator of $f$ ⟦Chapter18/ImplicitFunctionTheorem · SectionFunctor.zeroIndicator⟧, a functor ⟦Chapter18/ImplicitFunctionTheorem · SectionFunctor.isFunctorial_zeroIndicator⟧ with values in $\{0,1\}$ ⟦Chapter18/ImplicitFunctionTheorem · SectionFunctor.isOrbifoldTypeBranched_zeroIndicator⟧.

*Remark 18.1.6* (same). The functor $\Lambda : E \to \{0, 1\}$, $1$ exactly on the zero vectors ⟦Chapter18/ImplicitFunctionTheorem · VectorBundle.zeroFunctor⟧, is constant along morphisms ⟦Chapter18/ImplicitFunctionTheorem · VectorBundle.isMorInvariant_zeroFunctor⟧, $\Theta = \Lambda \circ f$, and in every model $W_{\bar\Psi} \to X_\Psi$ the pull-back $\Lambda \circ \bar\Gamma$ is Part III's trivial multisection $\Lambda_0$ ⟦Chapter11/MultisectionLocalStructure · SBLocalization.zeroMultisection⟧, an sc⁺-multisection ⟦Chapter18/ImplicitFunctionTheorem · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.remark_18_1_6⟧; $(f^{\bar\Psi}, \Lambda_0)$ is in good position at the point over $\alpha$ when $f_{\bar\Psi}$ is ⟦Chapter18/ImplicitFunctionTheorem · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.remark_18_1_6_goodPosition⟧ (G.240 settled).

*Added hypothesis and readings.* `hft`, tame germ data ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsScFredholmSectionFunctorTame⟧: the representatives $f_{\bar\Psi}$ have their germ data in tame charts. This is the standing reading of Part III for "over a tame polyfold", as in Theorem 15.3.7; it enters through Theorem 15.2.5. `hne`, $\bar F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$, the standing reading, enters through the linearization. The witnesses: the reflection bundle, at an interior zero with isotropy $\mathbb Z/2$ acting by $-1$ on the fibre (weak: $\partial C = \emptyset$), and the half-space bundle at the boundary zero $(0, 1)$ (§XVIII.7 and below). Conclusion (1) is read on the polyfold $C_U$ of §XVIII.6. Conclusion (2) is read in the model, as good position of $(f^{\bar\Psi}, \Lambda_0)$ in the sense of Definition 15.2.4 at the zeros of the natural representation, which is the ep-groupoid statement the text's proof uses. "Tame orbifold-type" is read as: $\Theta$ takes the values $0$ and $1$, and $\Theta \circ \Gamma$ has a tame local branching structure (Definition 9.3.6) at every point of its support.

**Proof of Remark 18.1.6.** *Step 1, $\Lambda \circ \bar\Gamma = \Lambda_0$* ⟦Chapter18/ImplicitFunctionTheorem · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.bundlePull_zeroFunctor⟧. $\bar\Gamma$ maps the zero of the fibre over $x$ to the zero vector over $\Gamma(x)$, and it is injective on objects of the model bundle over a given base point, so $\bar\Gamma(w)$ is a zero vector iff $w$ is the zero of its fibre; both functions are $1$ exactly there. *Step 2.* $\Theta = \Lambda \circ f$ by unfolding ⟦Chapter18/ImplicitFunctionTheorem · SectionFunctor.zeroIndicator_eq⟧. *Step 3, $\Theta \circ \Gamma = \Lambda_0 \circ f^{\bar\Psi}$* ⟦Chapter18/ImplicitFunctionTheorem · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.zeroIndicator_modelFunctor⟧: $f^{\bar\Psi}(x)$ is the zero of its fibre iff $f(\Gamma x) = 0$ ⟦Chapter18/ModelTransfer · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isZeroAt_modelSection_iff⟧. *Step 4.* $\Lambda_0$ is an sc⁺-multisection (Part XI) ⟦Chapter11/MultisectionLocalStructure · SBLocalization.isScPlusMultisection_zeroMultisection⟧.

*The good position clause.* *Step 1, the branch.* If $f_{\bar\Psi_\lambda}$ is in good position at $q_0$, the branch $f^{\bar\Psi} - 0$ is in good position at $x_0 = (\lambda, q_0)$ (Definition 15.2.4 (1), (3)) ⟦Chapter18/ImplicitFunctionTheorem · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isBranchGoodPosition_modelSection⟧: the local strong bundle isomorphism of the summand $K_\lambda$ into $W = \bigsqcup K_\mu$ carries good position (Part I's invariance under local strong bundle isomorphisms), and so does the restriction of $W$ to $X_\Psi$. *Step 2, surjectivity.* With the local section structure $(\{0\}, 1)$ on $X_\Psi$, the local solution set theorem of Part XV ⟦Chapter15/LocalIFT · SBLocalization.exists_solutionSet_of_isGoodPosition⟧ gives a neighbourhood $V$ of $x_0$ on whose zeros the chart linearization is onto; this is the surjectivity of $T(f^{\bar\Psi}, \Lambda_0)(x_0)$ at level $1$, and with $\Lambda_0(f^{\bar\Psi}(x_0)) = 1 > 0$ it is Definition 15.2.4 at $x_0$.

**Proof of Theorem 18.1.5 in a model.** Let $\bar\Psi$ be a family of strong bundle uniformizers, $\bar\Psi_\lambda$ a member, and $q_0$ with $f(\Psi_\lambda(q_0)) = 0$ at which $f_{\bar\Psi_\lambda}$ is in good position.

*Step 1, the model section.* $f^{\bar\Psi}$ is sc-Fredholm with tame germ data in the model $W_{\bar\Psi} \to X_\Psi$ (Remark 18.1.2 (a) in the model form of §XVIII.2: a section of $\bigsqcup K_\mu$ is sc-Fredholm iff each piece is) ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isScFredholmSectionFunctor_model_iff⟧ ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isScFredholmSectionTame_model_iff⟧. *Step 2.* $(f^{\bar\Psi}, \Lambda_0)$ is in good position at $x_0$ (Remark 18.1.6). *Step 3, Theorem 15.2.5.* Part XV's Theorem 15.2.5 for $(f^{\bar\Psi}, \Lambda_0)$ at $x_0$ ⟦Chapter15/BoundaryPerturbation · SBLocalization.theorem_15_2_5_hyp⟧ gives a natural representation $R_1$ of $G_{x_0}$ on whose support points $(f^{\bar\Psi}, \Lambda_0)$ is in good position. *Step 4, shrinking.* The local solution set theorem gives an open $V \ni x_0$ in which $\{f^{\bar\Psi} = 0\}$ is a smooth submanifold (Part I's Theorem 3.6.8 on $W|X$); $R_1$ is shrunk to a natural representation $R$ with $U(x_0) \subseteq V$ ⟦Chapter7/NaturalRep · EpGroupoid.exists_naturalRep_shrink⟧. *Step 5, the open neighbourhood.* $U = |\Gamma|(\pi(U(x_0)))$ is open because $\pi$ is open (Proposition 7.1.8) and $|\Gamma|$ is an open map, and it contains $|\alpha|$. *Step 6, (1).* $\Theta$ has the values $0, 1$, $\Theta \circ \Gamma = \Lambda_0 \circ f^{\bar\Psi}$ (Step 3 of Remark 18.1.6), and at every $y \in U(x_0)$ with $f(\Gamma y) = 0$ the good position of Step 3 and Theorem 15.2.5's tame form ⟦Chapter15/TameBranched · SBLocalization.theorem_15_2_5_tame_hyp⟧ give a tame local branching structure of $\Theta \circ \Gamma$ at $y$. *Step 7, (2).* At those $y$, $(f^{\bar\Psi}, \Lambda_0)$ is in good position (Step 3). *Step 8, the Note in the model.* $\{y \in U(x_0) \mid f(\Gamma y) = 0\} = \{y \in V \mid f^{\bar\Psi}(y) = 0\} \cap U(x_0)$ is an open subset of a smooth submanifold, hence one. *Step 9, invariance.* For $g \in G_{x_0}$ and $y \in U(x_0)$, the morphism $\Gamma(g, y) : y \to \Phi_g(y)$ of $X_\Psi$ and the functoriality of $\Theta \circ \Gamma$ give $f(\Gamma y) = 0 \Rightarrow f(\Gamma \Phi_g y) = 0$.

**Proof of Theorem 18.1.5 with the text's hypothesis.** Given $f'(\alpha)$ onto and $\ker f'(\alpha)$ in good position to $C_\alpha C$, the equivalence of §XVIII.7 ⟦Chapter18/GoodPositionIntrinsic · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isGoodPositionGermTame_localRep_iff⟧ turns them into Part I's good position of $f_{\bar\Psi_\lambda}$ at $q_0$, for any $\bar\Psi_\lambda \in \bar F(\alpha)$ in the family and $\Psi_\lambda(q_0) = \alpha$; the model form then applies, and $\pi(\Gamma(\lambda, q_0)) = \pi(\alpha)$.

**Proof of Theorem 18.1.5 on $C_U$.** *Step 1.* The model form gives $R$ and the open $U = |\Gamma|(\pi(U(x_0)))$. *Step 2, every object over $U$ is isomorphic to some $\Gamma(y)$, $y \in U(x_0)$*: by definition of $U$ and the description of equal orbits. Zeros of $f$ move along isomorphisms, since $\Theta$ is a functor. *Step 3, local branching at the uniformizers of $C$.* Let $\Psi \in F(\alpha')$, $|\alpha'| \in U$, and $q$ over $U$ with $\Theta(\Psi(q)) > 0$. Choose $y$ with $\Gamma(y) \cong \Psi(q)$; then $f(\Gamma y) = 0$ and Step 6 of the model form gives a tame local branching structure $T$ at $y$. Part XVII moves it from the model to the summand $\Psi_j$ of the family ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · PolyfoldConstruction.localBranching_summand⟧, and along the transition element $(q, \varphi, q')$ built from the isomorphism to $\Psi$ at $q$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · PolyfoldConstruction.localBranching_of_tr⟧, keeping the tame sheets. *Step 4.* The criterion of §XVIII.6 (c) gives that $\Theta|C_U$ is a branched ep⁺-subcategory of the polyfold $C_U$ and tame; it is of orbifold type because $\Theta$ is. *Step 5, (2).* For $\beta$ with $\Theta|C_U(\beta) = 1$, Step 2 gives $y \in U(x_0)$ with $\Gamma(y) \cong \beta$ and $f(\Gamma y) = 0$, and the model form gives good position of $(f^{\bar\Psi}, \Lambda_0)$ at $y$.

**Proof of the Note on $C_U$.** For a tame orbifold-type branched ep⁺-subcategory $\Theta|C_U$ and every covering family of $C_U$, Proposition 17.5.5 (2) (Part XVII) ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSupport · PolyfoldConstruction.proposition_17_5_5_two⟧ gives that the support of the pulled-back $\Theta|C_U$ is an ep-subgroupoid and a smooth submanifold of the model, whose orbit space is homeomorphic to $|\mathrm{supp}(\Theta|C_U)|$ through $|\Gamma|$: $|\mathrm{supp}\,\Theta|$ is a smooth orbifold (G.239 settled).

*Witnesses.* On the reflection bundle, $f(x) = (x, x)$ over $\mathbb Z/2 \ltimes \mathbb R$, in the model with two copies of $\bar\Psi$: $f$ is sc-Fredholm with tame germ data, $f(0) = 0$, the representative (the graph of the identity) is in good position at $0$, $f \ne 0$ away from $0$, and $-1$ acts non-trivially on the fibre ⟦Chapter18/ImplicitFunctionTheoremExamples · ReflectionBundle.theorem_18_1_5_hyp_witness⟧; the conclusions of the model form ⟦Chapter18/ImplicitFunctionTheoremExamples · ReflectionBundle.theorem_18_1_5_hyp_applies⟧ and on $C_U$, with $\Theta|C_U(0) = 1$ and $C_U$ a tame polyfold with uniformizers ⟦Chapter18/OpenSubcategoryExamples · ReflectionBundle.theorem_18_1_5_subcategory_hyp_applies⟧. Weak: $\partial C = \emptyset$. With boundary: on the half-space bundle $\mathbb Z/2 \ltimes (H \times \mathbb R) \to \mathbb Z/2 \ltimes H$, $H = [0, \infty) \times \mathbb R$, with $f(a, y) = a^2 + y^2 - 1$, at the boundary zero $(0, 1)$: $C_\alpha C \ne T_\alpha C$, $f'(\alpha)$ is onto, and $\ker f'(\alpha)$ is non-zero, not contained in $T^R_\alpha C$ and in good position to $C_\alpha C$ ⟦Chapter18/GoodPositionIntrinsicExamples · HalfSpaceReflection.theorem_18_1_5_intrinsic_hyp_witness⟧ (from Part I's good position of the representative and the equivalence of §XVIII.7; the kernel is non-zero because $\dim T_\alpha C = 2 > 1$); the conclusions there ⟦Chapter18/GoodPositionIntrinsicExamples · HalfSpaceReflection.theorem_18_1_5_intrinsic_hyp_applies⟧. The zeros $(0, \pm 1)$ have trivial isotropy. At the corner, every $\mathbb Z/2$-invariant section of this bundle has derivative $(\partial_a\varphi, 0)$ (the fibre action is trivial), so the kernel of a surjective derivative is $T^R_\alpha C$, which is not in good position; the corner witness of §XVIII.7 is therefore stated for subspaces, not for a zero of a section.

### XVIII.9 Definition 18.1.7 and Lemma 18.1.8 (2017: Definition 18.4, Lemma 18.1), pp. 846–847: auxiliary norms

**Statement** (explicit for Definition 18.1.7, narrower for Lemma 18.1.8). *Definition 18.1.7.* An auxiliary norm is a functor $N : E \to [0, \infty]$ with (1) $N = +\infty$ off $E_{0,1}$, (2) a complete norm on every fibre of $E_{0,1}$, (3) if $|h_k| \in |E_{0,1}|$, $|P(h_k)| \to |\alpha|$ and $|N|(|h_k|) \to 0$, then $|h_k| \to |0_\alpha|$ in $|E_{0,1}|$. The printed form is ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirect · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsAuxNormBook⟧, with the fibre condition (2) ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirect · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.FiberCond⟧ through complete norms with values in $[0, \infty]$ ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirect · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsCompleteNormOn⟧; the form with the continuity of $|N|$ on $|E_{0,1}|$ is ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirect · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsAuxNormDirect⟧, with $|N|$ ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirect · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.oneNorm⟧. In a model: $N \circ \bar\Gamma$ on $W_{0,1}$ as a real function ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNorms · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.modelNorm⟧; $N$ is an auxiliary norm in the model if it is constant along morphisms, $N \circ \bar\Gamma = +\infty$ exactly off $W_{0,1}$, and $N \circ \bar\Gamma$ is an auxiliary norm of Definition 12.2.1 on $W_{0,1}$ ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNorms · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsAuxNormIn⟧; and an auxiliary norm in every covering model ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNorms · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsAuxNormFunctor⟧.

*Lemma 18.1.8.* $N$ is an auxiliary norm iff $N \circ \bar\Gamma_{\bar\Psi}$ is one for a covering family: ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirect · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.lemma_18_1_8⟧ for $\mathrm{IsAuxNormDirect}$, and with every covering family ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirect · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isAuxNormDirect_iff_isAuxNormFunctor⟧ (G.241 settled).

*The objects of bi-regularity $(0,1)$.* The text does not define $E_{0,1}$ for a strong polyfold bundle. We read $e \in E_{0,1}$ as "$|e| = |\bar\Gamma(w)|$ for some $w \in W_{0,1}$ of a covering model" ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirect · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsOneObj⟧, and one covering model suffices ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirect · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isOneObj_iff⟧: an object $w'$ of another model with the same image class moves to $w$ in the given one, and membership in $W_{0,1}$ does not depend on the model ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNorms · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.mem_oneSet_iff_of_iso⟧, through the comparison isomorphism and the saturation of $W_{0,1}$ ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNorms · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.mem_oneSet_of_π_eq⟧. $|E_{0,1}|$ is the set of these classes with the topology of $|W[1]|$ of a covering model ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirect · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.EOneOrbit⟧; that topology does not depend on the model ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirect · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.oneTopology_eq⟧, because the comparison map of two models is a homeomorphism of their $|W[1]|$ compatible with the bijections to $|E_{0,1}|$.

*Readings.* The text prints $|C_{0,1}|$ in (3) and $N : C \to [0, \infty]$ in Lemma 18.1.8; both are read on $E$. A functor into $[0, \infty]$ is read as a function on objects constant along morphisms (`IsMorInvariant`, the reading of Part III). **The continuity, a reading.** Definition 12.2.1, whose notion Definition 18.1.7 is said to import from the ep-groupoid context, asks the auxiliary norm to be continuous on $W_{0,1}$; the printed clauses of Definition 18.1.7 ask for no continuity of $|N|$ on $|E_{0,1}|$, and none of them forces it ($E$ carries no topology on its objects, a functor is only constant along morphisms, and (3) is a condition at the zero section). We read Definition 18.1.7 with the continuity of Definition 12.2.1 ($\mathrm{IsAuxNormDirect}$). With it Lemma 18.1.8 holds as an iff; for the printed form alone the direction ⇒ does not hold as we read it (below). Presumably Definition 18.1.7 includes this continuity.

**Proof that the model does not matter** ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNorms · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsAuxNormIn.of_comparison⟧ ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNorms · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isAuxNormFunctor_iff_model⟧. Let $c, c'$ be covering families and $N$ an auxiliary norm in the model of $c'$. *Step 1.* For every $w$ of $W_c$ there is $w'$ of $W_{c'}$ with $|\bar\Gamma'(w')| = |\bar\Gamma(w)|$ (essential surjectivity of $\bar\Gamma'$) ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNorms · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.exists_bundleModelObj_iso⟧; then $w' \in W'_{0,1}$ iff $w \in W_{0,1}$, and $N(\bar\Gamma'w') = N(\bar\Gamma w)$ by invariance. This gives the two conditions on $+\infty$. *Step 2.* On $W_{0,1}$, $N \circ \bar\Gamma$ is the pull-back of $N \circ \bar\Gamma'$ along the generalized strong bundle isomorphism $\bar f_{c,c'}$ of Theorem 17.1.18 ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNorms · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.modelNorm_eq_pullNorm⟧: the representative of $|\bar f|(|e|)$ in $|W'[1]|$ has image class $|\bar\Gamma(e)|$ ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNorms · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.π_bundleModelObj_oneOrbitMap⟧, by (18.3) for the comparison. *Step 3.* Pull-backs of auxiliary norms along generalized strong bundle isomorphisms are auxiliary norms (Proposition 11.5.1, Part XI) ⟦Chapter11/AuxiliaryNormTransport · SBLocalization.GenSBMap.isAuxNorm_pullNorm⟧.

**Proof of Lemma 18.1.8, ⇐** (from a covering model to the direct form). Let $N$ be an auxiliary norm in the model of $c$.

*Step 1, (1).* For $e \notin E_{0,1}$ choose $w$ with $|\bar\Gamma(w)| = |e|$ ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirect · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.exists_bundleModelObj_eq⟧; then $w \notin W_{0,1}$ and $N(e) = N(\bar\Gamma w) = +\infty$. *Step 2, (2).* Every $\alpha$ is isomorphic to some $\Gamma(x)$; the fibre condition of Definition 12.2.1 at $x$ is (2) at $\Gamma(x)$ ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirect · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isCompleteFiberNorm_iff_fiberCond⟧, since a real complete norm on $(W_{0,1})_x$ and a $[0,\infty]$-valued complete norm on its image under the injective fibre map are the same thing ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirect · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isCompleteNormOn_range_iff⟧; and (2) moves along the morphism to $\alpha$, $\mu(\varphi)$ being a linear isomorphism preserving $N$ and $E_{0,1}$ ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirect · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.fiberCond_of_isomorphic⟧. *Step 3, (3)* ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirect · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsAuxNormIn.tendsto_oneMk⟧. Write $h_k \cong \bar\Gamma(w_k)$ with $w_k \in W_{0,1}$ and $\alpha \cong \Gamma(x)$. The convergence $|P(h_k)| \to |\alpha|$ is, through the homeomorphism $|X_\Psi| \cong |C|$, the convergence of $|P(w_k)| \to |x|$; a lifting lemma for convergent orbit sequences gives objects $v_k \to x$ with $|v_k| = |P(w_k)|$, and morphisms $P(w_k) \to v_k$ move $w_k$ to $b_k$ over $v_k$ with the same image class and the same norm. Definition 12.2.1 (2) in the model gives $b_k \to 0_x$ in $W_{0,1}$, and the homeomorphism $|W[1]| \cong |E_{0,1}|$ ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirect · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.oneHomeomorph⟧ carries this to $|h_k| \to |0_\alpha|$. *Step 4, the continuity* ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirect · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsAuxNormIn.continuous_oneNorm⟧. $|N|$ composed with the homeomorphism is the quotient lift of the continuous invariant $N \circ \bar\Gamma$ on $W_{0,1}$. Steps 1–3 give the printed form ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirect · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsAuxNormIn.isAuxNormBook⟧, Step 4 the direct form ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirect · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsAuxNormIn.isAuxNormDirect⟧.

**Proof of Lemma 18.1.8, ⇒** ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirect · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsAuxNormDirect.isAuxNormIn⟧. Let $N$ satisfy Definition 18.1.7 with the continuity, and $c$ a covering family. *Step 1, $+\infty$.* $N \circ \bar\Gamma = +\infty$ off $W_{0,1}$ by (1), since $\bar\Gamma(w) \in E_{0,1}$ iff $w \in W_{0,1}$ ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirect · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isOneObj_bundleModelObj_iff⟧; it is finite on $W_{0,1}$ by (2). *Step 2, continuity on $W_{0,1}$.* $N \circ \bar\Gamma$ on $W_{0,1}$ is $|N|$ composed with the homeomorphism and the quotient map; $|N|$ is continuous and finite, so its real part is continuous. *Step 3, the fibres* by (2) and the fibre comparison of ⇐, Step 2. *Step 4, Definition 12.2.1 (2).* Let $P(w_k) \to x$ in $X_\Psi$ and $N(\bar\Gamma w_k) \to 0$. Then $|P(\bar\Gamma w_k)| \to |\Gamma x|$ and (3) gives $|\bar\Gamma w_k| \to |0_{\Gamma x}|$ in $|E_{0,1}|$, i.e. $|w_k| \to |0_x|$ in $|W[1]|$. To reach $w_k \to 0_x$ in $W_{0,1}$ the proof takes an auxiliary norm $N_0$ of the model (Theorem 12.2.2, Part XII, on the paracompact orbit space of the model) ⟦Chapter12/AuxNormEp · EpStrongBundle.theorem_12_2_2⟧ ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNorms · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.hasParacompactOrbitSpace_model⟧: $N_0$ is continuous and invariant, so it descends to $|W[1]|$, hence $N_0(w_k) \to N_0(0_x) = 0$, and Definition 12.2.1 (2) for $N_0$ gives $w_k \to 0_x$. *Step 5, invariance* along the morphisms of the model, from the invariance of $N$.

**Lemma 18.1.8 for the printed form.** The model condition is exactly the printed form together with the continuity of $|N|$ ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsBookForm · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isAuxNormIn_iff_isAuxNormBook_continuous⟧. Without the continuity, ⇒ does not hold as we read it ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsBookForm · ReflectionBundle.lemma_18_1_8_book_without_continuity⟧. *Step 1, rescaling by a function of the base.* For any $a \ge 1$ on $|C|$, continuous or not, $e \mapsto a(|P(e)|)\,N(e)$ ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsBookForm · VectorBundle.baseScale⟧ satisfies the printed clauses when $N$ does ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsBookForm · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsAuxNormBook.baseScale⟧: (1) and (2) because $a$ is a positive constant on each fibre, (3) because $N \le aN$. *Step 2, the continuity sees $a$.* If $N$ and $aN$ both have continuous $|\cdot|$, then $a \circ |P| = |aN|/|N|$ is continuous wherever $|N| \ne 0$ ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsBookForm · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsAuxNormDirect.continuousAt_baseScale⟧ ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsBookForm · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsAuxNormDirect.not_isAuxNormDirect_baseScale⟧. *Step 3, the reflection bundle.* On $E \to \mathbb Z/2 \ltimes \mathbb R$ with $-1$ acting by $v \mapsto -v$, let $a(|0|) = 1$ at the point with isotropy $\mathbb Z/2$ and $a = 2$ elsewhere ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsBookForm · ReflectionBundle.reflJump⟧. Along the continuous path $t \mapsto |(t, 1)|$ in $|E_{0,1}|$, read in the model of one copy of $\bar\Psi$, $a \circ |P|$ is $2$ for $t \ne 0$ and $1$ at $t = 0$ ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsBookForm · ReflectionBundle.not_continuousAt_reflJump_oneBase⟧, and $|N_0|(|(0, 1)|) \ne 0$ by definiteness. *Step 4.* For an auxiliary norm $N_0$ (one exists, §XVIII.11), $aN_0$ satisfies the printed clauses and is an auxiliary norm in no covering model ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsBookForm · ReflectionBundle.isAuxNormBook_reflJump⟧ ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsBookForm · ReflectionBundle.reflQ_baseScale_witness⟧. The witness is non-degenerate: a non-trivial isotropy and a genuine jump of the factor at that point.

*Witnesses.* Both forms of Definition 18.1.7 are satisfiable on the reflection bundle ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirectExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflQ_exists_isAuxNormDirect⟧ ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirectExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflQ_exists_isAuxNormBook⟧; with boundary, $N(\alpha, w) = |w|$ is an auxiliary norm of the half-space bundle ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceBoundaryData · HalfSpaceReflection.isAuxNormFunctor_halfNorm⟧: since the fibres are $\mathbb R$, every auxiliary norm $N_0$ is $|w|\,N_0(\alpha, 1)$, and $|w| = N_0(\alpha, w)/N_0(\alpha, 1)$ is $N_0$ times a continuous positive weight.

### XVIII.10 Definitions 18.1.9–18.1.11 (2017: Definitions 18.5–18.7), p. 847: reflexive fibres and reflexive auxiliary norms

**Statement** (same for Definitions 18.1.9 and 18.1.10, explicit for Definition 18.1.11). *Definition 18.1.9 and the paragraph after it.* $P$ has reflexive $(0, 1)$-fibres if the $(0,1)$-fibres are reflexive; the text adds that then any sc-smooth model has reflexive $(0, 1)$-fibres. Reflexive fibres in a model are Definition 5.1.4 for the object bundle of the model ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNorms · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.HasReflexiveFibersIn⟧, and reflexive fibres of $P$ are reflexive fibres in every covering model ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNorms · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.HasReflexiveFibers⟧; one model suffices ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNorms · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.hasReflexiveFibers_iff_model⟧ (G.242 settled), and the pieces $K_\lambda$ of a model inherit them ⟦Chapter18/ReflexivePieces · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.HasReflexiveFibersIn.hasReflexivePieces⟧ ⟦Chapter18/ReflexivePieces · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.hasReflexivePieces_iff⟧, with the pieces' condition ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirect · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.HasReflexivePieces⟧.

*Definition 18.1.10*, in the model reading: $N$ is a reflexive auxiliary norm in a model if it is an auxiliary norm there and $N \circ \bar\Gamma$ is a reflexive auxiliary norm of Definition 12.2.10 ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNorms · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsReflexiveAuxNormIn⟧; in every covering model ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNorms · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsReflexiveAuxNormFunctor⟧; one model with reflexive fibres suffices ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNorms · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isReflexiveAuxNormFunctor_iff_model⟧.

*Definition 18.1.11*, in the direct form. Mixed convergence $y_k \to y$ in $|E_{0,1}|$ ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirect · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.MixedTendstoOne⟧: $|P|(y_k) \to |P|(y)$, and for some strong bundle uniformizer $\bar\Psi : G \ltimes K \to E$ of $\bar F$ with $\Psi(x)$ representing $|P|(y)$ there are $e_k$, $e$ in $K_{0,1}$ with $|\bar\Psi(e_k)| = y_k$ for large $k$, $p(e) = x$, $|\bar\Psi(e)| = y$, and $e_k \to e$ mixed convergent (Definition 5.1.6 in $K$). The printed condition, with suitable representatives $N(e) \le \liminf N(e_k)$ ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirect · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.HasMixedLscReps⟧, and the alternative $|N|(y) \le \liminf |N|(y_k)$ ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirect · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.HasMixedLsc⟧ agree for a functor $N$ ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirect · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.hasMixedLscReps_iff_hasMixedLsc⟧, since the value at any representative is $|N|$. A reflexive auxiliary norm is an auxiliary norm in the direct form with the printed condition ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirect · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsReflexiveAuxNormDirect⟧, equivalently with the alternative ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirect · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isReflexiveAuxNormDirect_iff⟧.

*Readings.* "For a choice of uniformizer $\bar\Psi$" is read as "for some strong bundle uniformizer of $\bar F$", and the representatives $e_k$ are asked for large $k$. The comparison of Definition 18.1.11 with the model reading uses reflexive $(0,1)$-fibres (`hB`), as the text presupposes in Definition 18.1.11.

**Proof that reflexive fibres do not depend on the model.** Reflexivity of $(0,1)$-fibres is preserved by generalized strong bundle isomorphisms (Part XVI) ⟦Chapter16/ReflexivePush · SBLocalization.GenSBMap.hasReflexiveFibers_iff⟧, applied to the comparison isomorphism $\bar f_{c,c'}$ ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNorms · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.hasReflexiveFibersIn_iff⟧.

**Proof that the pieces of a model have reflexive fibres** ⟦Chapter18/ReflexivePieces · StrongBundle.hasReflexiveFibers_of_sigma⟧. Let $\bigsqcup K_\lambda \to \bigsqcup O_\lambda$ have reflexive $(0,1)$-fibres and $x \in O_\lambda$. Take a chart of the disjoint union around $(\lambda, x)$ with reflexive $F_1$; restrict it to the open summand $\mathrm{range}(\mathrm{mk}_\lambda)$, which keeps the fibre model $F$; carry it to $K_\lambda$ along the inclusion of the piece, a local strong bundle isomorphism, which keeps the model $K \triangleleft F$. The result is a chart of $K_\lambda$ around $x$ with the same reflexive $F_1$. The converse holds as well ⟦Chapter18/ReflexivePieces · StrongBundle.hasReflexiveFibers_sigma_iff⟧, so the text's sentence after Definition 18.1.9 is proved in both directions.

**Proof that reflexivity of norms does not depend on the model** ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNorms · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsReflexiveAuxNormIn.of_comparison⟧. The auxiliary norm part is §XVIII.9; on $W_{0,1}$, $N \circ \bar\Gamma$ is the pull-back of $N \circ \bar\Gamma'$ along $\bar f_{c,c'}$, and reflexive auxiliary norms pull back to reflexive ones along generalized strong bundle isomorphisms when the target has reflexive fibres (Part XVI) ⟦Chapter16/MixedTransfer · SBLocalization.GenSBMap.isReflexiveAuxNorm_pullNorm⟧.

**Proof that the model reading gives Definition 18.1.11** ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirect · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsReflexiveAuxNormFunctor.hasMixedLsc⟧ ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirect · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsReflexiveAuxNormFunctor.isReflexiveAuxNormDirect⟧. Let $y_k \to y$ be mixed convergent through the uniformizer $\bar\Psi$. *Step 1.* Adjoin $\bar\Psi$ to a covering family $c$ ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirect · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.BundleCoveringFamily.withUnif⟧; then $K$ is a piece of the model of $c \sqcup \{\bar\Psi\}$. *Step 2.* Mixed convergence in a piece is mixed convergence in the disjoint union, through the pushed chart ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirect · StrongBundle.MixedTendsto.sigmaMk⟧. *Step 3.* Definition 12.2.10 (3) in that model gives $N(e) \le \liminf N(e_k)$ for the real parts; since all values are finite, this is the inequality in $[0, \infty]$ ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirect · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.le_liminf_iff_toReal⟧, and the values at representatives are $|N|(y)$, $|N|(y_k)$. With §XVIII.9 for the auxiliary norm part, $N$ is a reflexive auxiliary norm in the direct form.

**Proof of the converse** ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirect · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsReflexiveAuxNormDirect.isReflexiveAuxNormIn_hyp⟧, when the pieces of $c$ have reflexive $(0,1)$-fibres. Let $w_k \to w$ be mixed convergent in $W_{0,1}$, $w$ in the piece $K_\lambda$. *Step 1.* For large $k$ the $w_k$ lie in $K_\lambda$ and are mixed convergent there ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirect · StrongBundle.MixedTendsto.exists_summand⟧; this is where reflexivity of the piece enters, making the pushed charts available for the weak convergence. *Step 2.* So $|\bar\Gamma(w_k)| \to |\bar\Gamma(w)|$ is mixed convergent in the sense of Definition 18.1.10 with $\bar\Psi = \bar\Psi_\lambda$, and Definition 18.1.11 gives $|N|(|\bar\Gamma w|) \le \liminf |N|(|\bar\Gamma w_k|)$, which is Definition 12.2.10 (3) for $N \circ \bar\Gamma$. Hence Definition 18.1.11 and the model reading agree when the pieces have reflexive fibres ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirect · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isReflexiveAuxNormDirect_iff_hyp⟧, and, by the previous paragraph on pieces, for a bundle with reflexive $(0,1)$-fibres in the text's sense ⟦Chapter18/ReflexivePieces · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isReflexiveAuxNormDirect_iff_of_hasReflexiveFibersIn⟧ ⟦Chapter18/ReflexivePieces · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isReflexiveAuxNormDirect_iff_of_hasReflexiveFibers⟧; the hypothesis of the `_hyp` form is thereby the text's own.

*Witnesses.* On the reflection bundle: reflexive fibres ⟦Chapter18/CompactnessExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflQ_hasReflexiveFibers⟧; the pieces of the model with two copies of $\bar\Psi$ have reflexive fibres, the fibre $\mathbb R$ being finite-dimensional ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirectExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflPair_hasReflexivePieces⟧; Definition 18.1.11 is satisfiable ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirectExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflQ_exists_isReflexiveAuxNormDirect⟧, and it agrees with the model reading ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirectExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflQ_isReflexiveAuxNormDirect_iff⟧. With boundary: reflexive fibres of the half-space bundle ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceWitnesses · HalfSpaceReflection.halfQ_hasReflexiveFibers⟧. All examples are finite-dimensional, so reflexivity is automatic in them; the witnesses show the hypotheses satisfiable, not that they are restrictive.

### XVIII.11 The paragraph before Theorem 18.1.12 and Theorem 18.1.12 (2017: Theorem 18.2), pp. 847–848

**Statement** (same for the paragraph, explicit for the theorem). *The paragraph before Theorem 18.1.12.* An auxiliary norm $n$ of a model pushes forward to an auxiliary norm $N = \bar\Gamma_* n$ of $P$, and the notions around auxiliary norms do not depend on the model (G.243 settled). Formally: for an invariant $n$ on $W_{0,1}$, the function equal to $n$ on $W_{0,1}$ and $+\infty$ off it ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNorms · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.extendNorm⟧ pushes forward along $\bar\Gamma$ to a functor on $E$ ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNorms · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.pushNormFun⟧, which is an auxiliary norm in the model when $n$ is one ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNorms · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isAuxNormIn_pushNormFun⟧, and reflexive when $n$ is ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNorms · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isReflexiveAuxNormIn_pushNormFun⟧. Auxiliary norms exist, and reflexive ones for reflexive fibres ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNorms · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.exists_isAuxNormFunctor⟧ ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNorms · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.exists_isReflexiveAuxNormFunctor⟧. The sublevel sets $\{z \in |C| \mid |N| \circ |f|(z) \le C\}$ ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNorms · SectionFunctor.sublevel⟧ correspond to those of the model ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNorms · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.image_auxLe_eq⟧.

*Theorem 18.1.12.* (1) An auxiliary norm exists ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirect · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.theorem_18_1_12_one⟧. (2) With reflexive $(0,1)$-fibres, every auxiliary norm $N'$ lies between two reflexive ones, $N_1 \le N' \le N_2$ ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirect · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.theorem_18_1_12_two⟧. (3) Over a tame polyfold an auxiliary norm on $E|\partial C$ extends to one of $P$ ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirect · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.theorem_18_1_12_three⟧, and with reflexive fibres a reflexive one extends to a reflexive one ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirect · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.theorem_18_1_12_three_reflexive⟧.

*Hypotheses and readings.* `hF`, $\bar F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ for every $\alpha$ ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.HasStrongBundleUniformizers⟧, in (1), the standing reading; it gives a covering family, one $\bar\Psi_\alpha \in \bar F(\alpha)$ for every object ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNorms · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.refCoveringFamily⟧. (2) and (3) take a covering family $c$. `hB`, reflexive fibres, in (2) and the reflexive (3), as the text has. `hT`, tameness of $C$, in (3), as printed. In (3) an auxiliary norm on $E|\partial C$ is read in the covering model $c$ ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirect · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsBoundaryAuxNormIn⟧: $N$ constant along the morphisms starting over $\partial C$, $N \circ \bar\Gamma = +\infty$ exactly off $W_{0,1}$ over $\partial X$, and $N \circ \bar\Gamma$ an auxiliary norm of $W|\partial X$ (Definition 12.2.1 over $\partial X$, as Theorem 12.2.7 takes it); for the reflexive form, in addition lower semicontinuity along mixed convergent sequences over $\partial X$ ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirect · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsReflexiveBoundaryAuxNormIn⟧. "Extended to $P$" means an auxiliary norm $\tilde N$ with $\tilde N = N$ over $\partial C$. The sentence after the theorem, that the reader may transport other results, is an invitation (G.244, nothing to formalize).

**Proof of the push-forward.** *Step 1.* $n$ extended by $+\infty$ is constant on isomorphism classes of the model: if $|w| = |w'|$ then $w \in W_{0,1}$ iff $w' \in W_{0,1}$, and $n$ is invariant on $W_{0,1}$ ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNorms · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.extendNorm_eq_of_π_eq⟧. *Step 2.* An invariant function on the objects of the model pushes forward along $\bar\Gamma$ (essentially surjective, full and faithful on orbit classes) to an invariant function on $E$ with $N \circ \bar\Gamma$ equal to it ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNorms · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.bundlePull_pushNormFun⟧. *Step 3.* So $N \circ \bar\Gamma = +\infty$ exactly off $W_{0,1}$, and its real part on $W_{0,1}$ is $n$ ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNorms · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.modelNorm_pushNormFun⟧, an auxiliary norm (reflexive if $n$ is).

**Proof of existence.** *Step 1.* The orbit space of a covering model is paracompact, being homeomorphic to the metrizable $|C|$ ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · PolyfoldConstruction.metrizableSpace_modelOrbitSpace_of_family⟧ ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNorms · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.hasParacompactOrbitSpace_model⟧. *Step 2.* Theorem 12.2.2 gives an auxiliary norm of the model ⟦Chapter12/AuxNormEp · EpStrongBundle.theorem_12_2_2⟧, and Theorem 12.2.11 a reflexive one for reflexive fibres ⟦Chapter12/ReflexiveAuxNormEp · EpStrongBundle.theorem_12_2_11_exists⟧. *Step 3.* Push forward, and one covering model suffices (§§XVIII.9–XVIII.10).

**Proof of Theorem 18.1.12 (1).** The existence for the model of $\mathrm{refCoveringFamily}$, and Lemma 18.1.8 ⇐ to reach the direct form.

**Proof of Theorem 18.1.12 (2).** *Step 1.* In the model of $c$, Theorem 12.2.11 in its sandwich form gives reflexive $n_1 \le N' \circ \bar\Gamma \le n_2$ on $W_{0,1}$ ⟦Chapter12/ReflexiveAuxNormEp · EpStrongBundle.theorem_12_2_11_sandwich⟧. *Step 2.* Push $n_1, n_2$ forward to $N_1, N_2$, reflexive auxiliary norms. *Step 3, the inequalities at every $e$.* Choose $w$ with $|\bar\Gamma(w)| = |e|$; the values of the three functions at $e$ are their values at $\bar\Gamma(w)$ ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirect · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.bundlePull_eq_of_π_eq⟧. If $w \in W_{0,1}$ the inequalities are those of Step 1; otherwise all three are $+\infty$.

**Proof of Theorem 18.1.12 (3).** *Step 1.* The model of a tame polyfold is a tame ep-groupoid ⟦Chapter17/ModelEpGroupoid · PolyfoldConstruction.isTame_modelEpGroupoid⟧. *Step 2.* Theorem 12.2.7 (Part XII) extends the auxiliary norm $N \circ \bar\Gamma$ of $W|\partial X$ to an auxiliary norm $n$ of the model agreeing with it over $\partial X$ ⟦Chapter12/AuxNormLocalExtension · EpStrongBundle.theorem_12_2_7⟧; in the reflexive case Theorem 12.2.13 does so with reflexive $n$ ⟦Chapter12/ReflexiveLocalExtension · EpStrongBundle.theorem_12_2_13⟧. *Step 3.* Push $n$ forward to $\tilde N$. *Step 4, $\tilde N = N$ over $\partial C$* ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirect · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.pushNormFun_eq_of_boundary⟧. For $e$ over $\partial C$ choose $w$ with $|\bar\Gamma(w)| = |e|$; then $P(w) \in \partial X$ ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirect · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.modelP_mem_boundary⟧, through the description of $\partial X$ by the image of $\partial C$ ⟦Chapter18/Compactness · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.mem_boundary_model_iff⟧; $N(e) = N(\bar\Gamma w)$, by the invariance of $N$ along the morphism from $e$, which starts over $\partial C$; and on $W_{0,1}$ over $\partial X$, $n = N \circ \bar\Gamma$, while off $W_{0,1}$ both are $+\infty$. An auxiliary norm of $P$ restricts to one on $E|\partial C$ ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirect · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsAuxNormIn.isBoundaryAuxNormIn⟧ ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirect · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsReflexiveAuxNormIn.isReflexiveBoundaryAuxNormIn⟧, so the hypothesis is the restriction condition the text has in mind.

*Witnesses.* On the reflection bundle (weak: $\partial C = \emptyset$): (2) with an auxiliary norm and reflexive fibres ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirectExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflQ_theorem_18_1_12_two⟧; (3) with the restriction of an auxiliary norm ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirectExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflQ_theorem_18_1_12_three⟧ and its reflexive form ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirectExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflQ_theorem_18_1_12_three_reflexive⟧. With $\partial C \ne \emptyset$, on the half-space bundle: $N(\alpha, w) = |w|$ over $\partial C$ and $+\infty$ elsewhere is an auxiliary norm on $E|\partial C$ that is not an auxiliary norm of $P$ (it is $+\infty$ at the zero vector over the interior point $(1, 0)$) ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceBoundaryData · HalfSpaceReflection.not_isAuxNormFunctor_boundaryOnly⟧, and (3) extends it to $\tilde N$ with $\tilde N(\alpha, w) = |w|$ at every $\alpha \in \partial C$, the corner among them ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceBoundaryData · HalfSpaceReflection.theorem_18_1_12_three_witness_boundary⟧; the same for a reflexive auxiliary norm cut off to $\partial C$ ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceBoundaryData · HalfSpaceReflection.theorem_18_1_12_three_reflexive_witness_boundary⟧.

### XVIII.12 (18.3), Definition 18.2.1 and Theorem 18.2.2 (2017: Definition 18.8, Theorem 18.3), pp. 849–850: the solution set and compactness

§18.2 carries the compactness notions of §12.4 (Part XII) from the ep-groupoid level to a strong polyfold bundle $P : E \to C$. Everything is done by one device: a covering family $\bar\Psi$ of strong bundle uniformizers gives an sc-smooth model $p : W_{\bar\Psi} \to X_\Psi$ with functors $\Gamma : X_\Psi \to C$ and $\bar\Gamma : W_{\bar\Psi} \to E$ whose orbit maps are homeomorphisms (Part XVII), and the section functor $f$ has its model section $f^{\bar\Psi}$ with $\bar\Gamma \circ f^{\bar\Psi} = f \circ \Gamma$ (§XVIII.1). A statement about $|S_f| \subset |C|$ is moved to $|X_\Psi|$, proved there by Part XII, and moved back.

**The solution set.** $S_f$ is the set of objects $\alpha$ of $C$ with $f(\alpha) = (\alpha, 0)$ ⟦Chapter18/ModelTransfer · SectionFunctor.zeroSet⟧; the formalization keeps it as a set of objects, the full subcategory being determined by it, and $|S_f|$ is its image $\pi(S_f)$ in $|C|$. It is saturated: if $\alpha \in S_f$ and $\varphi : \alpha \to \beta$, then $f(\beta) = \mu(\varphi)f(\alpha) = \mu(\varphi)(\alpha, 0) = (\beta, 0)$, using that a section functor commutes with the action of morphisms ⟦Chapter18/SectionFunctors · SectionFunctor.toFun_eq_act⟧ and that the action of a morphism sends a zero vector to the zero vector ⟦Chapter18/ModelTransfer · act_objMk_zero⟧; this is ⟦Chapter18/Compactness · SectionFunctor.mem_zeroSet_of_isomorphic⟧.

**Statement of (18.3) for the solution sets** (restated). For a covering family, $|\Gamma|(|S_{f^{\bar\Psi}}|) = |S_f|$ ⟦Chapter18/ModelTransfer · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.image_solutionSet_modelSection⟧, and therefore $|S_f|$ is compact iff $|S_{f^{\bar\Psi}}|$ is ⟦Chapter18/ModelTransfer · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isCompact_zeroSet_iff⟧. The text states (18.3) for the whole map $|f| = |\bar\Gamma| \circ |f^{\bar\Psi}| \circ |\Gamma|^{-1}$ (§XVIII.1); what §18.2 uses is its consequence for the zero sets, stated here.

*Step 1, zeros correspond pointwise* ⟦Chapter18/ModelTransfer · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isZeroAt_modelSection_iff⟧. For an object $x$ of $X_\Psi$, $f^{\bar\Psi}(x) = 0_x$ iff $\Gamma(x) \in S_f$. The fibre map $\bar\Gamma_x : W_x \to \mu(\Gamma(x))$ is a linear isomorphism, so $\bar\Gamma(0_x) = (\Gamma(x), 0)$. If $f^{\bar\Psi}(x) = 0_x$, then $f(\Gamma(x)) = \bar\Gamma(f^{\bar\Psi}(x)) = (\Gamma(x), 0)$ by the defining identity of the model section ⟦Chapter18/SectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.bundleModelFunctor_obj_modelSection⟧. Conversely, both $f^{\bar\Psi}(x)$ and $0_x$ lie over $x$ and have the same image under $\bar\Gamma$, and $\bar\Gamma$ is injective on each fibre, so they agree. The model solution set $S_{f^{\bar\Psi}}$ is Part XII's solution set of the model section ⟦Chapter18/ModelTransfer · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.modelSolutionSet⟧, and Step 1 reads $x \in S_{f^{\bar\Psi}} \iff \Gamma(x) \in S_f$ ⟦Chapter18/ModelTransfer · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.mem_modelSolutionSet_iff⟧.

*Step 2, the images agree.* The inclusion $|\Gamma|(|S_{f^{\bar\Psi}}|) \subseteq |S_f|$ is Step 1. For the other, let $\alpha \in S_f$. The model functor of a covering family is essentially surjective ⟦Chapter17/ModelEquivalence · PolyfoldConstruction.modelFunctor_essSurj⟧, so $\Gamma(x) \cong \alpha$ for some $x$; by saturation $\Gamma(x) \in S_f$, so $x \in S_{f^{\bar\Psi}}$ by Step 1, and $|\Gamma|(|x|) = |\alpha|$.

*Step 3, compactness.* $|\Gamma|$ is a homeomorphism $|X_\Psi| \to |C|$ for a covering family (Lemma 17.1.15) ⟦Chapter17/ModelEquivalence · PolyfoldConstruction.isHomeomorph_modelOrbitMap⟧, and a homeomorphism preserves and reflects compactness of images.

**The sublevel sets.** For a functor $N : E \to [0, \infty]$ and $c \in [0, \infty]$, the set $\{|N| \circ |f| \le c\} \subset |C|$ is defined as $\pi\{\alpha \mid N(f(\alpha)) \le c\}$ ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNorms · SectionFunctor.sublevel⟧; since $N$ and $f$ are constant along morphisms, $|\alpha|$ lies in it iff $N(f(\alpha)) \le c$ for one, equivalently every, representative ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNorms · SectionFunctor.mem_sublevel_iff⟧. The text's $\{x \in U \mid |N| \circ |f|(x) \le C\}$ is $U \cap \{|N| \circ |f| \le C\}$.

*Readings.* The auxiliary norms are those of Definition 18.1.7 with the continuity of Definition 12.2.1, read through the models (§XVIII.9): $N$ is an auxiliary norm in the model of $\bar\Psi$ if $N$ is constant along morphisms, $N \circ \bar\Gamma = +\infty$ exactly off $W_{0,1}$, and $N \circ \bar\Gamma$ on $W_{0,1}$ is an auxiliary norm of Definitions 8.3.5 and 12.2.1 ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNorms · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsAuxNormIn⟧; an auxiliary norm is one in every covering model ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNorms · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsAuxNormFunctor⟧, which one covering model already gives ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNorms · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isAuxNormFunctor_iff_model⟧, and which is the direct form on $E$ by Lemma 18.1.8 ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirect · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isAuxNormDirect_iff_isAuxNormFunctor⟧. The standing reading $\bar F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ for every $\alpha$ ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.HasStrongBundleUniformizers⟧ provides the covering family used in the proofs, one uniformizer at every object ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNorms · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.refCoveringFamily⟧. Its witnesses are the reflection bundle ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctorsExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflQ_hasStrongBundleUniformizers⟧ and the half-space bundle ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceWitnesses · HalfSpaceReflection.halfQ_hasStrongBundleUniformizers⟧, the second with boundary.

**Statement of Definition 18.2.1** (same). (1) $f$ defines a compact moduli space if $|S_f|$ is compact ⟦Chapter18/Compactness · SectionFunctor.HasCompactModuliSpace⟧. (2) $f$ is proper if moreover, for every auxiliary norm $N$, there is an open $U \supseteq |S_f|$ with $\mathrm{cl}_{|C|}(U \cap \{|N| \circ |f| \le 1\})$ compact ⟦Chapter18/Compactness · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsProper⟧. (3) $f$ is proper$_{<\infty}$ if moreover one such $U$ works for every real $0 < C < \infty$ ⟦Chapter18/Compactness · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsProperLtInfty⟧, with $C$ entering as $\mathrm{ofReal}(C) \in [0, \infty]$. The 2021 text states the definition for sc-smooth section functors and adds (2) and (3) to the 2017 Definition 18.8; the definitions are taken for every section functor and used for sc-Fredholm ones.

**The transfer lemmas.** Three facts carry the topology of the model to $|C|$.

*(a)* For $U$ open in $X_\Psi$, $|\Gamma|(\pi(U))$ is open in $|C|$ ⟦Chapter18/Compactness · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isOpen_modelImage⟧: $\pi(U)$ is open in $|X_\Psi|$ by Proposition 7.1.8 (Part VII), and $|\Gamma|$ is a homeomorphism.

*(b)* For every set $A$ of objects of $X_\Psi$ and every real $C \ge 0$, $|\Gamma|(\pi\{x \in A \mid N(f^{\bar\Psi}(x)) \le C\}) = |\Gamma|(\pi(A)) \cap \{|N| \circ |f| \le C\}$ ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNorms · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.image_auxLe_eq⟧. Here "$N(f^{\bar\Psi}(x)) \le C$" is Part XII's relation $\mathrm{AuxLe}$ for the model norm $N \circ \bar\Gamma$ on $W_{0,1}$ ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNorms · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.modelNorm⟧, which includes $f^{\bar\Psi}(x) \in W_{0,1}$. It is equivalent to $N(f(\Gamma(x))) \le C$ ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNorms · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.auxLe_modelNorm_iff⟧, because $N \circ \bar\Gamma \circ f^{\bar\Psi} = N \circ f \circ \Gamma$ ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNorms · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.bundlePull_modelSection⟧ and $N \circ \bar\Gamma = \infty$ off $W_{0,1}$; then (b) follows element by element with `mem_sublevel_iff`.

*(c)* Compactness of closures transfers: $\mathrm{cl}_{|C|}(|\Gamma|(\pi A) \cap \{|N| \circ |f| \le C\})$ is compact iff $\mathrm{cl}_{|X_\Psi|}\pi\{x \in A \mid N(f^{\bar\Psi}(x)) \le C\}$ is ⟦Chapter18/Compactness · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isCompact_closure_modelImage_iff⟧. By (b) the first set is the image of the second under the homeomorphism $|\Gamma|$, which commutes with closures.

From these: if the model section is proper$_{<\infty}$ in Part XII's sense, then $f$ is proper$_{<\infty}$ ⟦Chapter18/Compactness · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isProperLtInfty_of_model⟧. The compactness of $|S_f|$ comes from `isCompact_zeroSet_iff`; for an auxiliary norm $N$, the model gives an open saturated $U \supseteq S_{f^{\bar\Psi}}$ with the closures compact for $N \circ \bar\Gamma$, and $|\Gamma|(\pi U)$ is open by (a), contains $|S_f|$ by `image_solutionSet_modelSection`, and has compact closures by (c).

**Statement of Theorem 18.2.2** (explicit). For an sc-Fredholm section functor $f$, under $\bar F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$, the three conditions of Definition 18.2.1 are equivalent ⟦Chapter18/Compactness · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.theorem_18_2_2⟧. The added hypothesis is the standing reading `HasStrongBundleUniformizers`, with the witnesses above.

**Proof.** The equivalence is proved as a cycle (1) ⇒ (3) ⇒ (2) ⇒ (1).

*Step 1, (1) ⇒ (3).* Take the covering family $c$ of `refCoveringFamily`. The model section is sc-Fredholm by Remark 18.1.2 (a) in the model (§XVIII.2) ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.remark_18_1_2_a_model⟧, here in the form ⟦Chapter18/Compactness · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.modelSection_isScFredholm⟧. Its solution set has compact orbit space by `isCompact_zeroSet_iff`. Part XII's implication "compact solution set ⇒ proper$_{<\infty}$", the step (2) ⇒ (3) of Theorem 12.4.2 ⟦Chapter12/CompactnessEp · EpStrongBundle.isProperLtInftyFunctor_of_hasCompactSolutionSetEp⟧ ⟦Chapter12/CompactnessEp · EpStrongBundle.theorem_12_4_2⟧, makes the model section proper$_{<\infty}$, and `isProperLtInfty_of_model` carries this to $f$.

*Step 2, (3) ⇒ (2).* Take $C = 1$.

*Step 3, (2) ⇒ (1).* Part of the definition.

The text cites Theorem 12.4.2 in an sc-smooth model; the formal proof uses exactly its implication (2) ⇒ (3), and paracompactness of the model, which Theorem 12.4.2 carries, is not needed for it.

**Point G.245.** The paragraph before Definition 18.2.1 notes that a solution object is smooth, since $f$ is regularizing. Its step on levels, that $f(\alpha) \in E_{m, m+1}$ forces $\alpha \in C_{m+1}$, is the level form of Remark 18.1.2 (b) ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.remark_18_1_2_b_levels⟧. Read through a strong bundle uniformizer with $\Psi(q) = \alpha$ and applied at every level, it gives that a zero is smooth ⟦Chapter18/SolutionRegularity · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isSmoothObj_of_mem_zeroSet⟧; so $f(\alpha) = 0$ with $\bar F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ gives $\alpha \in C_\infty$ ⟦Chapter18/SolutionRegularity · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.solution_isSmoothObj⟧ and $S_f \subseteq C_\infty$ ⟦Chapter18/SolutionRegularity · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.zeroSet_subset_isSmoothObj⟧ (point G.245 settled); on the reflection bundle ⟦Chapter18/SolutionRegularity · ReflectionBundle.reflDiag_zeroSet_subset_isSmoothObj⟧. The theorems of §18.2 use the zero set directly and do not need it.

**Witnesses.** On the reflection bundle ($C = \mathbb Z/2 \ltimes \mathbb R$, the fibre $\mathbb R$ with $-1$ acting by $v \mapsto -v$, $f(x) = (x, x)$), $S_f = \{0\}$ ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctorsExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflDiag_zeroSet⟧, so $|S_f|$ is compact ⟦Chapter18/CompactnessExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflDiag_hasCompactModuliSpace⟧, and Theorem 18.2.2 gives proper ⟦Chapter18/CompactnessExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflDiag_isProper⟧ and proper$_{<\infty}$ ⟦Chapter18/CompactnessExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflDiag_isProperLtInfty⟧; auxiliary norms exist there ⟦Chapter18/CompactnessExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflQ_exists_isAuxNormFunctor⟧, so (2) and (3) are not vacuous. On the half-space bundle ($\mathbb Z/2 \ltimes H$ with $H = [0, \infty) \times \mathbb R$, $-1$ acting by $(a, y) \mapsto (a, -y)$, trivially on the fibre $\mathbb R$, and $f(a, y) = a^2 + y^2 - 1$), $S_f$ is the half circle ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceWitnesses · HalfSpaceReflection.halfF_zeroSet⟧, compact ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceWitnesses · HalfSpaceReflection.halfF_hasCompactModuliSpace⟧, meeting $\partial C$ at $(0, \pm 1)$ ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceWitnesses · HalfSpaceReflection.topPoint_mem_zeroSet_boundarySet⟧, and $f$ is proper$_{<\infty}$ ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceWitnesses · HalfSpaceReflection.halfF_isProperLtInfty⟧.

### XVIII.13 Definitions 18.2.3, 18.2.4 and Remark 18.2.5 (2017: Definitions 18.9, 18.10, Remark 18.3), p. 850

**Statement of Definition 18.2.3** (same). For a functor $N : E \to [0, \infty]$ and an open neighbourhood $U$ of $|S_f|$, $(N, U)$ controls compactness if $\mathrm{cl}_{|C|}(U \cap \{|N| \circ |f| \le 1\})$ is compact ⟦Chapter18/Compactness · SectionFunctor.ControlsCompactness⟧, and strongly controls compactness if this holds with $1$ replaced by every real $0 < C < \infty$ ⟦Chapter18/Compactness · SectionFunctor.StronglyControlsCompactness⟧. In both, "$U$ open and $U \supseteq |S_f|$" is part of the definition. Strongly controlling implies controlling, by $C = 1$ ⟦Chapter18/Compactness · SectionFunctor.StronglyControlsCompactness.controlsCompactness⟧. The 2021 text adds "strongly controls compactness" to the 2017 Definition 18.9.

**Statement of the observation after Definition 18.2.3** (same). For sc-Fredholm $f$ with compact moduli space and an auxiliary norm $N$, under $\bar F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$: $(N, U)$ controls compactness for some $U$ iff $(N, U')$ strongly controls compactness for some $U'$ ⟦Chapter18/Compactness · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.definition_18_2_3_observation⟧. It is proved through a sharper form: inside every open neighbourhood $U$ of $|S_f|$ there is a $U' \subseteq U$ that strongly controls compactness ⟦Chapter18/Compactness · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.exists_stronglyControlsCompactness_subset⟧ (point G.246 settled).

**Proof of the sharper form.** *Step 1.* By Theorem 18.2.2 ((1) ⇒ (3), §XVIII.12), $f$ is proper$_{<\infty}$: there is an open $U'' \supseteq |S_f|$ with every $\mathrm{cl}(U'' \cap \{|N| \circ |f| \le C\})$ compact. *Step 2.* Put $U' = U \cap U''$: it is open, contains $|S_f|$, and $U' \cap \{\ldots \le C\} \subseteq U'' \cap \{\ldots \le C\}$, so its closure is a closed subset of a compact set, hence compact.

**Proof of the observation.** "⇒": given $U$ that controls, the sharper form gives $U' \subseteq U$ that strongly controls; only that $U$ is an open neighbourhood of $|S_f|$ is used. "⇐": strongly controlling implies controlling, with the same set.

**Statement of Definition 18.2.4** (same). With reflexive $(0, 1)$-fibres (Definition 18.1.9, read in the models, §XVIII.10) ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNorms · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.HasReflexiveFibers⟧, $f$ has the reflexive local compactness property if for every reflexive auxiliary norm $N$ ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNorms · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsReflexiveAuxNormFunctor⟧ and every $z \in |C|$ there is an open $U(z) \ni z$ with $\mathrm{cl}_{|C|}(U(z) \cap \{|N| \circ |f| \le 1\})$ compact ⟦Chapter18/Compactness · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.HasReflexiveLocalCompactness⟧. The text prints $N : C \to [0, \infty]$; we read $N : E \to [0, \infty]$, as in Definition 18.1.7. The standing assumptions, reflexive fibres and sc-Fredholm $f$, are hypotheses of the statements that use the property.

**A criterion: locally compact uniformizer domains** ⟦Chapter18/CompactnessExamples · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.hasReflexiveLocalCompactness_of_locallyCompact⟧. If the domains $O_\lambda$ of a covering family are locally compact, every section functor has the reflexive local compactness property, for every $N$. *Proof.* Given $z$, write $z = |\Gamma|(\pi(\lambda, y))$ (surjectivity of $|\Gamma|$ and $\pi$). Choose a compact neighbourhood $K_0$ of $y$ in $O_\lambda$ and let $K$ be its copy in the $\lambda$-th summand of $X_\Psi$. Then $U(z) = |\Gamma|(\pi(\mathrm{int}\,K))$ is open (`isOpen_modelImage`), contains $z$, and lies in the compact set $|\Gamma|(\pi K)$; the closure of $U(z) \cap \{\ldots\}$ is a closed subset of that compact set.

**Statement of Remark 18.2.5** (explicit). Let $P$ have reflexive $(0, 1)$-fibres, $f$ be sc-Fredholm, $N$ a reflexive auxiliary norm, and $\bar F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$. If $z = |\alpha|$ lies in $|C|_0 \setminus |C|_1$, read as: $\alpha$ has no regularity $1$, then $N(f(\alpha)) = +\infty$, and there is an open $U \ni z$ with $U \cap \{|N| \circ |f| \le 1\} = \emptyset$, whose closure is therefore compact ⟦Chapter18/Compactness · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.remark_18_2_5⟧. The text says the argument uses the reflexive local compactness property; the argument it prints uses the lower semicontinuity of $|N| \circ |f|$, which comes from the reflexivity of $N$, and the regularizing property, and the formal statement has no local compactness hypothesis.

**Proof.** Work in the covering model $c$ of `refCoveringFamily`; the model section is sc-Fredholm (`modelSection_isScFredholm`).

*Step 1, a representative in the model.* By essential surjectivity, $\Gamma(x) \cong \alpha$ for some $x$ ⟦Chapter18/Compactness · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.exists_modelObj_iso⟧. The regularity of $\Gamma(x)$ is that of $x$, and regularity is invariant under isomorphism, so $x$ is not on level $1$.

*Step 2, $f^{\bar\Psi}(x) \notin W_{0,1}$.* If it were, then $f^{\bar\Psi}(x) \in W_{0,1} \subseteq W_{0,0}$ is a vector on level $(0, 1)$ over a point on level $0$, and the regularizing property of the sc-Fredholm model section (Definition 3.1.16 (1)) would put $x$ on level $1$.

*Step 3, $N(f(\alpha)) = \infty$.* $N \circ f$ is constant on isomorphism classes ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNorms · SectionFunctor.comp_eq_of_isomorphic⟧, so $N(f(\alpha)) = N(f(\Gamma x)) = N(\bar\Gamma(f^{\bar\Psi}(x)))$ (`bundlePull_modelSection`), and this is $\infty$ since $f^{\bar\Psi}(x) \notin W_{0,1}$ and $N$ is an auxiliary norm in the model.

*Step 4, the set $A$ is closed.* Let $A = \{y \mid N(f^{\bar\Psi}(y)) \le 1\}$ in $X_\Psi$. For a reflexive auxiliary norm with reflexive fibres and a continuous section, $A$ is closed: this is the lower semicontinuity of Part I ⟦Chapter5/ReflexiveCompactness · StrongBundle.isClosed_setOf_auxLe⟧, applied to the model section, which is continuous since it is sc-smooth. By Step 2, $x \notin A$.

*Step 5, the neighbourhood.* $U_0 = |\Gamma|(\pi(A^c))$ is open by `isOpen_modelImage` and contains $z$. By `image_auxLe_eq` applied to $A^c$, $U_0 \cap \{|N| \circ |f| \le 1\}$ is the image of $\{y \in A^c \mid N(f^{\bar\Psi}(y)) \le 1\}$, which is empty. The empty set has empty, compact closure.

*No witness off level $1$.* Every example of Part IV is finite-dimensional, so every object is smooth and the hypothesis $z \in |C|_0 \setminus |C|_1$ is never met; the remark has the text's hypothesis and no witness of it.

**Witnesses.** On the reflection bundle an auxiliary norm exists and for each one some $U$ strongly controls compactness ⟦Chapter18/CompactnessExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflDiag_stronglyControlsCompactness⟧ (from proper$_{<\infty}$), and so on the half-space bundle ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceWitnesses · HalfSpaceReflection.halfF_stronglyControlsCompactness⟧. A concrete controlling pair with boundary: for $N(\alpha, w) = |w|$, an auxiliary norm of the half-space bundle (§XVIII.20), $(N, |C|)$ controls compactness ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceBoundaryData · HalfSpaceReflection.halfF_controlsCompactness_halfNorm⟧, since $|a^2 + y^2 - 1| \le 1$ forces $|(a, y)| \le 2$ ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceBoundaryData · HalfSpaceReflection.norm_le_two_of_norm_halfDiscMap_le_one⟧ and $|\{|(a, y)| \le 2\}|$ is compact ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceWitnesses · HalfSpaceReflection.isCompact_closedBallH⟧. Both bundles have reflexive fibres, the fibre $\mathbb R$ being finite-dimensional ⟦Chapter18/CompactnessExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflQ_hasReflexiveFibers⟧ ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceWitnesses · HalfSpaceReflection.halfQ_hasReflexiveFibers⟧, reflexive auxiliary norms ⟦Chapter18/CompactnessExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflQ_exists_isReflexiveAuxNormFunctor⟧, and the reflexive local compactness property by the criterion, $\mathbb R$ and $H$ being locally compact ⟦Chapter18/CompactnessExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflDiag_hasReflexiveLocalCompactness⟧ ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceWitnesses · HalfSpaceReflection.halfF_hasReflexiveLocalCompactness⟧.

### XVIII.14 Theorem 18.2.6 and Remark 18.2.7 (2017: Theorem 18.4, Remark 18.4), p. 851

**Boundaries in the model** ⟦Chapter18/Compactness · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.mem_boundary_model_iff⟧. An object $x$ of $X_\Psi$ lies in $\partial X_\Psi$ iff $|\Gamma(x)| \in |\partial C|$, where $\partial C$ is the set of objects of degeneracy index $\ge 1$ (Definition 17.1.30). *Proof.* The degeneracy index of $\Gamma(x)$ is that of $x$ (Part XVII), and $\partial C$ is saturated, so $|\Gamma(x)| \in \pi(\partial C)$ iff $\Gamma(x) \in \partial C$ iff $d(x) \ge 1$.

**Statement of Theorem 18.2.6** (explicit). Let $P : E \to C$ be a strong polyfold bundle over a tame $C$ with reflexive $(0, 1)$-fibres and $\bar F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$, $N$ a reflexive auxiliary norm, and $f$ an sc-Fredholm section functor with compact moduli space and the reflexive local compactness property. Let $U_\partial = W' \cap |\partial C|$ with $W'$ open in $|C|$, containing $|S_f \cap \partial C|$, with $\mathrm{cl}_{|C|}(U_\partial \cap \{|N| \circ |f| \le 1\})$ compact. Then there is $U$ with $U \cap |\partial C| = U_\partial$ such that $(N, U)$ controls compactness ⟦Chapter18/Compactness · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.theorem_18_2_6⟧. *Readings.* The text prints $N : C \to [0, \infty]$, read as $E$. $\partial f = f|\partial C$ and $S_{\partial f}$ are read as $S_f \cap \partial C$, and "$U_\partial$ open in $|\partial C|$" as the trace of an open set of $|C|$, as in Theorem 12.4.6. Tameness of $C$ is a hypothesis as in the text and is not used, as in Theorem 12.4.6. The hypotheses taken: $\bar F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ (standing), reflexive fibres and reflexive local compactness (the text's).

**Proof.** The theorem is Theorem 12.4.6 (Part XII) in the covering model $c$ of `refCoveringFamily`, in its form for a continuous section ⟦Chapter12/ReflexiveCompactnessEp · EpStrongBundle.theorem_12_4_6_of_continuous⟧ (the form of ⟦Chapter12/ReflexiveCompactnessEp · EpStrongBundle.theorem_12_4_6⟧ without tameness and paracompactness). Write $h = |\Gamma| : |X_\Psi| \to |C|$, a homeomorphism.

*Step 1, $U_\partial$ in the model.* Let $W_X = \pi^{-1}(h^{-1}(W'))$, open and saturated in $X_\Psi$, and $U_{\partial, X} = W_X \cap \partial X_\Psi$, saturated because $\partial X_\Psi$ is. By `mem_boundary_model_iff`, $h(\pi(U_{\partial, X})) = W' \cap |\partial C| = U_\partial$.

*Step 2, the hypotheses of Theorem 12.4.6 in the model.* The model section is sc-Fredholm, hence continuous and a functor. Its solution set has compact orbit space by `isCompact_zeroSet_iff`. Reflexive fibres in the model and the reflexivity of $N \circ \bar\Gamma$ are the hypotheses read in $c$. $S_{f^{\bar\Psi}} \cap \partial X_\Psi \subseteq U_{\partial, X}$: for such $x$, $|\Gamma(x)|$ is the class of a boundary object $\beta \cong \Gamma(x)$, $\beta \in S_f$ by saturation, so $|\Gamma(x)| \in |S_f \cap \partial C| \subseteq U_\partial \subseteq W'$. The closure of $\pi\{y \in U_{\partial, X} \mid N(f^{\bar\Psi}(y)) \le 1\}$ is compact, by `isCompact_closure_modelImage_iff` and Step 1.

*Step 3, local compactness in the model.* For each object $x$ of $X_\Psi$, the property of Definition 18.2.4 at $h(\pi x)$ gives an open $U' \ni h(\pi x)$ with compact closed sublevel set in $|C|$; its preimage $U_2 = \pi^{-1}(h^{-1}(U'))$ satisfies $h(\pi(U_2)) = U'$, so the closure of $\pi\{y \in U_2 \mid N(f^{\bar\Psi}(y)) \le 1\}$ is compact by `isCompact_closure_modelImage_iff`. Theorem 12.4.6 wants the closure in the object space $X_\Psi$, not in the orbit space. For this, the properness of the ep-groupoid gives an open $V_0 \ni x$ on which $\pi$ restricts to a proper map, and then a set inside $V_0$ whose image under $\pi$ has compact closure has compact closure itself ⟦Chapter12/ReflexiveCompactnessEp · EpGroupoid.isCompact_closure_of_isProperMap⟧. So $U_2 \cap V_0$ is the neighbourhood that Theorem 12.4.6 needs.

*Step 4, Theorem 12.4.6.* It gives an open saturated $U$ in $X_\Psi$ with $S_{f^{\bar\Psi}} \subseteq U$, $U \cap \partial X_\Psi = U_{\partial, X}$, and compact closure of $\pi\{y \in U \mid N(f^{\bar\Psi}(y)) \le 1\}$.

*Step 5, back to $C$.* The required set is $h(\pi(U))$. It is open (`isOpen_modelImage`), contains $|S_f|$ (`image_solutionSet_modelSection`), controls compactness by `isCompact_closure_modelImage_iff`, and $h(\pi U) \cap |\partial C| = h(\pi(U \cap \partial X_\Psi)) = h(\pi U_{\partial, X}) = U_\partial$, by `mem_boundary_model_iff` in both directions and Step 1.

**Witnesses.** On the reflection bundle $\partial C = \emptyset$ ⟦Chapter18/CompactnessExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflection_boundarySet⟧, and all hypotheses hold with $U_\partial = \emptyset$; the theorem gives a controlling $U$ disjoint from $|\partial C|$ ⟦Chapter18/CompactnessExamples · ReflectionBundle.theorem_18_2_6_witness⟧ (weak: $\partial C = \emptyset$). On the half-space bundle, with boundary and the corner $(0, 0)$ of isotropy $\mathbb Z/2$, take $W' = |\{|(a, y)| < 2\}|$ and $U_\partial = W' \cap |\partial C|$ ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceWitnesses · HalfSpaceReflection.halfUb⟧. The zeros of $f$ have norm $\le 1$, so $|S_f \cap \partial C| \subseteq U_\partial$ ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceWitnesses · HalfSpaceReflection.zeroSet_boundary_subset_halfUb⟧, $U_\partial$ contains $|(0, 1)|$ ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceWitnesses · HalfSpaceReflection.topPoint_mem_halfUb⟧, and the closure of its sublevel set lies in the compact $|\{|(a, y)| \le 2\}|$. With a reflexive auxiliary norm all hypotheses hold, and the theorem gives $U$ with $U \cap |\partial C| = U_\partial \ne \emptyset$ ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceWitnesses · HalfSpaceReflection.theorem_18_2_6_witness_boundary⟧.

**Remark 18.2.7** (nothing to formalize). The remark comments that an auxiliary norm given only over the boundary can first be extended and then Theorem 18.2.6 applied. Its two steps are Theorem 18.1.12 (3), the extension of an auxiliary norm from $\partial C$ (§XVIII.11) ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirect · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.theorem_18_1_12_three⟧, and Theorem 18.2.6 itself; the remark makes no further claim.

### XVIII.15 Definition 18.3.1, Remark 18.3.2 and the pointwise norm (2017: Definition 18.11, Remark 18.5), p. 852

**Functors on $E$.** A "functor $\Lambda : E \to \mathbb Q^+$" is read, as all functors from $E$ to a set in Part IV, as a function on the objects of $E$ that is constant along morphisms ⟦Chapter18/ModelTransfer · IsMorInvariant⟧, the target being a discrete category. In terms of the fibres: $\Lambda$ is a functor iff $\Lambda(t\varphi, \mu(\varphi)v) = \Lambda(s\varphi, v)$ for every morphism $\varphi$ of $C$ and $v \in \mu(s\varphi)$ ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionFunctors · isMorInvariant_iff⟧ ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionFunctors · IsMorInvariant.apply_map⟧, because a morphism of $E$ is a pair $(\varphi, e)$ with $e$ over $s\varphi$. Its pull-back to a model is $\Gamma^*\Lambda = \Lambda \circ \bar\Gamma$ ⟦Chapter18/ModelTransfer · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.bundlePull⟧, a functor on $W_{\bar\Psi}$ in the sense of Chapter 11 ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isWFunctor_bundlePull⟧, since $\bar\Gamma$ is a functor.

**Statement of Definition 18.3.1** (same). $\Lambda$ is an sc⁺-multisection functor if it is a functor and, for some covering family $\bar\Psi$, $\Lambda \circ \bar\Gamma$ is an sc⁺-multisection functor of the strong bundle $W_{\bar\Psi} \to X_\Psi$ in the sense of Definition 13.2.1 ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsScPlusMultisectionIn⟧ ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsScPlusMultisectionFunctor⟧. "For an sc-smooth model" is read as "for some covering family"; Remark 18.3.2 gives every one.

**The pull-backs to two models correspond** ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.bundlePull_eq_pullMult⟧. For a functor $\Lambda$ and two covering families $c$, $c'$, $\Gamma_c^*\Lambda$ is the pull-back of $\Gamma_{c'}^*\Lambda$ along the comparison generalized strong bundle isomorphism $\bar f_{c, c'}$ of Theorem 17.1.18 ⟦Chapter17/BundleModelComparison · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isGeneralizedIso_bundleComparison⟧. *Proof.* *Step 1.* For $w$ in $W_c$ there is $w'$ in $W_{c'}$ with $\bar\Gamma_{c'}(w') \cong \bar\Gamma_c(w)$ ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNorms · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.exists_bundleModelObj_iso⟧. *Step 2.* The orbit map of $\bar f_{c, c'}$ sends $|w|$ to $|w'|$ ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.orbitMap_bundleComparison_eq⟧: the orbit map of the comparison satisfies $|\bar\Gamma_{c'}| \circ |\bar f_{c,c'}| = |\bar\Gamma_c|$ ⟦Chapter17/BundleModelComparison · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.orbitMap_bundleComparison⟧, and $|\bar\Gamma_{c'}|$ is injective. *Step 3.* The pull-back of a functor along a diagram of strong bundles with an equivalence as left leg is computed at any $w'$ in the image orbit ⟦Chapter11/StrongBundleUpToEquivalence · SDiagram.pullMult_eq⟧, applied to a representative diagram of $\bar f_{c,c'}$, so the pull-back at $w$ is $\Lambda(\bar\Gamma_{c'}(w'))$, which is $\Lambda(\bar\Gamma_c(w))$ because $\Lambda$ is constant on isomorphism classes.

**Statement of Remark 18.3.2** (same). For every covering family $c$: $\Lambda$ is an sc⁺-multisection functor iff it is a functor and $\Gamma_c^*\Lambda$ is an sc⁺-multisection functor in the model of $c$ ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.remark_18_3_2⟧. *Proof.* "⇐" is the definition. "⇒": if $\Gamma_{c'}^*\Lambda$ is an sc⁺-multisection functor, so is $\Gamma_c^*\Lambda$ ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsScPlusMultisectionIn.of_comparison⟧: by `bundlePull_eq_pullMult` it is the pull-back along the generalized isomorphism $\bar f_{c, c'}$, and Proposition 11.5.3 (Part XI) shows that such pull-backs preserve sc⁺-multisection functors ⟦Chapter11/MultisectionLocalStructure · GenSBMap.proposition_11_5_3_book⟧.

**Point G.247.** The introduction of §18.3 invites the reader to transport the remaining concepts of Chapter 13 in the same way; it is an invitation, nothing to formalize (point G.247). The notions the chapter uses are those of §§XVIII.15–XVIII.17.

**Statement of the pointwise norm** (same). For $N : E \to [0, \infty]$ and $\Lambda : E \to \mathbb Q^+$, $N(\Lambda)(\alpha) = \max\{N(\alpha, e) \mid \Lambda(\alpha, e) > 0\}$ is defined as the supremum in $[0, \infty]$ ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionFunctors · pointwiseNormFunctor⟧. It is a functor $C \to [0, \infty]$ when $N$ and $\Lambda$ are ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionFunctors · pointwiseNormFunctor_tgt⟧ ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionFunctors · pointwiseNormFunctor_eq_of_isomorphic⟧: the linear isomorphism $\mu(\varphi)$ carries the set $\{v \mid \Lambda(s\varphi, v) > 0\}$ onto $\{v \mid \Lambda(t\varphi, v) > 0\}$ and preserves $N$. For an auxiliary norm $N$ and an sc⁺-multisection functor $\Lambda$ the supremum is a maximum: finite and attained, with $N(\alpha, e) \le N(\Lambda)(\alpha)$ for every $e$ with $\Lambda(\alpha, e) > 0$ ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.pointwiseNormFunctor_isGreatest⟧.

**Proof.** *Step 1, the model's pointwise norm.* At $\Gamma(x)$, $N(\Lambda)(\Gamma x)$ is finite and its real value is the pointwise norm of Definition 13.2.3 of $\Gamma^*\Lambda$ at $x$ for the model norm ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.pointwiseNorm_bundlePull⟧. The fibre map $\bar\Gamma_x$ carries the support $\{w \in W_x \mid \Gamma^*\Lambda(w) > 0\}$ onto $\{v \mid \Lambda(\Gamma x, v) > 0\}$ ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.image_suppAt_bundlePull⟧, so the two suprema are over the same values. The support of an sc⁺-multisection functor at a point is finite and lies in $W_{0,1}$, where $N \circ \bar\Gamma$ is finite; a finite set of finite values has a finite supremum, and on $W_{0,1}$ the value $N \circ \bar\Gamma$ is the model norm. *Step 2, every object.* For an arbitrary $\alpha$, choose $x$ with $\Gamma(x) \cong \alpha$; $N(\Lambda)$ is constant on isomorphism classes. *Step 3, attained.* At $\Gamma(x)$ the model maximum of Part XIII ⟦Chapter13/MultisectionSupport · IsScPlusMultisection.isGreatest_pointwiseNorm⟧ gives $w$ in the support with model norm equal to the pointwise norm; its image $v = \bar\Gamma_x(w)$ has $\Lambda(\Gamma x, v) > 0$ and $N(\Gamma x, v) = N(\Lambda)(\Gamma x)$, comparing real values of finite elements of $[0, \infty]$. The property "attained" moves along the isomorphism $\Gamma(x) \to \alpha$ by $\mu$. The inequality $N(\alpha, e) \le N(\Lambda)(\alpha)$ is that of a supremum.

### XVIII.16 Definition 18.3.3, Remark 18.3.4, sums and products, Theorem 18.3.5 (2017: Definition 18.12, Theorem 18.5), pp. 852–853

**Statement of Definition 18.3.3** (same). An sc⁺-multisection functor $\Lambda$ is structurable if, for some covering family, $\Gamma^*\Lambda$ is structurable in the sense of Definition 13.3.7 ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsStructurableIn⟧ ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsStructurableFunctor⟧. Structurable functors are sc⁺-multisection functors ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsStructurableFunctor.isScPlusMultisectionFunctor⟧, since a structurable multisection is one.

**Statement of Remark 18.3.4** (same; new in the 2021 edition, the 2017 text has a sentence after Definition 18.12). For every covering family $c$, $\Lambda$ is structurable iff it is a functor and $\Gamma_c^*\Lambda$ is structurable ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.remark_18_3_4⟧. *Proof.* As for Remark 18.3.2, with structurability in place of sc⁺: $\Gamma_c^*\Lambda$ is the pull-back of $\Gamma_{c'}^*\Lambda$ along $\bar f_{c,c'}$ (`bundlePull_eq_pullMult`), and pull-backs along generalized strong bundle isomorphisms preserve structurability, Theorems 13.4.1 and 13.4.3 (Part XIII) ⟦Chapter13/MultisectionPushforward · GenSBMap.isStructurable_pullMult⟧; this is ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsStructurableIn.of_comparison⟧.

**The sum and the product** (same). $(\Lambda_1 \oplus \Lambda_2)(\alpha, e) = \sum_{e' + e'' = e} \Lambda_1(\alpha, e')\Lambda_2(\alpha, e'')$, a finite sum for sc⁺-multisection functors and, formally, a sum over pairs of the fibre with finite support ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionFunctors · multisectionFunctorAdd⟧. For $\beta : C \to \mathbb R$, $(\beta \odot \Lambda)(\alpha, e) = \Lambda(\alpha, \beta(\alpha)^{-1}e)$ if $\beta(\alpha) \ne 0$, and $1_{e = 0}$ if $\beta(\alpha) = 0$ ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionFunctors · multisectionFunctorSmul⟧.
