Both are functors: for $\oplus$, reindex the sum by the bijection $\mu(\varphi) \times \mu(\varphi)$, which preserves $e' + e'' = e$ by linearity ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionFunctors · isMorInvariant_multisectionFunctorAdd⟧; for $\odot$, use $\beta(t\varphi) = \beta(s\varphi)$, $\mu(\varphi)(\beta^{-1}v) = \beta^{-1}\mu(\varphi)v$ and $\mu(\varphi)v = 0 \iff v = 0$ ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionFunctors · isMorInvariant_multisectionFunctorSmul⟧.

*Pull-back commutes with them.* $\Gamma^*(\Lambda_1 \oplus \Lambda_2) = \Gamma^*\Lambda_1 \oplus \Gamma^*\Lambda_2$, the sum of Definition 13.2.9 in the model ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.bundlePull_multisectionFunctorAdd⟧, and $\Gamma^*(\beta \odot \Lambda) = (\beta \circ \Gamma) \odot \Gamma^*\Lambda$, the product of Lemma 13.2.11 ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.bundlePull_multisectionFunctorSmul⟧. *Proof.* $\bar\Gamma(w) = (\Gamma(x), \bar\Gamma_x(w))$ for $w \in W_x$ ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.bundleModelObj_eq_objMk⟧, so $\Lambda \circ \bar\Gamma$ on the fibre $W_x$ is $\Lambda(\Gamma x, \bar\Gamma_x(\cdot))$ ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.bundlePull_fiber⟧. The sum over pairs in $\mu(\Gamma x)$ is reindexed by $\bar\Gamma_x \times \bar\Gamma_x$, and $\bar\Gamma_x$ is linear and injective; for the product, $\bar\Gamma_x$ commutes with scalars and has trivial kernel, and the case distinction on $\beta(\Gamma x) = 0$ is the same on both sides.

**Sc-smooth functions.** "$\beta : C \to \mathbb R$ an sc-smooth functor" is read as: constant along the morphisms of $C$, and $\beta \circ \Psi : O \to \mathbb R$ sc-smooth for every uniformizer $\Psi$ ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionFunctors · PolyfoldConstruction.IsScSmoothFunctionFunctor⟧. Then $\beta \circ \Gamma$ is constant along the morphisms of every model ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionFunctors · PolyfoldConstruction.IsScSmoothFunctionFunctor.mor_model⟧, since $\Gamma$ is a functor, and sc-smooth on every model, piece by piece of the disjoint union ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionFunctors · PolyfoldConstruction.IsScSmoothFunctionFunctor.isScSmoothMap_model⟧. "$\beta\Lambda_1$" in the text is read as $\beta \odot \Lambda_1$.

*Sums and products of sc⁺-multisection functors* ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isScPlusMultisectionFunctor_add_smul⟧. If $\Lambda_1$, $\Lambda_2$ are sc⁺-multisection functors and $\beta$ is an sc-smooth functor, $\Lambda_1 \oplus \Lambda_2$ and $\beta \odot \Lambda_1$ are sc⁺-multisection functors. *Proof.* Take the model $c$ of $\Lambda_1$; Remark 18.3.2 puts $\Lambda_2$ in the same model. After the pull-back identities, these are Definition 13.2.9 ⟦Chapter13/MultisectionAlgebra · definition_13_2_9⟧ and Lemma 13.2.11 ⟦Chapter13/MultisectionAlgebra · lemma_13_2_11⟧ in the model.

**Statement of Theorem 18.3.5** (same). For structurable $\Lambda_1$, $\Lambda_2$ and an sc-smooth functor $\beta : C \to \mathbb R$, $\Lambda_1 \oplus \Lambda_2$ and $\beta \odot \Lambda_1$ are structurable ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.theorem_18_3_5⟧.

**Proof.** *Step 1.* Both are functors (above). *Step 2.* Take the model $c$ in which $\Gamma^*\Lambda_1$ is structurable; by Remark 18.3.4, $\Gamma_c^*\Lambda_2$ is structurable in the same model. *Step 3.* By the pull-back identities, $\Gamma_c^*(\Lambda_1 \oplus \Lambda_2) = \Gamma_c^*\Lambda_1 \oplus \Gamma_c^*\Lambda_2$ is structurable by Proposition 13.3.12 ⟦Chapter13/StructurableMultisection · proposition_13_3_12⟧, and $\Gamma_c^*(\beta \odot \Lambda_1) = (\beta \circ \Gamma) \odot \Gamma_c^*\Lambda_1$ by Lemma 13.3.14 for functions ⟦Chapter13/StructurableMultisection · lemma_13_3_14_fun⟧, whose hypotheses are the two properties of $\beta \circ \Gamma$ above.

**Witness.** On the reflection bundle, with the structurable $\Lambda$ of Proposition 18.4.3 at the vector $(0, 1)$ over the isotropy point (§XVIII.19) and $\beta = 2$, $\Lambda \oplus \Lambda$ and $2 \odot \Lambda$ are structurable ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionFunctorsExamples · ReflectionBundle.theorem_18_3_5_witness⟧; $\Lambda(0, 1) > 0$, so $\Lambda$ is not the zero multisection.

### XVIII.17 Theorem 18.3.6 (2017: Theorem 18.6), p. 853: pull-back by a proper covering functor

**The covering functor, read through models.** The text describes an sc-smooth strong bundle proper covering functor between strong polyfold bundles in §17.8 (p. 842) through sc-smooth models, a proper strong bundle covering functor $A : \tilde W \to W$ of Definition 8.5.9, and leaves the details to the reader. We read it as the following data ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.StrongBundleProperCovering⟧: a functor $\tilde P : E' \to E$ covering a functor $P : C' \to C$ of the bases; covering families $c'$ of $E'$ and $c$ of $E$, with their models; a functor $F : X'_{\Psi'} \to X_\Psi$ and a map $A : W'_{\bar\Psi'} \to W_{\bar\Psi}$ that is a proper strong bundle covering of Definition 8.5.9 in the 2021 form ⟦Chapter8/Book · IsProperStrongBundleCovering2021⟧; and the square $\bar\Gamma \circ A \cong \tilde P \circ \bar\Gamma'$, commuting up to isomorphism in $E$, the square of (17.106).

**Statement of Theorem 18.3.6** (restated). For such a $\tilde P$ and a structurable $\Lambda : E \to \mathbb Q^+$, $\tilde P^*\Lambda = \Lambda \circ \tilde P$ is structurable ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.theorem_18_3_6⟧.

**Proof.** *Step 1, a functor.* For a morphism $a$ of $E'$, $\tilde P$ sends source and target of $a$ to source and target of $\tilde P(a)$, and $\Lambda$ is constant along $\tilde P(a)$. *Step 2, the pull-back to $c'$.* $\Gamma_{c'}^*(\Lambda \circ \tilde P) = \Gamma_c^*\Lambda \circ A$: at $w$, $\Lambda(\tilde P(\bar\Gamma'(w))) = \Lambda(\bar\Gamma(A(w)))$ by the commuting square and the invariance of $\Lambda$ under isomorphism. *Step 3.* $\Gamma_c^*\Lambda$ is structurable in the model $c$ by Remark 18.3.4, and the pull-back of a structurable multisection along a proper strong bundle covering is structurable by Theorem 13.4.4 (Part XIII) ⟦Chapter13/MultisectionPullback · theorem_13_4_4⟧.

**Witness.** The identity covering of the reflection bundle, with the same model on both sides and $A = \mathrm{id}$ ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionFunctorsExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflIdCovering⟧, a proper strong bundle covering by ⟦Chapter8/Book · EpStrongBundle.isProperStrongBundleCovering2021_id⟧; the pull-back of the non-zero structurable $\Lambda$ of §XVIII.19 is structurable ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionFunctorsExamples · ReflectionBundle.theorem_18_3_6_witness⟧. The witness is weak: the covering is the identity.

### XVIII.18 Definition 18.4.1 and Remark 18.4.2 (2017: Definition 18.13, Remark 18.6), pp. 853–854

**Statement of Definition 18.4.1** (same). $C$ admits sc-smooth partitions of unity if the model $X_\Psi$ of some covering family admits sc-smooth partitions of unity in the ep-groupoid sense of Definition 7.5.5 ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionConstructions · PolyfoldConstruction.AdmitsModelPartitionsOfUnity⟧, and sc-smooth bump functions if some model admits them in the pointwise sense of Definition 12.3.1 ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionConstructions · PolyfoldConstruction.AdmitsModelBumpFunctions⟧. The pointwise ep-groupoid sense is Part I's Definition 5.3.1 for the object M-polyfold, by Theorem 12.3.2 (1) ⟦Chapter12/BumpEp · EpGroupoid.theorem_12_3_2_1⟧.

**Statement of Remark 18.4.2** (same). For every covering family $c$: $C$ admits sc-smooth partitions of unity iff $X_c$ does; $C$ admits sc-smooth bump functions iff $X_c$ does; and if the object M-polyfold of $X_c$ admits sc-smooth partitions of unity, then $X_c$ admits them in the ep-groupoid sense ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionConstructions · PolyfoldConstruction.remark_18_4_2⟧. The first sentence of the remark, invariance under generalized isomorphisms of ep-groupoids, is ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionConstructions · PolyfoldConstruction.remark_18_4_2_generalizedIso⟧.

**Proof.** *Step 1, two models, partitions of unity* ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionConstructions · PolyfoldConstruction.hasScPartitionsOfUnity_model_iff⟧. The comparison $f_{\Psi, \Psi'}$ between the models of two covering families is a generalized isomorphism (Theorem 17.1.18) ⟦Chapter17/ModelComparison · PolyfoldConstruction.isGeneralizedIso_comparison⟧. A property of ep-groupoids that is invariant under equivalences in both directions is invariant under generalized isomorphisms, since these are zig-zags of equivalences ⟦Chapter10/LocalizationProperties · EPLoc.iff_of_isGeneralizedIso⟧; and partitions of unity are invariant under equivalences, the descent written out in Part XVI for Proposition 16.1.4 (2) ⟦Chapter16/PolyfoldPartitions · EpFunctor.IsEquivalence.hasScPartitionsOfUnity_iff⟧.

*Step 2, two models, bump functions* ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionConstructions · PolyfoldConstruction.admitsBumpFunctionsPointwise_model_iff⟧. By Theorem 12.3.2 (1) the pointwise notion is bump functions on the object M-polyfold, and that is invariant under equivalences (Proposition 16.1.4 (1), Part XVI); then as in Step 1.

*Step 3, the M-polyfold criterion.* The orbit space of a model is paracompact ⟦Chapter17/ModelEquivalence · PolyfoldConstruction.hasParacompactOrbitSpace_model⟧, metrizable as $|C|$ is, and Theorem 7.5.7 (§7.6.2, Part VII) turns M-polyfold partitions of unity on the objects into ep-groupoid partitions of unity ⟦Chapter7/PartitionsOfUnity · EpGroupoid.theorem_7_5_7⟧.

*Step 4, the remark.* "Some model" iff "the model $c$" is Steps 1 and 2; the third clause is Step 3. The generalized-isomorphism form is Steps 1 and 2 for an arbitrary generalized isomorphism.

**The M-polyfold notion and Theorem 14.1.9.** Theorem 18.4.4 and Theorem 15.3.9 in the polyfold context need partitions of unity in the sense in which Theorem 14.1.9 uses them: on the object M-polyfold of a model ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationModelIndependence · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.HasModelMPolyfoldPartitionsOfUnity⟧. By Step 3 this implies Definition 18.4.1. Part III compares partitions of unity between two models only in the ep-groupoid sense, so the M-polyfold notion is taken "in some model" and is not compared between models (§XVIII.20).

**Witnesses.** The model $\mathbb R \sqcup \mathbb R$ of the reflection polyfold admits M-polyfold partitions of unity, its charts being those of $\mathbb R$, a Hilbert space ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionFunctorsExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflModel_admitsScPartitionsOfUnity⟧, so the reflection polyfold satisfies both parts of Definition 18.4.1 ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionFunctorsExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflection_admitsModelPartitionsOfUnity⟧ ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionFunctorsExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflection_admitsModelBumpFunctions⟧. The model of the half-space polyfold, one copy of $H$, admits them by Theorem 5.5.10, its chart having the finite-dimensional model $\mathbb R \oplus \mathbb R$ ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceWitnesses · HalfSpaceReflection.halfModel_admitsScPartitionsOfUnity⟧, and so it admits bump functions ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceWitnesses · HalfSpaceReflection.halfS_admitsModelBumpFunctions⟧; this witness has boundary.

### XVIII.19 The construction on p. 854 and Proposition 18.4.3 (2017: Proposition 18.1), pp. 854–855

**The construction, read in a model.** The text constructs, at a smooth object $\alpha$ and a smooth vector $(\alpha, e)$, a multisection from one strong bundle uniformizer $\bar\Psi \in \bar F(\alpha)$ with $G = G_\alpha$: a bump section $s_1$ through the preimage of $e$, its translates $s_g$, and $\Lambda(\bar\Psi(h)) = |G|^{-1}\#\{g \mid s_g(p(h)) = h\}$, extended along morphisms and by $1_{e = 0}$ elsewhere. The formalization carries the construction out in the model $W_{\bar\Psi} \to X_\Psi$ of a covering family, the "suitable sc-smooth model" of Proposition 18.4.3: the rôle of $G \ltimes O$ is played by a natural representation of $G_x$ at a point $x$ of the model over $\alpha$ (Theorem 7.1.13), and the multisection is pushed forward to $E$ along $\bar\Gamma$. "$\alpha$ smooth" and "$(\alpha, e)$ a smooth vector" are read in the model: $x$ in the smooth part of $X_\Psi$, and a smooth $w \in W_x$ with $\bar\Gamma(w) \cong (\alpha, e)$.

**Pushing a functor forward.** For a covering family, a function $\Lambda'$ on $W_{\bar\Psi}$ that is constant on isomorphism classes is the pull-back of a unique functor on $E$, $\Lambda = \bar\Gamma_*\Lambda'$ ⟦Chapter18/ModelTransfer · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.bundlePush⟧, defined through the bijection $|W_{\bar\Psi}| \cong |E|$ ⟦Chapter18/ModelTransfer · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.bundleOrbitEquiv⟧; it is a functor ⟦Chapter18/ModelTransfer · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isMorInvariant_bundlePush⟧ and $\Gamma^*\Gamma_*\Lambda' = \Lambda'$ ⟦Chapter18/ModelTransfer · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.bundlePull_bundlePush⟧. A functor on $W_{\bar\Psi}$ is constant on isomorphism classes ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionConstructions · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.eq_of_isomorphic_of_isWFunctor⟧.

**The atomic data** ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionConstructions · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.exists_atomicData⟧. If $C$ admits sc-smooth bump functions, then for a smooth point $x = p(w)$ of the model and a smooth $w \in W_x$ there are atomic data of Definition 13.5.1 (1)–(2) ⟦Chapter13/AtomicMultisection · AtomicData⟧ at $x$ with a non-empty index set and a section through $w$. *Proof.* *Step 1.* By Remark 18.4.2, the model of $c$ (as a base covering family ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionConstructions · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.baseCoveringFamily⟧) admits bump functions in the pointwise sense. *Step 2.* $x$ has a neighbourhood with the properness property ⟦Chapter7/EpGroupoid · EpGroupoid.exists_hasPropernessProperty⟧, and inside it a natural representation of $G_x$ on $U(x)$ with an open invariant $V(x) \ni x$, $\mathrm{cl}\,V(x) \subseteq U(x)$ ⟦Chapter7/PartitionsOfUnity · EpGroupoid.exists_invariant_nhds_of_isOpen⟧; the properness property passes to the smaller $U(x)$. *Step 3.* Theorem 13.5.4 (1) (Part XIII) ⟦Chapter13/AtomicConstruction · theorem_13_5_4_1⟧ gives a symmetric family of sc⁺-sections on $U(x)$, indexed by $G_x$, vanishing off $V(x)$, with $s_{i_0}(x) = w$. Its seed is the text's bump section: Part I's Lemma 5.3.3 ⟦Chapter5/BumpFunctions · lemma_5_3_3⟧ with an auxiliary norm of Proposition 5.1.3 ⟦Chapter5/AuxNorm · proposition_5_1_3_exists⟧ gives an sc⁺-section through $w$ supported in $V(x)$, and the family is its translates $s_g$. These data form an atomic datum ⟦Chapter13/AtomicConstruction · AtomicData.ofFamily⟧.

**Statement of Proposition 18.4.3** (same). Let $C$ admit sc-smooth bump functions, and let $x$, $w$ be as above with $\bar\Gamma(w) \cong e$. There is an sc⁺-multisection functor $\Lambda : E \to \mathbb Q^+$ with $\Lambda(e) > 0$, whose pull-back to the model is atomic (Definition 13.5.1), and which is structurable ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionConstructions · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.proposition_18_4_3⟧. The proof is left to the reader in the text (point G.248 settled).

**Proof.** *Step 1.* Take the atomic data $D$ of `exists_atomicData`. Its multisection $D.\mathrm{mult}$ ⟦Chapter13/AtomicMultisection · AtomicData.mult⟧ is the text's: over $U(x)$ it is $|I|^{-1}\#\{i \mid s_i(p(h)) = h\}$ ⟦Chapter13/AtomicMultisection · AtomicData.count⟧; at a point joined by a morphism $\varphi$ to $U(x)$ it is the count at $\mu(\varphi, h)$; elsewhere it is $1_{h = 0}$. The value at a point joined to $U(x)$ is defined through a chosen morphism; that it does not depend on the choice is the statement that $D.\mathrm{mult}$ is a functor ⟦Chapter13/AtomicMultisection · AtomicData.isWFunctor_mult⟧, which rests on the symmetry of the family under $G_x$. And $D.\mathrm{mult}$ is atomic ⟦Chapter13/AtomicMultisection · AtomicData.isAtomic_mult⟧, in particular an sc⁺-multisection functor.

*Step 2, push forward.* Let $\Lambda = \bar\Gamma_*(D.\mathrm{mult})$. Then $\Lambda$ is a functor and $\Gamma^*\Lambda = D.\mathrm{mult}$, so $\Lambda$ is an sc⁺-multisection functor in the model $c$ and $\Gamma^*\Lambda$ is atomic.

*Step 3, positivity.* $\Lambda(e) = \Lambda(\bar\Gamma(w)) = D.\mathrm{mult}(w)$, using $\bar\Gamma(w) \cong e$. Since $p(w) = x \in U(x)$, $D.\mathrm{mult}(w)$ is the count at $w$ ⟦Chapter13/AtomicMultisection · AtomicData.mult_of_mem⟧, which is positive because $s_{i_0}(x) = w$ ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionConstructions · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.pos_count_of_mem⟧.

*Step 4, structurable.* An atomic multisection is structurable when $|X|$ is paracompact, by Theorem 13.5.3 ⟦Chapter13/AtomicStructurable · theorem_13_5_3_of_paracompact⟧, and the orbit space of the model is paracompact ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNorms · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.hasParacompactOrbitSpace_model⟧.

**The formula of p. 854** ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionConstructions · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.proposition_18_4_3_formula⟧. With the same data, there is a functor $\Lambda$ with $\Gamma^*\Lambda = D.\mathrm{mult}$, with $\Lambda(\bar\Gamma(h)) = |I|^{-1}\#\{i \mid s_i(p(h)) = h\}$ for $p(h) \in U(x)$, and $\Lambda(\bar\Gamma(h)) = 1_{h = 0}$ for $p(h)$ joined to no point of $U(x)$ ⟦Chapter13/AtomicMultisection · AtomicData.mult_of_not_reach⟧.

**Point G.249.** The paragraph after Proposition 18.4.3, on sums of such multisections and their push-forwards with details left to the reader, is a comment, with nothing to formalize (point G.249). What the chapter uses of it is the push-forward from a model in Theorem 18.6.1 (§XVIII.24), the sums of §XVIII.16 and Theorem 18.4.4 (§XVIII.20).

**Witness.** On the reflection bundle, every point of the model $\mathbb R \sqcup \mathbb R$ and of its bundle is smooth ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionFunctorsExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflModel_mem_smoothSet⟧ ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionFunctorsExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflModelW_mem_smoothSet⟧. At the vector $1$ over the point $0$ ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionFunctorsExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflVec⟧, with $\bar\Gamma$ of it equal to $(0, 1)$ ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionFunctorsExamples · ReflectionBundle.bundleModelObj_reflVec⟧, the isotropy $\mathbb Z/2$ of $0$ acts by $v \mapsto -v$, so no non-zero vector is invariant, and the proposition gives an atomic, structurable sc⁺-multisection functor with $\Lambda(0, 1) > 0$ ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionFunctorsExamples · ReflectionBundle.proposition_18_4_3_witness⟧.

**The construction of p. 854 in one uniformizer.** The statement above builds the atomic multisection in a model and pushes it forward. The text builds it in one strong bundle uniformizer $\bar\Psi : G \ltimes K \to E$ over $\Psi : G \ltimes O \to C$, and this construction is also formalized, with Proposition 18.4.3 for it.

*The functor $\Lambda$ of p. 854* ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionConstructionTextLam · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.textLam⟧. For an sc⁺-section $s_1$ of $p : K \to O$, the translates are $s_g(q) = g \ast s_1(g^{-1} \ast q)$ ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionConstructionTextLam · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.textTranslate⟧ and $\Lambda(\bar\Psi(h)) = |G|^{-1}\#\{g : s_g(p(h)) = h\}$ ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionConstructionTextLam · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.textLamK⟧ ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionConstructionTextLam · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.textLam_obj⟧. For $(\beta, e)$ with a morphism $\varphi : \beta \to \Psi(q)$, $\Lambda(\beta, e)$ is the value at $\bar\Psi(h_0) = (\Psi(q), \hat\varphi(e))$; otherwise $\Lambda(\beta, e) = 1_{e = 0}$ ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionConstructionTextLam · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.textLam_of_not_reach⟧.
- *The value does not depend on $(q, \varphi)$* ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionConstructionTextLam · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.textValue_indep⟧ ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionConstructionText · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.textLam_indep⟧. Two choices differ by a morphism of $M(\Psi, \Psi)$, which acts on $K$ by some $g' \in G$, and $\#\{g : s_g(p(g'h)) = g'h\} = \#\{g : s_g(p(h)) = h\}$ by reindexing $g \mapsto g'g$ ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionConstructionTextLam · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.textLamK_smul⟧.
- $\Lambda$ is a functor ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionConstructionTextLam · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isMorInvariant_textLam⟧, and $\Lambda(\bar\Psi(s_1(q))) > 0$, the term $g = 1$ being counted ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionConstructionTextLam · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.textLam_pos⟧. Where all translates vanish, $\Lambda = 1_{h = 0}$ ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionConstructionTextLam · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.textLamK_of_notMem⟧.

**Proposition 18.4.3 for the functor of p. 854** (same) ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionConstructionText · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.proposition_18_4_3_text⟧. *Statement.* For $\bar\Psi \in \bar F(\alpha)$ and $\bar q \in O$ there is an open $W \ni \bar q$, invariant under the stabilizer $G_{\bar q}$, such that for every $G_{\bar q}$-invariant $V \subseteq W$ and every sc⁺-section $s_1$ vanishing off $V$: $\Lambda$ is an sc⁺-multisection functor (Definition 18.3.1), $\Lambda(\bar\Psi(s_1(\bar q))) > 0$, $\bar\Gamma^*\Lambda$ is atomic in the model of $c \sqcup \{\bar\Psi\}$ for every covering family $c$, and $\Lambda$ is structurable. *Proof.*
1. *Natural representations along an open piece* ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionConstructionTextRep · EpGroupoid.PieceEmbedding⟧. In the model of $c \sqcup \{\bar\Psi\}$ the uniformizer is one open summand, and the morphisms between its points are those of $M(\Psi, \Psi)$, whatever $c$ is ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionConstructionTextAtomic · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.pieceEmbedding⟧. A natural representation of $G_{x_0}$ chosen in the model of $\{\bar\Psi\}$ alone is carried to every such model, with $\Phi(g)(f(y)) = f(\Phi_0(g)(y))$ ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionConstructionTextRep · EpGroupoid.PieceEmbedding.naturalRep⟧, through $G_{x_0} \cong G_{f(x_0)}$ ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionConstructionTextRep · EpGroupoid.PieceEmbedding.isotropyEquiv⟧. So the neighbourhoods can be chosen before $c$. The properness property of Proposition 17.1.11 for an open set of $O$ holds in every model containing $\Psi$ ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionConstructionTextAtomic · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.hasPropernessProperty_objMk_image⟧.
2. *The neighbourhood $W$* ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionConstructionText · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.exists_textData⟧. Choose, in $\bar\Psi$ alone, a natural representation $R_0$ of $G_{\bar q}$ on a set with the properness property that meets none of its translates $g \ast U_0$, $g \notin G_{\bar q}$ ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionConstructionText · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.exists_nhds_smul_disjoint⟧, an invariant $V_0$ with $\operatorname{cl}V_0 \subset U_0$, and $W$ the interior of a closed neighbourhood of $\bar q$ inside $V_0$, so that $\operatorname{cl}V \subset V_0$ for every $V \subseteq W$; $W$ is then shrunk to be $G_{\bar q}$-invariant ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionConstructionText · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.exists_stab_invariant⟧. These are the data $\mathrm{TextData}$ ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionConstructionTextAtomic · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.TextData⟧.
3. *The atomic data* ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionConstructionTextAtomic · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.TextData.atomicData⟧. The index set is $G_x \times G$; the seeds are $s_g$ for $g \in G_{\bar q}$ and $0$ for the other $g$, whose translates vanish near $\bar q$; the sections are the $k$-th translates of the seeds, as in Theorem 13.5.4. On $U(x)$ the $(k, g)$-th section is $s_{\gamma g}$ for a $\gamma \in G$ depending on $k$ and the point ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionConstructionTextAtomic · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.TextData.natTerm_seed⟧, so the count of the atomic data is $|G|^{-1}\#\{g : s_g(p(h)) = h\}$ ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionConstructionTextAtomic · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.TextData.count_atomicData⟧.
4. *$\bar\Gamma^*\Lambda$ is the multisection of the atomic data* in the model of $c \sqcup \{\bar\Psi\}$, for every $c$ ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionConstructionTextAtomic · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.TextData.bundlePull_textLam⟧: on $U(x)$ by step 3, at points joined to $U(x)$ by functoriality, and elsewhere both are $1_{h = 0}$. Equivalently, $\Lambda$ is the push-forward of that multisection, the functor of the statement above for these atomic data ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionConstructionText · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.TextData.textLam_eq⟧.
5. *Conclusion.* $\bar\Gamma^*\Lambda$ is atomic (Part XIII), hence an sc⁺-multisection functor; $\Lambda(\bar\Psi(s_1(\bar q))) > 0$ by the positivity above; structurability is Theorem 13.5.3 in the model, whose orbit space is paracompact. $\square$

*Readings.* "Pick $V$" is read as "for $V$ small enough": $W$ is chosen first. For a uniformizer not centred at $\bar q$, the stabilizer $G_{\bar q}$ takes the place of $G$, $W$ is chosen disjoint from its translates $g \ast W$ for $g \notin G_{\bar q}$, and the formula of p. 854 is used unchanged. Smoothness of $s_1$ is read in $p : K \to O$. "Atomic in a suitable model" is read as atomic in the model of every covering family containing $\bar\Psi$. The standing hypothesis $\bar F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ supplies a covering family.

*With the choices of p. 854 made* ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionConstructionText · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.proposition_18_4_3_text_exists⟧. With sc-smooth bump functions, for a smooth $\bar q$ and a smooth $\bar h$ over $\bar q$, an sc⁺-section $s_1$ with $s_1(\bar q) = \bar h$ vanishing off $W$ exists ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionConstructionText · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.exists_textSection⟧, by Lemma 5.3.3 on $O$, which admits bump functions as an open piece of a model ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionConstructionText · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.admitsBumpFunctions_unif⟧. So the hypotheses are satisfiable, and $\Lambda(e) > 0$ for every $e \cong \bar\Psi(\bar h)$.

*With a local section and a cut-off* ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionConstructionText · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.proposition_18_4_3_cutoff⟧. The same for $s_1 = \beta s$, with $s$ a local sc⁺-section on an open $V_s \ni \bar q$ and $\beta : O \to [0, 1]$ sc-smooth, $\beta(\bar q) = 1$, supported in $W \cap V_s$; here $\Lambda$ is written through the morphisms $\varphi : \Psi(q) \to P(e)$ ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionConstructionText · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.cutoffLam⟧, with values independent of $\varphi$, and it is the functor above for $\beta s$ ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionConstructionText · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.cutoffLam_eq⟧.

### XVIII.20 Theorem 18.4.4 (2017: Theorem 18.7), pp. 855–856: the ∂-extension theorem

**The domain supports.** For $\Lambda : E \to \mathbb Q^+$, $|\mathrm{dom\text{-}supp}(\Lambda)| \subset |C|$ is the closure of the classes of the objects $\alpha$ carrying a vector $e \ne 0$ with $\Lambda(\alpha, e) > 0$ ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionConstructions · orbitDomSupp⟧, the categorical form of Definition 13.2.3; for $\Lambda$ on $E|\partial C$, the same with $\alpha \in \partial C$ ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionConstructions · orbitBoundaryDomSupp⟧, the categorical form of Definition 14.1.7's boundary domain support. The set of such objects is saturated for a functor $\Lambda$ ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionConstructions · IsMorInvariant.exists_ne_zero_pos_of_isomorphic⟧.

**Three comparisons with the model.** *(a)* Whenever $N(\Lambda)(\Gamma x)$ is finite, its real value is the model's pointwise norm of $\Gamma^*\Lambda$ at $x$, for any $\Lambda$ ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionConstructions · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.pointwiseNorm_bundlePull_of_ne_top⟧: a finite supremum forces every vector of the support into $W_{0,1}$, since $N \circ \bar\Gamma = \infty$ off $W_{0,1}$; then the two suprema run over the same finite values, as in §XVIII.15. *(b)* The boundary domain support of $\Gamma^*\Lambda$ in the model lies over $|\mathrm{dom\text{-}supp}(\Lambda)| \subset |\partial C|$ ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionConstructions · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.mem_orbitBoundaryDomSupp_of_model⟧, by continuity of $x \mapsto |\Gamma x|$, `mem_boundary_model_iff` and the fibre isomorphism $\bar\Gamma_x$. *(c)* The preimage of $|\mathrm{dom\text{-}supp}(\Lambda)|$ in the model lies in the domain support of $\Gamma^*\Lambda$ ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionConstructions · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.preimage_orbitDomSupp_subset⟧: since $|\Gamma|$ is a homeomorphism, the preimage of a closure is the closure of the preimage; the preimage under $\pi$ of a closure is the closure of the saturated preimage; and each object over a class of the set carries, through $\bar\Gamma_x^{-1}$, a non-zero vector of positive weight.

**Statement of Theorem 18.4.4 in a model** (explicit). Let $c$ be a covering family, $C$ tame, the object M-polyfold of $X_c$ admit sc-smooth partitions of unity, $N$ an auxiliary norm in the model of $c$, and $\Lambda$ a functor whose pull-back $\Gamma_c^*\Lambda$ is structurable over $\partial X_c$ (Definition 14.1.7). Let $U \subset |C|$ be open with $|\mathrm{dom\text{-}supp}(\Lambda)| \subseteq U$ (the boundary domain support), and $\beta : C \to \mathbb R$ constant along morphisms, $\beta \ge 0$, $\beta \circ \Gamma$ continuous, $\mathrm{cl}\,\pi\{\beta \ne 0\} \subseteq U$, and $N(\Lambda)(\alpha) < \beta(\alpha)$ in $[0, \infty]$ whenever $|\alpha| \in |\mathrm{dom\text{-}supp}(\Lambda)|$. Then there is a structurable $\Lambda'$ with (1) $N(\Lambda') \le \beta$, (2) $|\mathrm{dom\text{-}supp}(\Lambda')| \subseteq U$, (3) $\Lambda' = \Lambda$ on $E|\partial C$ ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionConstructions · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.theorem_18_4_4⟧. *Readings.* "$\Lambda : E|\partial C \to \mathbb Q^+$ structurable" is a functor on $E$, of which only the values over $\partial C$ enter, structurable over the boundary in the model. "Continuous" is continuity of $\beta \circ \Gamma$ on the model, equivalently of $|\beta|$ on $|C| \cong |X_c|$, and $|\mathrm{supp}\,\beta|$ is the closure of $\pi\{\beta \ne 0\}$. "$C$ admits sc-smooth partitions of unity" is read as Theorem 14.1.9 uses it, on the object M-polyfold; by Remark 18.4.2 (§XVIII.18) it gives Definition 18.4.1.

**Proof.** The text proves the theorem "by using an sc-smooth model"; the formal proof is Theorem 14.1.9 (Part XIV) ⟦Chapter14/BoundaryExtensionTheorem · theorem_14_1_9⟧ in the model, pushed forward along $\bar\Gamma$.

*Step 1, the data in the model.* $X_c$ is tame because $C$ is (Part XVII) and has paracompact orbit space. Put $U_X = \pi^{-1}(|\Gamma|^{-1}(U))$, open and saturated, and $b = \beta \circ \Gamma$, constant along morphisms of the model, continuous, non-negative. The support of $b$ lies in $U_X$, by continuity of $x \mapsto |\Gamma x|$ and $\mathrm{cl}\,\pi\{\beta \ne 0\} \subseteq U$. The boundary domain support of $\Gamma^*\Lambda$ lies in $U_X$ by comparison (b). On it the model's pointwise norm of $\Gamma^*\Lambda$ is $< b$: at such $x$, $N(\Lambda)(\Gamma x) < \beta(\Gamma x)$ by hypothesis and (b), so $N(\Lambda)(\Gamma x)$ is finite, and comparison (a) turns the inequality in $[0, \infty]$ into the real inequality.

*Step 2, Theorem 14.1.9.* It gives a structurable $\Lambda_m$ on $W_c$ with model pointwise norm $\le b$, domain support in $U_X$, and $\Lambda_m = \Gamma^*\Lambda$ over $\partial X_c$.

*Step 3, push forward.* $\Lambda_m$ is a functor, hence constant on isomorphism classes; let $\Lambda' = \bar\Gamma_*\Lambda_m$ (§XVIII.19), so $\Gamma^*\Lambda' = \Lambda_m$ and $\Lambda'$ is structurable in the model $c$.

*Step 4, the norm bound.* For an object $\alpha$, take $x$ with $\Gamma(x) \cong \alpha$; $N(\Lambda')$ and $\beta$ are constant on isomorphism classes. $N(\Lambda')(\Gamma x)$ is finite and equal to the model's pointwise norm (`pointwiseNorm_bundlePull`, §XVIII.15), which is $\le b(x) = \beta(\Gamma x)$.

*Step 5, the domain support.* A class $y \in |\mathrm{dom\text{-}supp}(\Lambda')|$ is $|\Gamma(x)|$ for some $x$, which by comparison (c) lies in the domain support of $\Gamma^*\Lambda' = \Lambda_m$, hence in $U_X$, so $y \in U$.

*Step 6, the boundary.* For $e$ over a boundary object, choose $w$ in $W_c$ with $\bar\Gamma(w) \cong e$, by the homeomorphism $|W_c| \cong |E|$ ⟦Chapter17/BundleModel · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isHomeomorph_bundleModelOrbitMap⟧. Its base point $p(w)$ is in $\partial X_c$ by `mem_boundary_model_iff`, since the base of $e$ is a boundary object isomorphic to $\Gamma(p(w))$. Then $\Lambda'(e) = \Lambda_m(w) = \Gamma^*\Lambda(w) = \Lambda(e)$.

**The model-free form.** *Boundary structurability does not depend on the model* ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationModelIndependence · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsBoundaryStructurableIn.of_comparison⟧. For tame $C$ and two covering families, $\Gamma_c^*\Lambda$ is structurable over $\partial X_c$ if $\Gamma_{c'}^*\Lambda$ is over $\partial X_{c'}$: $\Gamma_c^*\Lambda$ is the pull-back along $\bar f_{c, c'}$ (`bundlePull_eq_pullMult`, §XVIII.15), and Proposition 14.1.8 in its class form, over the tame model, carries boundary structurability along generalized strong bundle isomorphisms ⟦Chapter14/FacePush · GenSBMap.proposition_14_1_8_pullMult⟧, with no hypothesis beyond the text. So "$\Lambda$ structurable over $\partial C$" ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationModelIndependence · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsBoundaryStructurableIn⟧ ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationModelIndependence · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsBoundaryStructurableFunctor⟧, defined with some model, holds in every model ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationModelIndependence · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isBoundaryStructurableFunctor_iff⟧. "Continuous functor $\beta$" is read as in Theorem 18.6.1: constant along morphisms and continuous in every uniformizer ⟦Chapter18/Perturbation · PolyfoldConstruction.IsContinuousFunctionFunctor⟧, which gives continuity on every model, piece by piece ⟦Chapter18/Perturbation · PolyfoldConstruction.IsContinuousFunctionFunctor.continuous_model⟧.

**Statement of Theorem 18.4.4, model-free** (explicit). Over a tame $C$ whose objects admit sc-smooth partitions of unity in some covering model (`HasModelMPolyfoldPartitionsOfUnity`), for an auxiliary norm $N$, a functor $\Lambda$ structurable over $\partial C$, an open $U \supseteq |\mathrm{dom\text{-}supp}(\Lambda)|$ and a continuous functor $\beta \ge 0$ with $|\mathrm{supp}\,\beta| \subseteq U$ and $N(\Lambda) < \beta$ on $\mathrm{dom\text{-}supp}(\Lambda)$, there is a structurable $\Lambda'$ with (1)–(3) ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationModelIndependence · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.theorem_18_4_4_modelFree⟧. *Proof.* Take the model $c$ with partitions of unity. $N$ is an auxiliary norm in it, $\Lambda$ is boundary-structurable in it by `isBoundaryStructurableFunctor_iff`, and $\beta \circ \Gamma_c$ is continuous; apply the theorem in the model. Only the partitions of unity are tied to a model, since the M-polyfold notion is not compared between models (§XVIII.18).

**Witnesses.** *Reflection bundle* (weak: $\partial C = \emptyset$). The model has no boundary ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionFunctorsExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflModel_boundary⟧, so every functor is structurable over $\partial X$, a good system for $\partial X$ existing by Theorem 14.1.6 ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionFunctorsExamples · SBLocalization.isBoundaryStructurable_of_boundary_eq_empty⟧. With the non-zero structurable $\Lambda$ of §XVIII.19, $U = |C|$ and $\beta = 1$, the theorem in the model applies ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionFunctorsExamples · ReflectionBundle.theorem_18_4_4_witness⟧, and so does the model-free form ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationModelIndependenceExamples · ReflectionBundle.theorem_18_4_4_modelFree_witness⟧, with partitions of unity in the model $\mathbb R \sqcup \mathbb R$ ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationModelIndependenceExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflQ_hasModelMPolyfoldPartitionsOfUnity⟧ and $|\mathrm{dom\text{-}supp}(\Lambda|\partial C)| = \emptyset$ ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationModelIndependenceExamples · ReflectionBundle.orbitBoundaryDomSupp_reflection⟧; condition (3) is then empty, while (1) and (2) are not.

*Half-space bundle, the zero datum* (weak: empty domain support on $\partial C$). The partitions of unity hold in the one-copy model ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceWitnesses · HalfSpaceReflection.halfQ_hasModelMPolyfoldPartitionsOfUnity⟧. The boundary datum is the multisection functor $\Lambda_0$ of the zero section (Remark 18.1.6), structurable over $\partial C$ since its pull-back is the zero multisection ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceWitnesses · HalfSpaceReflection.isBoundaryStructurableFunctor_zeroFunctor⟧, positive on every $(\alpha, 0)$ with $\alpha \in \partial C$, with empty domain support on $\partial C$ ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceWitnesses · HalfSpaceReflection.orbitBoundaryDomSupp_zeroFunctor⟧ and $N(\Lambda_0) = 0$ ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceWitnesses · HalfSpaceReflection.pointwiseNormFunctor_zeroFunctor_eq_zero⟧. With $U = |C|$, $\beta = 1$ ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceWitnesses · HalfSpaceReflection.halfS_isContinuousFunctionFunctor_one⟧, the theorem gives $\Lambda'$ equal to $\Lambda_0$ over $\partial C$, the corner with isotropy $\mathbb Z/2$ among the boundary objects ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceBundleConstruction · HalfSpaceReflection.corner_mem_boundarySet⟧ ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceBundleConstruction · HalfSpaceReflection.exists_ne_unit_corner⟧; this is ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceWitnesses · HalfSpaceReflection.theorem_18_4_4_modelFree_witness_boundary⟧.

*Half-space bundle, a constant datum* (non-degenerate). For a vector $v$, $\Lambda_v(\alpha, w) = 1$ if $w = v$ and $0$ otherwise ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceBoundaryData · HalfSpaceReflection.constFunctor⟧ is a functor because $\mathbb Z/2$ acts trivially on the fibres ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceBoundaryData · HalfSpaceReflection.isMorInvariant_constFunctor⟧; its pull-back to the model is the multisection of the constant section $x \mapsto (x, v)$ ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceBoundaryData · HalfSpaceReflection.bundlePull_constFunctor⟧. That this is structurable over $\partial X$ holds because the multisection of an sc⁺-section that is a functor is structurable over the boundary, with a one-element index set ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceBoundaryData · SBLocalization.isBoundaryStructurable_sectionMulti_of_isWFunctor⟧ ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceBoundaryData · SBLocalization.equivariantBoundaryStructure⟧. Hence $\Lambda_v$ is structurable over $\partial C$ ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceBoundaryData · HalfSpaceReflection.isBoundaryStructurableFunctor_constFunctor⟧, and for $v \ne 0$ its boundary domain support is $\mathrm{cl}\,|\partial C|$ ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceBoundaryData · HalfSpaceReflection.orbitBoundaryDomSupp_constFunctor⟧, containing the corner ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceBoundaryData · HalfSpaceReflection.corner_mem_orbitBoundaryDomSupp⟧. The auxiliary norm is $N(\alpha, w) = |w|$ ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceBoundaryData · HalfSpaceReflection.halfNorm⟧: every auxiliary norm $N_0$ of this bundle has the form $N_0(\alpha, w) = |w| N_0(\alpha, 1)$, the fibres being $\mathbb R$ ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceBoundaryData · HalfSpaceReflection.auxNorm_objMk_eq⟧, and dividing an existing one by the continuous positive weight $N_0(\alpha, 1)$ gives $|w|$, an auxiliary norm ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceBoundaryData · HalfSpaceReflection.isAuxNormFunctor_halfNorm⟧. With $v = -3/4$, $N(\Lambda_v) = 3/4 < 1 = \beta$ ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceBoundaryData · HalfSpaceReflection.pointwiseNormFunctor_halfNorm_constDatum⟧, and the theorem gives a structurable $\Lambda'$ with $N(\Lambda') \le 1$ and $\Lambda' = \Lambda_v$ over $\partial C$, in particular $\Lambda'(\alpha, -3/4) = 1$ at every boundary object, the corner included ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceBoundaryData · HalfSpaceReflection.theorem_18_4_4_modelFree_witness_constDatum⟧.

### XVIII.21 The orientation category O_f and Definition 18.5.1 (2017: Definition 18.14), pp. 856–858

**Statement.** *Definition 18.5.1* (explicit) ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.OrCatObj⟧ ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.OrCatMor⟧ ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.definition_18_5_1_hyp⟧: the objects of $O_f$ are the pairs of a smooth object $\alpha$ of $C$ and one of the two orientations of $\mathrm{DET}(f, \alpha)$; the morphisms are the pairs of a smooth morphism $\varphi$ and an orientation at its source; the target of $(\varphi, o)$ is $(t(\varphi), \varphi_* o)$, and the projection $\sigma : O_f \to C_\infty$ commutes with source and target. Over every smooth $\alpha$ the fibre of $\sigma$ has two points, and $\varphi_*$ is a bijection between the fibres over $s(\varphi)$ and $t(\varphi)$ ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.orPush_bijective_two⟧, with $\mathrm{Id}_* = \mathrm{Id}$ and $(\psi \circ \varphi)_* = \psi_* \circ \varphi_*$ ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.orPush_unit⟧ ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.orPush_comp⟧. *The obstruction after the definition* (same) ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.not_exists_isOrSectionFunctor⟧: if an isotropy morphism $g \in G_\alpha$ reverses an orientation of $\mathrm{DET}(f, \alpha)$, then $\sigma$ has no section functor. The verdict for the definition is "explicit" because $\mathrm{DET}(f, \alpha)$ and $\varphi_*$ are read through the determinant of Part III in one chosen model; the verdict for the obstruction is "same".

**The readings and the added hypotheses.** The text forms $\mathrm{DET}(f, \alpha)$ from the linearizations of $f$ at $\alpha$ and transports orientations along morphisms. The formalization does this in a sc-smooth model where Part III has the determinant and its transport.

- *The reference model.* Under the standing reading $\bar F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ for every object (`hF`, ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.HasStrongBundleUniformizers⟧), one strong bundle uniformizer $\bar\Psi_\alpha$ is chosen for every object. The family of all of them is the reference covering family $R$ ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNorms · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.refCoveringFamily⟧, and $q_\alpha$ is the point of $\bar\Psi_\alpha$ over $\alpha$ ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.refPt⟧ ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.obj_refPt⟧. The point $q_\alpha$ is smooth exactly when $\alpha$ is ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.mem_smoothSet_model_iff'⟧ ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.refPt_mem⟧.
- *$\mathrm{DET}(f, \alpha)$.* It is read as the two-point set of orientations of the model section $f^R$ at $q_\alpha$ ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.DetOr⟧. The model section is sc-Fredholm when $f$ is (`hf`, ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.modelFred⟧).
- *$\varphi_*$.* A morphism $\varphi : \alpha \to \alpha'$ of $C$ gives the morphism $(q_\alpha, \varphi, q_{\alpha'})$ of the reference model ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.refMor⟧. $\varphi_*$ is the action of that morphism on orientations, Definition 12.5.2 (Part XII) ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.orPush⟧ ⟦Chapter12/OrientationBundleEp · EpStrongBundle.orMor⟧. Definition 12.5.2 needs sc⁺-sections through the points, and these come from sc-smooth bump functions. So the formalization assumes the tame form of $C$ (`hT`, `S.IsTame`) and bump functions on the object M-polyfold of some covering model (`hbump`, ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionConstructions · PolyfoldConstruction.AdmitsModelBumpFunctions⟧), the hypotheses under which Theorem 6.6.17 (Part VI) holds in the models. Bump functions pass from one covering model to every other one ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.modelBump⟧ and give the sc⁺-sections ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.modelSec⟧ (Theorem 12.3.2 (1), ⟦Chapter12/BumpEp · EpGroupoid.theorem_12_3_2_1⟧). The tame forms enter through the tame model ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.modelTame⟧ (Part XVII, ⟦Chapter17/ModelEpGroupoid · PolyfoldConstruction.isTame_modelEpGroupoid⟧). The tame germ data (`hfT`, ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsScFredholmSectionFunctorTame⟧, every local representative tame sc-Fredholm) enter through the tame model section ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.modelFredTame⟧.

The joint witness of `hT`, `hbump`, `hf`, `hfT` and `hF` is the diagonal section of the reflection bundle ⟦Chapter18/OrientationsExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflDiag_orientation_hyp⟧. It is weak in one respect: $\partial C = \emptyset$. It is not weak in another: the isotropy $-1$ at $0$ acts non-trivially on the fibre, and $f$ vanishes only at $0$.

**Proof of the functor laws.** *Step 1, morphisms in the reference model.* The morphism $(q_\alpha, \varphi, q_{\alpha'})$ is built from $\varphi$ by the transition construction of the polyfold construction. The unit of $C$ goes to the unit of the model ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.refMor_unit⟧, and composites go to composites ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.refMor_comp⟧.

*Step 2, $\varphi_*$ is functorial.* $\mathrm{Id}_* = \mathrm{Id}$ comes from Step 1 and the unit law of Definition 12.5.2 ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.orPush_unit⟧. $(\psi\varphi)_* = \psi_*\varphi_*$ comes from Step 1, the composition law of Definition 12.5.2, and the independence of the transported orientation from the proofs of the endpoint equalities ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.orPush_comp⟧.

*Step 3, two points and a bijection.* Every fibre of the orientation bundle of a sc-Fredholm section has exactly two points ⟦Chapter6/OrientationFunctor · exists_two_orx⟧. $\varphi_*$ is an equivalence, hence a bijection ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.orPush_bijective_two⟧.

*Step 4, the category.* An object is a smooth $\alpha$ with an orientation; a morphism is a $\varphi$ with smooth source and an orientation at the source. Its target uses $\varphi_*$, and the target object is smooth ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isSmoothObj_tgt⟧ ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.OrCatMor.tgt⟧. That $\sigma$ commutes with source and target holds by definition (`⟨rfl, rfl⟩`) ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.definition_18_5_1_hyp⟧. The name keeps `_hyp` because the target uses `hbump`.

**Proof of the obstruction.** A section of $\sigma$ is a choice $\alpha \mapsto o_\alpha$ for every smooth $\alpha$ ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.OrSection⟧. It is a section functor if $o_{t(\varphi)} = \varphi_* o_{s(\varphi)}$ for every morphism with smooth source ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsOrSectionFunctor⟧.

*Step 1.* Let $g \in G_\alpha$, and let $\omega$ be an orientation at $\alpha$ with $g_*\omega \ne \omega$. If $o$ is a section functor, the functor property at $g$ (source and target $\alpha$) gives $g_* o_\alpha = o_\alpha$.

*Step 2.* The fibre has two points, and $g_*$ is injective. Since $g_*$ leaves $o_\alpha$ unchanged, it leaves the other point unchanged as well. So $g_*$ is the identity, against $g_*\omega \ne \omega$ ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.not_exists_isOrSectionFunctor⟧.

The second orientation of a fibre, $-o$, is ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · otherOr⟧, with ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · otherOr_ne⟧ and ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · eq_otherOr_of_ne⟧.

**Witnesses.** *The fibre over $0$.* For the reflection bundle at $0$, the fibre of $\sigma$ has two points and the isotropy $g = (-1, 0)$ acts by a bijection ⟦Chapter18/OrientationsExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflDiag_orPush_isotropy⟧.

*$g_*$ is the identity there* ⟦Chapter18/OrientationWitness · ReflectionBundle.reflDiag_orPush_isotropy_eq⟧. The proof is general.

- At a point where the linearization is bijective, the determinant line is canonically trivial ⟦Chapter18/OrientationWitness · conjDet_eq_refl_of_bijective⟧ ⟦Chapter18/OrientationWitness · ray_map_conjDet_of_bijective⟧.
- The linearization transforms as $f'(x')\circ T\sigma = \mu(\sigma)\circ f'(x)$ ⟦Chapter18/OrientationWitness · EpStrongBundle.zeroLin_morTx⟧.
- The linearization set at such a point is contractible, and the conjugation acts by the identity on its rays ⟦Chapter18/OrientationWitness · EpStrongBundle.orMor_eq_self_of_bijective⟧.

For the diagonal section $f'(0) = \mathrm{Id}$ ⟦Chapter18/OrientationWitness · ReflectionBundle.bijective_zeroLin_reflSingleModel_zero⟧. So $-1$ acts on the tangent space and on the fibre by the same sign, and it reverses no orientation. The isotropy is non-trivial on the fibre, yet it does not obstruct here.

### XVIII.22 The push-forward Ψ̄_*, Definition 18.5.2 and Remark 18.5.3 (2017: Definition 18.15, Remark 18.7), pp. 858–859: the continuity property

**Statement.** *The paragraph before Definition 18.5.2* (same) ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.toRef⟧ ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.catOf⟧ ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.pullCat⟧ ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.pullCat_germ⟧: a section functor $o$ of $\sigma$ gives in every covering model an invariant section $\bar\Gamma^* o$ of the orientation bundle of the model section. For a morphism $\Phi$ of the model, the germ of $\bar\Gamma^* o$ at the source is carried by the local strong bundle isomorphism of $\Phi$ to its germ at the target. This settles point G.250. *Definition 18.5.2* (restated) ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.HasContinuityProperty⟧: $o$ has the continuity property if for every smooth $\alpha$, every $\bar\Psi \in \bar F(\alpha)$ and every $\bar o$ with $\Psi(\bar o) = \alpha$, $\bar\Gamma^* o$ is a continuous orientation (Definition 6.6.12) on a neighbourhood of $\bar o$ in the model of $R \sqcup \{\bar\Psi\}$. *Remark 18.5.3* (stronger) ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.remark_18_5_3_hyp⟧: for a section functor, the continuity property is equivalent to continuity of $\bar\Gamma^* o$ on all of every covering model. It is also equivalent to its form with one object and one uniformizer per orbit ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.HasContinuityPropertyRep⟧.

**The readings.** We read $\bar\Gamma^* o$ in Definition 18.5.2 as $\bar\Psi^* o$, and the object $\Psi(\bar o) = \alpha$ as the representative $\alpha_z$ of its class. We read the local representative $f_{\bar\Psi}$ with the orientations $\bar\Psi^* o$ as the piece $\bar\Psi$ of the model of $R \sqcup \{\bar\Psi\}$ ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.adjoinFamily⟧. We read "related by continuation near $\bar o$" as Definition 6.6.12 (Part VI): a continuous orientation over an open neighbourhood, for the local transport of Theorem 6.6.13 ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsModelContOrOn⟧ ⟦Chapter6/OrientationSpace · IsContOrOn⟧ ⟦Chapter6/OrientationFunctor · orLocalTransport⟧. The added hypotheses are those of §XVIII.21, with the same witness.

**Proof, the transport between models.** A section of $\sigma$ in the model of a covering family $c$ is a choice of orientation at every smooth point of the model ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.ModelOr⟧. It is invariant if the action of every morphism of the model preserves it ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsModelInvariant⟧.

*Step 1, along an inclusion of families.* An inclusion $c \subset d$ of covering families gives a strong bundle equivalence $W_c \to W_d$ ⟦Chapter17/BundleModelComparison · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.equivalences_bundleIncl⟧ which pulls back the model section of $d$ to that of $c$. Part XII pulls orientations back and pushes them forward along it ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.inclPullOr⟧ ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.inclPushOr⟧. Both preserve invariance ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isModelInvariant_inclPullOr⟧ ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isModelInvariant_inclPushOr⟧. On invariant sections they are inverse to each other ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.inclPullOr_inclPushOr⟧ ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.inclPushOr_inclPullOr⟧ (⟦Chapter18/Orientations · EpStrongBundle.IsStrongBundleEquivalence.pushOr_pullOr_of_invariant⟧). They are compatible with composites of inclusions ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.inclPullOr_comp⟧ ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.inclPushOr_comp⟧.

*Step 2, between any two models.* For covering families $a$, $b$, let $b \sqcup a$ be their sum ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.sumFamily⟧. The transport ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.modelTransport⟧ pushes a section of the model of $a$ forward along $W_a \to W_{b \sqcup a}$ and pulls it back along $W_b \to W_{b \sqcup a}$. The cospan represents the comparison $\bar f_{b,a}$ of Theorem 17.1.18 (Part XVII). The transport preserves invariance ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isModelInvariant_modelTransport⟧. On invariant sections it has the following properties.

- It can be computed through any larger family that contains both $a$ and $b$ ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.modelTransport_eq_via⟧, by Step 1.
- It satisfies the cocycle law, through $c \sqcup (b \sqcup a)$ ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.modelTransport_comp⟧.
- The transports from $a$ to $b$ and from $b$ to $a$ are inverse to each other, through $a \sqcup b$ and the swap of summands ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.modelTransport_modelTransport⟧.
- From a model to itself it is the identity ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.inclPushOr_id⟧ ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.modelTransport_self⟧.
- Along an inclusion it is the pull-back ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.modelTransport_eq_inclPullOr⟧.
- It is injective ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.modelTransport_injective⟧.

**Proof, $\bar\Gamma^* o$.** *Step 1, the reference model.* For a smooth point $x$ of the reference model, the morphism $q_{\Gamma x} \to x$ has the unit of $C$ underneath ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.refLift⟧. The section $\bar\Gamma^* o$ of the reference model is $x \mapsto (q_{\Gamma x} \to x)_* o_{\Gamma x}$ ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.toRef⟧. It is invariant ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isModelInvariant_toRef⟧: a morphism $x \to y$ composed with $q_{\Gamma x} \to x$ is $q_{\Gamma y} \to y$ composed with the morphism of $C$ underneath ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.comp_refLift⟧. With the functor property of $o$ and the composition law of Definition 12.5.2 ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · EpStrongBundle.orMor_orMor_eq⟧, both sides agree.

*Step 2, the converse.* An invariant section $o'$ of the reference model gives the section $\alpha \mapsto o'(q_\alpha)$ of $\sigma$ ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.catOf⟧. It is a section functor ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isOrSectionFunctor_catOf⟧. The two constructions are inverse to each other ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.toRef_catOf⟧ ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.catOf_toRef⟧, because $q_\alpha \to q_\alpha$ is the unit ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.refLift_refPt⟧.

*Step 3, any model.* In a covering model $c$, $\bar\Gamma^* o$ is the transport of Step 1 from $R$ to $c$ ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.pullCat⟧. It is invariant ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isModelInvariant_pullCat⟧. Along an inclusion $c \subset d$ it pulls back to itself ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.inclPullOr_pullCat⟧ (by the inclusion form and the cocycle law of the transport). It is the section of Step 1 in $R$ ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.pullCat_ref⟧. For an invariant $o$ of a model $c$, the section of $\sigma$ defined by the transport of $o$ to $R$ pulls back to the transport of $o$ ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.pullCat_catOf⟧.

*Step 4, the germ identity* ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.pullCat_germ⟧. Let $\Phi$ be a morphism of the model. The pull-back of an orientation by the local strong bundle isomorphism of $\Phi^{-1}$ is the action of the morphism of the sheet through that point ⟦Chapter12/OrientationGerm · pullOrx_morIso_eq_orMor⟧. Invariance of $\bar\Gamma^* o$ then gives the identity on the target set of the sheet, an open neighbourhood of the source of $\Phi$.

**Proof of the continuity lemmas.** A section is a model orientation if it is invariant and continuous on all of the model ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsModelOrientation⟧.

*Step 1, along inclusions.* Continuity on $U$ pulls back along an inclusion $c \subset d$ to continuity on the preimage of $U$ ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isModelContOrOn_inclPullOr⟧, by Part XII's continuity of the pull-back along a strong bundle equivalence ⟦Chapter12/OrientationEquivalenceContinuity · isContOrOn_pullOr_hyp⟧. Continuity also pushes forward ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isModelContOrOn_inclPushOr⟧ (⟦Chapter12/OrientationEquivalenceContinuity · isContOrOn_pushOr_hyp⟧). Continuity of the pull-back near a point gives continuity near its image ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.exists_isModelContOrOn_of_inclPullOr⟧ (⟦Chapter18/Orientations · EpStrongBundle.IsStrongBundleEquivalence.exists_isContOrOn_of_pullOr⟧). So model orientations pull back, push forward, and are transported to model orientations ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isModelOrientation_inclPullOr⟧ ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isModelOrientation_inclPushOr⟧ ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isModelOrientation_modelTransport⟧.

*Step 2, along morphisms.* For an invariant section, continuity near the source of a morphism of the model gives continuity near its target ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.exists_isModelContOrOn_of_mor⟧ (⟦Chapter18/Orientations · EpStrongBundle.exists_isContOrOn_of_mor⟧, through the local strong bundle isomorphism of the morphism).

*Step 3, local to global.* Continuity near every point is continuity on all of the model ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isModelContOrOn_univ_of_local⟧ ⟦Chapter12/DyadicPathInduction · isContOrOn_of_local_or⟧.

**Proof of Remark 18.5.3.** *Step 1, the representative form.* In the class of every smooth $\alpha$ there should be an object $\alpha'$, a morphism $\alpha' \to \alpha$, a $\bar\Psi \in \bar F(\alpha')$ and a point over $\alpha'$ near which $\bar\Gamma^* o$ is continuous in the model of $R \sqcup \{\bar\Psi\}$ ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.HasContinuityPropertyRep⟧. The continuity property gives it with $\alpha' = \alpha$, the unit, and the chosen $\bar\Psi_\alpha$.

*Step 2, from one representative to every model* ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isModelContOrOn_pullCat_of_rep⟧.

1. Let $x$ be a point of the model of $c$. Take the representative data for $\Gamma x$: $\alpha'$, $\varphi : \alpha' \to \Gamma x$, $\bar\Psi$, a point $q$, and a neighbourhood on which $\bar\Gamma^* o$ is continuous in the model $J$ of $R \sqcup \{\bar\Psi\}$.
2. Pass to the model of $M = c \sqcup J$. Since $\bar\Gamma^* o$ in $J$ is the pull-back of $\bar\Gamma^* o$ in $M$, Step 1 of the continuity lemmas gives continuity near $q$ in $M$.
3. The morphism $(q, \varphi, x)$ of $M$ carries this continuity to a neighbourhood of $x$, by Step 2 of the continuity lemmas.
4. Pull it back to $c$ along the first summand.

Step 3 of the continuity lemmas then gives continuity on all of the model of $c$.

*Step 3, the equivalences* ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.remark_18_5_3_hyp⟧. Continuity on all of every model gives the continuity property with the neighbourhood the whole model. Together with Steps 1 and 2 this closes both equivalences. The verdict is "stronger" because both reductions of the remark are equivalences. The name keeps `_hyp` for the hypotheses of §XVIII.21.

**Witnesses.** For the diagonal section of the reflection bundle, the transported orientation is continuous near every point of the model of $R \sqcup \{\bar\Psi\}$ ⟦Chapter18/OrientationWitness · ReflectionBundle.exists_isModelContOrOn_reflDiag⟧. The proof takes a section of the orientation bundle that agrees along every sc-homotopy of the chart ⟦Chapter12/OrientationGeneralizedIsoOriented · exists_continuous_orSec_of_hom_eq⟧. The chart is the single model $x \mapsto x$ ⟦Chapter18/OrientationWitness · ReflectionBundle.reflSingleModel⟧, whose paths are all sc-homotopic ⟦Chapter18/OrientationWitness · ReflectionBundle.scHomotopic_reflSingleModel⟧ ⟦Chapter18/OrientationWitness · ReflectionBundle.pathCat_hom_eq_reflSingleModel⟧. The witness is weak in that $\partial C = \emptyset$.

### XVIII.23 Definition 18.5.4, Remark 18.5.5 and Proposition 18.5.6 (2017: Definition 18.16, Remark 18.8, Proposition 18.2), p. 859

**Statement.** *Definition 18.5.4* (same) ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsOrientation⟧ ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsOrientable⟧: an orientation of $f$ is a section functor of $\sigma$ with the continuity property, and $f$ is orientable if it has one. An orientable $f$ has two orientations that differ at every smooth object ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.exists_two_orientations⟧. In a model, the model orientations are the orientations of Definition 12.5.4 (Part XII), for every topology on the orientation bundle with the properties of Theorem 6.6.17 ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isModelOrientation_iff⟧.

*Remark 18.5.5* (explicit) ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.remark_18_5_5_mp_hyp⟧ ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.remark_18_5_5_mpr_hyp⟧: $f$ is orientable iff $|\sigma|$ is $2:1$ and has a continuous section. This settles point G.251.

*Proposition 18.5.6* (stronger), settling point G.252:

- (1): $f$ is orientable iff the model section of any covering family is ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.proposition_18_5_6_one_hyp⟧.
- (2): an orientation of $f$ pulls back to orientations of any two models ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.proposition_18_5_6_two_hyp⟧.
- (3): orientations $o$, $o'$ of the models of $c$ and $c'$ come from one orientation of $f$ iff $\bar f$ pulls $o'$ back to $o$ ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.proposition_18_5_6_three_hyp⟧.

**The readings and the added hypotheses.**

- *Remark 18.5.5.* The text gives no topology on $|O_f|$. We read $|\sigma| : |O_f| \to |C_\infty|$ as the orbit map of $\sigma$ in the reference model; its orbit space is homeomorphic to $|C|$ by Theorem 17.1.18. The topology is any one with the properties of Theorem 6.6.17 (`ht`, ⟦Chapter6/OrientationSpace · IsOrientationTopology⟧). Its witness is the orientation topology of Part XII in the model ⟦Chapter18/Perturbation · SBLocalization.isOrientationTopology_orTopology⟧, which exists under `hT`, `hbump` and `hfT`.
- *Proposition 18.5.6 (3).* We read "they correspond to the same orientation for $f$ provided …" as an equivalence; the converse holds as well. We read the pull-back by $\bar f$ as the transport from $c'$ to $c$ (§XVIII.22).
- The `_hyp` names carry `hT`, `hbump`, `hf`, `hfT` and `hF` as in §XVIII.21, with the witness ⟦Chapter18/OrientationsExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflDiag_orientation_hyp⟧.

**Proof of the model orientations.** *Step 1, a model orientation comes from an orientation.* An orientation $o$ gives the model orientation $\bar\Gamma^* o$ in every model ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isModelOrientation_pullCat⟧. It is invariant by §XVIII.22, and continuous on the whole model by Remark 18.5.3.

*Step 2, an orientation comes from a model orientation* ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isOrientation_catOf⟧. Let $o$ be a model orientation in $c$. Transport it to $R$ and read it as a section of $\sigma$ (§XVIII.22, Step 2). That section is a section functor. Its pull-back to every model $d$ is the transport of $o$ to $d$ ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.pullCat_catOf⟧, which is a model orientation. By Remark 18.5.3 it has the continuity property.

*Step 3, Definition 12.5.4* ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isModelOrientation_iff⟧. For an invariant section of the model, continuity on the whole model in the sense of Definition 6.6.12 is continuity of the section into the orientation bundle for the topology of Theorem 6.6.17 ⟦Chapter12/ContinuousOrientationSection · isContOrOn_univ_iff_continuous⟧. That topology exists ⟦Chapter6/OrientationMainClean · theorem_6_6_17⟧ and is unique ⟦Chapter6/OrientationSpace · eq_orTopology_of⟧, so the statement holds for every topology with these properties.

*Step 4, the opposite orientation.* $-o$ is again a model orientation ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isModelOrientation_otherOr⟧. It is invariant by ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · EpStrongBundle.isOrInvariant_otherOr⟧ and continuous by ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · isContOrOn_otherOr⟧.

**Proof of Proposition 18.5.6.** *(1).* From left to right, by Step 1 in the model of $c$. From right to left, by Step 2 ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.proposition_18_5_6_one_hyp⟧.

*(2).* Step 1 in both models ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.proposition_18_5_6_two_hyp⟧.

*(3)* ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.proposition_18_5_6_three_hyp⟧. From left to right: if $o = \bar\Gamma_c^* O$ and $o' = \bar\Gamma_{c'}^* O$, both are transports of one invariant section of $R$. The cocycle law gives that the transport of $o'$ to $c$ is $o$. From right to left: take $O$ as in Step 2, made from the transport of $o$ to $R$. Then $\bar\Gamma_c^* O$ is the transport of $o$ from $c$ to itself, which is $o$. And $\bar\Gamma_{c'}^* O$ is the transport of $o$ to $c'$. By hypothesis $o$ is the transport of $o'$ to $c$, so $\bar\Gamma_{c'}^* O$ is $o'$, since the two transports are inverse to each other. The verdict is "stronger" because (3) is proved as an equivalence.

**Proof of the two orientations** ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.exists_two_orientations⟧. Let $o$ be an orientation. The model orientation $\bar\Gamma_R^* o$ and its opposite give, by Step 2, two orientations of $f$. Since the transport of a section of $R$ to $R$ is itself, they are $\bar\Gamma_R^* o$ and its opposite, read at $q_\alpha$. They differ at every smooth $\alpha$ ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · otherOr_ne⟧.

**Proof of Remark 18.5.5.** *Necessity* ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.remark_18_5_5_mp_hyp⟧. By (1) in $R$, the reference model has a model orientation, which is an orientation in the sense of Definition 12.5.4 by Step 3. Proposition 12.5.6 (Part XII) then gives that the orbit map of $\sigma$ is $2:1$ and has a continuous section ⟦Chapter12/OrientationOrbit · EpStrongBundle.proposition_12_5_6_mp⟧.

*Sufficiency* ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.remark_18_5_5_mpr_hyp⟧. The given topology is the one of Theorem 6.6.17 ⟦Chapter6/OrientationSpace · eq_orTopology_of⟧. The converse of Proposition 12.5.6 ⟦Chapter12/OrientationFromSection · EpStrongBundle.proposition_12_5_6_mpr_hyp⟧ gives an orientation of the reference model in the sense of Definition 12.5.4, hence a model orientation by Step 3. Then (1) applies.

**Witnesses.** *The reflection bundle is orientable* ⟦Chapter18/OrientationWitness · ReflectionBundle.reflDiag_isOrientable⟧, by (1) in the single model ⟦Chapter17/StrongBundleStructuresExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflSingle⟧. The model orientation is ⟦Chapter18/OrientationWitness · ReflectionBundle.exists_isModelOrientation_reflDiag⟧. It is continuous by §XVIII.22 and invariant by ⟦Chapter18/OrientationWitness · ReflectionBundle.isModelInvariant_reflDiag⟧.

- The morphisms of the model with $g = -1$ form a connected sheet $\{(-1)\cdot q\}$ ⟦Chapter18/OrientationWitness · ReflectionBundle.reflMor⟧ ⟦Chapter18/OrientationWitness · ReflectionBundle.continuous_reflMor⟧ ⟦Chapter18/OrientationWitness · ReflectionBundle.eq_reflMor⟧.
- Invariance at a morphism is locally constant along such a sheet ⟦Chapter18/OrientationWitness · EpStrongBundle.eventually_isOrInvariantAt_iff⟧. This uses Theorem 12.5.3 for the push-forward ⟦Chapter12/OrientationMorphismContinuity · theorem_12_5_3_push_hyp⟧, the sheets and sc-paths. Invariance holds at each morphism exactly when it holds at its points ⟦Chapter18/OrientationWitness · EpStrongBundle.isOrInvariant_iff_forall_isOrInvariantAt⟧ ⟦Chapter18/OrientationWitness · EpStrongBundle.isOrInvariantAt_iff'⟧.
- Invariance holds at $q = 0$ because $f'(0)$ is bijective ⟦Chapter18/OrientationWitness · EpStrongBundle.isOrInvariantAt_of_bijective⟧.

*Two orientations* ⟦Chapter18/OrientationWitness · ReflectionBundle.reflDiag_exists_two_orientations⟧, with the earlier, conditional form ⟦Chapter18/OrientationsExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflDiag_two_orientations⟧. Every object of the reflection polyfold is smooth ⟦Chapter18/OrientationsExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflection_isSmoothObj⟧. The witness is weak in that $\partial C = \emptyset$ and the model is one chart.

### XVIII.24 The paragraph before Theorem 18.6.1 and Theorem 18.6.1 (2017: Theorem 18.8), pp. 860–861: the perturbation theorem

**Statement.** *Theorem 18.6.1* (explicit) ⟦Chapter18/Perturbation · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.theorem_18_6_1⟧. Let $C$ be tame and admit sc-smooth bump functions, and let $f$ be a sc-Fredholm section functor with tame germ data and compact $|S_f|$. Let $N$ be an auxiliary norm, $U \supset |S_f|$ an open set such that $(N, U)$ controls compactness, and $h : C \to (0, 1]$ a continuous functor ⟦Chapter18/Perturbation · PolyfoldConstruction.IsContinuousFunctionFunctor⟧. Then there is a structurable sc⁺-multisection functor $\Lambda$ with:

1. $N(\Lambda) < h$;
2. $|\mathrm{dom\text{-}supp}(\Lambda)| \subset U$;
3. $T(f, \Lambda)$ onto at the objects of $\mathrm{supp}(\Lambda \circ f)$ ⟦Chapter18/Perturbation · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsTransversalIn⟧;
4. the kernels in general position to $\partial C$ ⟦Chapter18/Perturbation · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsInGeneralPositionIn⟧.

Moreover $\Lambda \circ f$ is a compact, tame, branched ep⁺-subcategory ⟦Chapter18/Perturbation · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.theorem_18_6_1_in_particular⟧. Clauses (3) and (4) are read in a covering model $c$. The model-free form ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationModelIndependence · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.theorem_18_6_1_modelFree⟧ ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationModelIndependence · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.theorem_18_6_1_in_particular_modelFree⟧ reads them in some model, and so in every model ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationModelIndependence · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isTransversalFunctor_iff_forall⟧ ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationModelIndependence · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isInGeneralPositionFunctor_iff_forall⟧.

*The paragraph before the theorem* (explicit) ⟦Chapter18/Perturbation · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.perturbation_orbifoldType⟧ ⟦Chapter18/Perturbation · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.perturbation_manifoldType⟧. If $\Lambda \circ f$ is of orbifold type, $|\mathrm{supp}(\Lambda \circ f)|$ is compact, and in every covering model the support is an ep-subgroupoid and a smooth submanifold whose orbit space is homeomorphic to $|\mathrm{supp}(\Lambda \circ f)|$. If it is of manifold type, the support carries an M-polyfold structure in every model. The proof "in a smooth model" of the text is written out, together with the independence of the model; this settles point G.253.

**The readings and the added hypotheses.**

- *sc-Fredholm.* The theorem prints "sc-smooth section functor". The preamble of §18.6 says sc-Fredholm, and we read the theorem the same way (`hf`, ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsScFredholmSectionFunctor⟧).
- *Tame germ data* (`hfT`), the standing reading of Part IV, as in §XVIII.21.
- *Bump functions* (`hbump`): Definition 18.4.1 is read as bump functions on the object M-polyfold of some covering model, as in §XVIII.21.
- *The model-free form* needs `hF` for the reference model.

The witnesses are the diagonal section of the reflection bundle, which is weak in that $\partial C = \emptyset$, and the half-space bundle, with $\partial C \ne \emptyset$ (§XVIII.27):

- `hT` and `hbump`: ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstructionExamples · reflectionConstruction_isTame⟧, ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionFunctorsExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflection_admitsModelBumpFunctions⟧ and ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceWitnesses · HalfSpaceReflection.halfS_admitsModelBumpFunctions⟧.
- `hf` and `hfT`: ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctorsExamples · ReflectionBundle.isScFredholmSectionFunctorTame_reflDiag⟧ and ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceWitnesses · HalfSpaceReflection.isScFredholmSectionFunctorTame_halfF⟧.
- `hF`: ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceWitnesses · HalfSpaceReflection.halfQ_hasStrongBundleUniformizers⟧.

**Proof in the model** ⟦Chapter18/Perturbation · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.theorem_18_6_1_existence⟧. *Step 0, the data in the model of $c$.*

- The model ep-groupoid is tame (Part XVII).
- Its object M-polyfold admits bump functions, because the bump functions of `hbump` pass from their model to the model of $c$ ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionConstructions · PolyfoldConstruction.admitsBumpFunctionsPointwise_model_iff⟧.
- The model section is sc-Fredholm with tame germ data ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.modelFred⟧ ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.modelFredTame⟧.
- Its zero set has compact orbit image iff $|S_f|$ is compact ⟦Chapter18/ModelTransfer · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isCompact_zeroSet_iff⟧.
- $N$ pulls back to an auxiliary norm of the model.
- $(N\circ\bar\Gamma, |\Gamma|^{-1}(U))$ controls compactness for the model section ⟦Chapter18/Perturbation · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.controlsCompactness_model⟧. The orbit map of the model is a homeomorphism, the solution set of the model lies over $S_f$, and the closure of the relevant image is compact iff its image in $|C|$ is.
- $h\circ\Gamma$ is continuous and invariant ⟦Chapter18/Perturbation · PolyfoldConstruction.IsContinuousFunctionFunctor.continuous_model⟧ ⟦Chapter18/Perturbation · PolyfoldConstruction.IsContinuousFunctionFunctor.mor_model⟧.

*Step 1, Theorem 15.3.7 in the model.* Part XV's existence theorem ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionExistence · theorem_15_3_7_existence⟧ gives a sc⁺-multisection $\Lambda_m$ of the model. It has $N(\Lambda_m) < h\circ\Gamma$, domain support over $U$, and the model section transversal to it and in general position.

*Step 2, the push-forward.* $\Lambda_m$ is constant on isomorphism classes. Its push-forward $\Lambda = \bar\Gamma_*\Lambda_m$ ⟦Chapter18/ModelTransfer · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.bundlePush⟧ is a functor on $E$ with $\bar\Gamma^*\Lambda = \Lambda_m$ ⟦Chapter18/ModelTransfer · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.bundlePull_bundlePush⟧ ⟦Chapter18/ModelTransfer · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isMorInvariant_bundlePush⟧.

*Step 3, (1) and (2)* ⟦Chapter18/Perturbation · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.exists_push_of_model⟧.

- For (1): every $\alpha$ is isomorphic to some $\Gamma x$, because the model functor is essentially surjective. Both $N(\Lambda)$ and $h$ are invariant, and $N(\Lambda)(\Gamma x)$ is the model norm of $\Lambda_m$ at $x$ ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.pointwiseNorm_bundlePull⟧.
- For (2): a point of $|\mathrm{dom\text{-}supp}(\Lambda)|$ is the orbit of a point of the model. The preimage of $|\mathrm{dom\text{-}supp}(\Lambda)|$ lies in the domain support of $\Lambda_m$ ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionConstructions · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.preimage_orbitDomSupp_subset⟧.

*Step 4, (3) and (4).* Rewrite $\bar\Gamma^*\Lambda = \Lambda_m$.

**Proof of the structurable form and of the "in particular".** *Structurable* ⟦Chapter18/Perturbation · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.theorem_18_6_1_structurable⟧. The orbit space of a covering model is paracompact ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNorms · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.hasParacompactOrbitSpace_model⟧. So Part XV's version with a good system ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionGoodSystem · theorem_15_3_7_of_paracompact⟧ gives a structurable $\Lambda_m$, and Step 3 pushes it forward to a structurable sc⁺-multisection functor (Definition 18.3.3).

*In particular* ⟦Chapter18/Perturbation · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.theorem_18_6_1_in_particular⟧. Let $\Lambda$ be a functor with $N(\Lambda) \le 1$, domain support over $U$, a sc⁺-multisection in the model of $c$, and in general position there.

1. The model norm of $\bar\Gamma^*\Lambda$ is at most $1$, and its domain support lies over $|\mathrm{dom\text{-}supp}(\Lambda)|$ ⟦Chapter18/Perturbation · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.domSupp_model_subset⟧.
2. Part XV's "in particular", with Remark 15.3.6 for the tame form ⟦Chapter15/TameBranched · theorem_15_3_7_in_particular_tame_hyp⟧, shows that $\bar\Gamma^*\Lambda\circ f^{\bar\Psi}$ is a compact, tame, branched ep⁺-subgroupoid of the model.
3. That function is the pull-back $\Gamma^*(\Lambda \circ f)$ ⟦Chapter18/Perturbation · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.modelPull_comp⟧, and $\Lambda \circ f$ is a functor ⟦Chapter18/Perturbation · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isFunctorial_comp_sectionFunctor⟧.
4. Definition 17.5.1 with its covering form ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · definition_17_5_1_covering⟧, the comparison of branched ep⁺-subgroupoids with the model ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · PolyfoldConstruction.isBranchedEpSubgroupoid_modelPull_iff⟧, of compactness ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · PolyfoldConstruction.isCompactBranched_iff_model⟧, and tameness in one model ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSubcategory · PolyfoldConstruction.IsTameModel⟧ carry the three properties to $C$.

*All together* ⟦Chapter18/Perturbation · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.theorem_18_6_1⟧. A structurable functor is a sc⁺-multisection functor ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.remark_18_3_4⟧, and $N(\Lambda) < h \le 1$. So the "in particular" applies to the $\Lambda$ of the structurable form.

**Proof of the independence of the model.** *Step 1, along an inclusion.* For an inclusion $c \subset d$, the pull-back multisection of $c$ is the pull-back of that of $d$ along the strong bundle equivalence $W_c \to W_d$ ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationModelIndependence · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.pullMult_bundleIncl_bundlePull⟧.
