Part XV's invariance of transversality and of general position under pull-back by equivalences ⟦Chapter15/TransversalityEquivalence · isTransversal_pullback_iff⟧ ⟦Chapter15/GeneralPositionEquivalence · isInGeneralPosition_pullback_iff⟧ gives (3) and (4) in $c$ iff in $d$ ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationModelIndependence · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isTransversalIn_incl_iff⟧ ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationModelIndependence · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isInGeneralPositionIn_incl_iff⟧.

*Step 2, two models.* For two covering families $c$ and $c'$, pass through $c \sqcup c'$. A sc⁺-multisection functor is one in every model ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.remark_18_3_2⟧. So (3) and (4) in $c$ are equivalent to (3) and (4) in $c'$ ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationModelIndependence · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isTransversalIn_iff⟧ ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationModelIndependence · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isInGeneralPositionIn_iff⟧.

*Step 3, the model-free statements.* (3) and (4) "in some model" ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationModelIndependence · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsTransversalFunctor⟧ ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationModelIndependence · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsInGeneralPositionFunctor⟧ are equivalent to (3) and (4) in any given model ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationModelIndependence · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isTransversalFunctor_iff⟧ ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationModelIndependence · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isInGeneralPositionFunctor_iff⟧. The model-free theorem is the theorem in the reference model ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationModelIndependence · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.theorem_18_6_1_modelFree⟧. Its "in particular" takes the model in which (4) is given ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationModelIndependence · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.theorem_18_6_1_in_particular_modelFree⟧.

**Proof of the paragraph before the theorem.** *Orbifold type* ⟦Chapter18/Perturbation · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.perturbation_orbifoldType⟧: the "in particular", then Proposition 17.5.5 (2) (Part XVII) ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSupport · PolyfoldConstruction.proposition_17_5_5_two⟧ in every covering model of the base. *Manifold type* ⟦Chapter18/Perturbation · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.perturbation_manifoldType⟧: the "in particular", then Proposition 17.5.5 (1) ⟦Chapter17/BranchedSupport · PolyfoldConstruction.proposition_17_5_5_one⟧. The oriented case is §XVIII.25.

**Witnesses.** *Without boundary.* The reflection bundle in the model of two charts ⟦Chapter17/StrongBundleStructuresExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflPair⟧ with $h = 1$ ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationExamples · ReflectionBundle.theorem_18_6_1_witness⟧. The model-free form ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationModelIndependenceExamples · ReflectionBundle.theorem_18_6_1_modelFree_witness⟧. $h = 1$ is a continuous functor ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflection_isContinuousFunctionFunctor_one⟧. These are weak: $\partial C = \emptyset$.

*With boundary.* The half-space bundle ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceWitnesses · HalfSpaceReflection.theorem_18_6_1_witness_boundary⟧ ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceWitnesses · HalfSpaceReflection.theorem_18_6_1_modelFree_witness_boundary⟧, with $h = 1$ ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceWitnesses · HalfSpaceReflection.halfS_isContinuousFunctionFunctor_one⟧. Here $\partial C \ne \emptyset$, and a zero of $f$ lies on the boundary.

### XVIII.25 Theorem 18.6.1, the orientation clause, p. 861

**Statement.** *The last sentence of Theorem 18.6.1* (explicit) ⟦Chapter18/Perturbation · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.theorem_18_6_1_oriented⟧. Let $o$ be an orientation of $f$ and $\Lambda$ as in the "in particular" of §XVIII.24. Let the Fredholm index be $n$ at the points of the support. Then $\Lambda \circ f$ carries an oriented ep⁺-subcategory $O$ (Part XVII, ⟦Chapter17/CategoryStokes · PolyfoldConstruction.OrientedSubcategory⟧) with $O.\Theta = \Lambda \circ f$. On the smooth points of the model of $c$, its orientation is the canonical orientation of Part XV determined by $\bar\Gamma^* o$ ⟦Chapter15/CanonicalOrientation · canonicalOrientation⟧, and that orientation is canonical for index $n$ ⟦Chapter15/CanonicalOrientation · IsCanonicalFor⟧. The model-free form ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationModelIndependence · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.theorem_18_6_1_oriented_modelFree⟧ takes $\Lambda$ a sc⁺-multisection functor and (4) in any model, and describes the orientation in every covering model $c$.

**The readings and the added hypotheses.**

- *The index* (`hidx`). It is tacit in the text through Theorem 15.4.3, and it is a hypothesis as in Part XV: the model section has index $n$ at every point $x$ with $\bar\Gamma^*\Lambda(f^{\bar\Psi}(x)) > 0$. In the model-free form `hidx` stays in the model $c$ in which the orientation is described. Its witness is the single model of the reflection bundle, where the index is $0$ ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationOrientedWitness · ReflectionBundle.isIndexAt_reflSingleModel⟧ (the tangent space and the kernel computed in ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationOrientedWitness · ReflectionBundle.finrank_T_reflSingleChartPt⟧ ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationOrientedWitness · ReflectionBundle.finrank_K_reflSingleChartPt⟧). This witness is weak: $\partial C = \emptyset$.
- *The §18.5 hypotheses* `hT`, `hbump`, `hf`, `hfT`, `hF`, with their witness as in §XVIII.21.

**Proof.** *Step 1, branched.* By the "in particular" of §XVIII.24, $\Lambda \circ f$ is a branched ep⁺-subcategory.

*Step 2, the orientation in the model.* $\bar\Gamma_c^* o$ is a model orientation (§XVIII.23) ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isModelOrientation_pullCat⟧. Part XII's orientation topology of the model has the properties of Theorem 6.6.17 ⟦Chapter18/Perturbation · SBLocalization.isOrientationTopology_orTopology⟧. So $\bar\Gamma_c^* o$ is an orientation in the sense of Definition 12.5.4 ⟦Chapter18/Orientations · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isModelOrientation_iff⟧ ⟦Chapter12/OrientationOrbit · EpStrongBundle.definition_12_5_4⟧.

*Step 3, good position.* At every point of $\mathrm{supp}(\bar\Gamma^*\Lambda \circ f^{\bar\Psi})$, general position (4) with tame germ data gives good position, by Remark 15.3.6 ⟦Chapter15/GoodPositionBranches · remark_15_3_6_goodPosition_hyp⟧. The index hypothesis is restated on that support.

*Step 4, Theorem 15.4.1 in the model.* Part XV's canonical orientation, for an orientation in the sense of Definition 12.5.4 ⟦Chapter15/CanonicalOrientationOrientedSection · theorem_15_4_1_of_definition_12_5_4⟧, gives an orientation of the branched ep⁺-subgroupoid $\bar\Gamma^*\Lambda\circ f^{\bar\Psi}$ of the model. By ⟦Chapter18/Perturbation · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.modelPull_comp⟧ that subgroupoid is $\Gamma^*(\Lambda \circ f)$, and the orientation is canonical for index $n$.

*Step 5, to $C$.* Restrict the orientation to its smooth part. An oriented ep⁺-subcategory is built from an oriented branched ep⁺-subgroupoid of one model ⟦Chapter17/CategoryStokes · PolyfoldConstruction.OrientedSubcategory.ofModel⟧. Its $\Theta$ is $\Lambda \circ f$ by construction. On the smooth points of the model of $c$, its orientation is the smooth part of the canonical orientation, which is the canonical orientation there.

*The model-free form* ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationModelIndependence · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.theorem_18_6_1_oriented_modelFree⟧. The sc⁺-multisection functor is one in the model of $c$ ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.remark_18_3_2⟧. (4) in some model is (4) in the model of $c$ (§XVIII.24, Step 3 of the independence). Then apply the theorem in $c$.

**Witnesses.** *Existence* ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationOrientedWitness · ReflectionBundle.theorem_18_6_1_oriented_witness⟧: $\Lambda$ is taken from the structurable form of §XVIII.24 in the single model of the reflection bundle, with the orientation of §XVIII.23 and index $0$.

*A non-empty support* ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationOrientedSupport · ReflectionBundle.theorem_18_6_1_oriented_witness_support⟧. Take for $\Lambda$ the zero multisection functor ⟦Chapter18/ImplicitFunctionTheorem · VectorBundle.zeroFunctor⟧. Then $\Theta = \Lambda \circ f$ has support $\{0\}$ and $\Theta(0) = 1$.

- It is in general position in the model ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationOrientedSupport · ReflectionBundle.isInGeneralPositionIn_reflDiag_zeroFunctor⟧, by the chart form ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationOrientedSupport · SBLocalization.isInGeneralPositionAt_zeroMultisection_of_chart⟧.
- It is positive exactly at $0$ ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationOrientedSupport · ReflectionBundle.zeroFunctor_reflDiag_pos_iff⟧.
- Its domain support is computed in ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationOrientedSupport · ReflectionBundle.orbitDomSupp_reflV_zeroFunctor⟧, its norm is $0$ ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationOrientedSupport · ReflectionBundle.pointwiseNormFunctor_reflV_zeroFunctor_eq_zero⟧, and it is a sc⁺-multisection in the model ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationOrientedSupport · ReflectionBundle.isScPlusMultisectionIn_reflV_zeroFunctor⟧.

Both witnesses have $\partial C = \emptyset$. The half-space bundle of §XVIII.27 has no orientable section functor, so no witness of this clause has $\partial C \ne \emptyset$.

### XVIII.26 Theorem 15.3.9 in the polyfold context (2017: §18.6), p. 861

**Statement.** *The sentence after Theorem 18.6.1* (explicit) ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationModelIndependence · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.theorem_15_3_9_categorical⟧. Let $C$ be tame with partitions of unity on the object M-polyfold of some covering model. Let $f$ be sc-Fredholm with tame germ data and compact $|S_f|$, $N$ an auxiliary norm, and $(N, U)$ controlling compactness. Let $\Lambda : E|\partial C \to \mathbb Q^+$ be structurable ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationModelIndependence · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsBoundaryStructurableFunctor⟧, with $|\mathrm{dom\text{-}supp}(\Lambda|\partial C)| \subset U$ and $N(\Lambda) \le 1$ on $\partial C$. Let $h : C \to (0, 1]$ be a continuous functor with $N(\Lambda) < h$ on $\partial C$, and let $T(f|\partial C, \Lambda)$ be surjective with respect to the intersections of local faces ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationModelIndependence · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsBoundaryFaceOntoFunctor⟧. Then there is a structurable sc⁺-multisection functor $\Lambda'$ with:

1. $\Lambda' = \Lambda$ over $\partial C$;
2. $|\mathrm{dom\text{-}supp}(\Lambda')| \subset U$ and $N(\Lambda') < h$;
3. $T(f, \Lambda')$ onto on $\mathrm{supp}(\Lambda' \circ f)$, in some model and so in every model.

Moreover $\Lambda' \circ f$ is a compact branched ep⁺-subcategory. As in Part XV, the "moreover tame" of Theorem 15.3.9 is not concluded. With that exception this settles point G.254. The two exercises after it (Theorems 5.3.12 and 5.3.15 for ep-groupoids and polyfolds) are not formalized (point G.255).

**The readings and the added hypotheses.**

- *Partitions of unity* (`hPU`, ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationModelIndependence · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.HasModelMPolyfoldPartitionsOfUnity⟧). We read "admits sc-smooth partitions of unity", as Theorem 14.1.9 (Part XIV) uses it, as partitions of unity (Definition 5.5.1) on the object M-polyfold of some covering model. Part III does not compare the M-polyfold notion between two models, so one model is named. Witnesses: ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationModelIndependenceExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflQ_hasModelMPolyfoldPartitionsOfUnity⟧ (weak: $\partial C = \emptyset$) and ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceWitnesses · HalfSpaceReflection.halfQ_hasModelMPolyfoldPartitionsOfUnity⟧ ($\partial C \ne \emptyset$).
- *Tame germ data* (`hfT`) and sc-Fredholm (`hf`), as in §XVIII.24.
- *Theorem 15.3.9's own hypotheses read as functors.*
  - Boundary structurability is read in some model (Definition 14.1.7, ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationModelIndependence · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsBoundaryStructurableIn⟧); it does not depend on the model ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationModelIndependence · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isBoundaryStructurableFunctor_iff⟧.
  - The surjectivity on faces is read in some model as Part XV's form ⟦Chapter15/TransversalExtension · IsBoundaryFaceOnto⟧; it does not depend on the model ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationModelIndependence · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isBoundaryFaceOntoFunctor_iff⟧.

**Proof of the independence of the model.** *Boundary structurability* ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationModelIndependence · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsBoundaryStructurableIn.of_comparison⟧. The pull-back of $\Lambda$ to one model is the pull-back of its pull-back to another along the comparison $\bar f$ of Theorem 17.1.18. Proposition 14.1.8 (Part XIV), at the class level ⟦Chapter14/FacePush · GenSBMap.proposition_14_1_8_pullMult⟧, carries boundary structurability along it.

*Surjectivity on faces.* Along a strong bundle equivalence, the face condition at a point holds iff it holds at the image ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationModelIndependence · SBLocalization.isBoundaryFaceOnto_pullback_iff⟧. The proof uses the push and pull of multisections, the reduced-onto condition at a level and its invariance along morphisms, and Lemma 11.5.2 ⟦Chapter11/MultisectionLocalStructure · lemma_11_5_2_book⟧. Then:

- the model section pulls back along the inclusion ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationModelIndependence · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.bundlePull_modelSection_eq⟧;
- the boundary of the model lies over $\partial C$ ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationModelIndependence · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.mem_boundary_model_iff_obj⟧;
- these give the inclusion form ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationModelIndependence · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isBoundaryFaceOnto_incl_iff⟧, the form for two models through their sum ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationModelIndependence · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isBoundaryFaceOntoIn_iff⟧, and the functor form.

**Proof of the theorem** ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationModelIndependence · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.theorem_15_3_9_categorical⟧. *Step 1, the model.* Take the covering family $c$ given by `hPU`. Move boundary structurability and the face condition to the model of $c$. Rewrite the domain support over $U$, $N(\Lambda) \le 1$ and $N(\Lambda) < h$ on $\partial C$ in the model: a point of the boundary of the model lies over $\partial C$, and the model norm is the norm of $C$ where it is finite.

*Step 2, Theorem 15.3.9 in the model.* Part XV's transversal extension theorem ⟦Chapter15/RelativeLocalFamilies · theorem_15_3_9⟧ applies with the tame model, its paracompact orbit space, the partitions of unity, the sc-Fredholm model section with tame germ data, compactness, and the transferred norm and control of compactness (§XVIII.24, Step 0). It gives a structurable $\Lambda_m$ equal to $\bar\Gamma^*\Lambda$ over the boundary, with domain support over $U$, $N(\Lambda_m) < h \circ \Gamma$, transversal, and $\Lambda_m \circ f^{\bar\Psi}$ a compact branched ep⁺-subgroupoid.

*Step 3, the push-forward.* §XVIII.24, Step 3 gives a functor $\Lambda'$ with $\bar\Gamma^*\Lambda' = \Lambda_m$, $N(\Lambda') < h$ and domain support in $U$ ⟦Chapter18/Perturbation · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.exists_push_of_model⟧. $\Lambda'$ is structurable because $\Lambda_m$ is, and transversal in $c$.

*Step 4, agreement over $\partial C$.* Let $e$ be a point of $E$ over $\partial C$. Then $e$ is isomorphic to the image of a point $w$ of the model, because the orbit map of the bundle model is a homeomorphism ⟦Chapter17/BundleModel · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.isHomeomorph_bundleModelOrbitMap⟧. The base point of $w$ lies on the boundary of the model, since the boundary is invariant. Both $\Lambda'$ and $\Lambda$ are functors, so $\Lambda'(e) = \Lambda_m(w) = \bar\Gamma^*\Lambda(w) = \Lambda(e)$.

*Step 5, branched and compact.* As in the "in particular" of §XVIII.24 (Steps 3–4), through ⟦Chapter18/Perturbation · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.modelPull_comp⟧.

*The ∂-extension theorem free of the model*, used on the way, is ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationModelIndependence · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.theorem_18_4_4_modelFree⟧: Theorem 18.4.4 ⟦Chapter18/MultisectionConstructions · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.theorem_18_4_4⟧ in the model with partitions of unity.

**Witnesses.** *Without boundary* ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationModelIndependenceExamples · ReflectionBundle.theorem_15_3_9_categorical_witness⟧, with a boundary-structurable functor ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationModelIndependenceExamples · ReflectionBundle.exists_isBoundaryStructurableFunctor⟧. This is weak: $\partial C = \emptyset$, so the boundary hypotheses are empty.

*With boundary, the zero functor* ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceWitnesses · HalfSpaceReflection.theorem_15_3_9_categorical_witness_boundary⟧. It uses ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceWitnesses · HalfSpaceReflection.isBoundaryStructurableFunctor_zeroFunctor⟧, ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceWitnesses · HalfSpaceReflection.orbitBoundaryDomSupp_zeroFunctor⟧, and the face condition ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceWitnesses · HalfSpaceReflection.isBoundaryFaceOntoFunctor_halfF⟧ (from ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceWitnesses · HalfSpaceReflection.isBoundaryFaceOnto_model⟧).

*With boundary, a constant datum* ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceBoundaryData · HalfSpaceReflection.theorem_15_3_9_categorical_witness_constDatum⟧. The datum is the constant multisection $v = -3/4$ ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceBoundaryData · HalfSpaceReflection.constFunctor⟧, with:

- boundary structurability ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceBoundaryData · HalfSpaceReflection.isBoundaryStructurableFunctor_constFunctor⟧;
- the corner in its domain support ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceBoundaryData · HalfSpaceReflection.corner_mem_orbitBoundaryDomSupp⟧;
- the face condition ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceBoundaryData · HalfSpaceReflection.isBoundaryFaceOntoFunctor_constFunctor⟧;
- an auxiliary norm with $N(\Lambda_v) = 3/4$ ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceBoundaryData · HalfSpaceReflection.halfNorm⟧ ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceBoundaryData · HalfSpaceReflection.isAuxNormFunctor_halfNorm⟧, which controls compactness ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceBoundaryData · HalfSpaceReflection.halfF_controlsCompactness_halfNorm⟧.

The boundary datum is non-zero and its domain support meets the corner. The ∂-extension theorem has the matching witnesses ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationModelIndependenceExamples · ReflectionBundle.theorem_18_4_4_modelFree_witness⟧ ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceWitnesses · HalfSpaceReflection.theorem_18_4_4_modelFree_witness_boundary⟧ ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceBoundaryData · HalfSpaceReflection.theorem_18_4_4_modelFree_witness_constDatum⟧.

### XVIII.27 The witness bundles

**Statement.** *The two witness bundles of §§18.1–18.6* (extra). They are not in the text; they serve as witnesses for the hypotheses of the chapter.

**The reflection bundle** ⟦Chapter17/StrongBundleStructuresExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflQ⟧. The base is the translation groupoid $\mathbb Z/2 \ltimes \mathbb R$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstructionExamples · reflectionConstruction⟧, and the fibre is $\mathbb R$, with $-1$ acting by $v \mapsto -v$ ⟦Chapter17/StrongBundleStructuresExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflV⟧. The section is the diagonal $x \mapsto (x, x)$ ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctorsExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflDiag⟧, sc-Fredholm ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctorsExamples · ReflectionBundle.isScFredholmSectionFunctor_reflDiag⟧ and vanishing only at $0$ ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctorsExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflDiag_nondegenerate⟧. Every point has a strong bundle uniformizer ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctorsExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflQ_hasStrongBundleUniformizers⟧. The models are one chart ⟦Chapter17/StrongBundleStructuresExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflSingle⟧ or two ⟦Chapter17/StrongBundleStructuresExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflPair⟧. $\partial C = \emptyset$. The bundle is orientable (§XVIII.23), and its isotropy at $0$ acts non-trivially on the fibre and trivially on orientations (§XVIII.21).

**The half-space bundle** ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceBundleConstruction · HalfSpaceReflection.halfQ⟧. The base is $\mathbb Z/2 \ltimes ([0, \infty) \times \mathbb R)$ ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceBundleConstruction · HalfSpaceReflection.halfS⟧, tame ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceBundleConstruction · HalfSpaceReflection.halfS_isTame⟧, with $-1$ acting by $(a, y) \mapsto (a, -y)$. The fibre is $\mathbb R$ with the trivial action ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceBundleConstruction · HalfSpaceReflection.halfV⟧. Its uniformizers and charts are ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceBundleConstruction · HalfSpaceReflection.halfUnif⟧ ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceBundleConstruction · HalfSpaceReflection.halfSingle⟧. The section is $f(a, y) = a^2 + y^2 - 1$ ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceWitnesses · HalfSpaceReflection.halfF⟧, sc-Fredholm ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceWitnesses · HalfSpaceReflection.isScFredholmSectionFunctor_halfF⟧, with compact moduli space ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceWitnesses · HalfSpaceReflection.halfF_hasCompactModuliSpace⟧. $\partial C \ne \emptyset$ ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceBundleConstruction · HalfSpaceReflection.mem_boundarySet_iff⟧.

- The corner $(0, 0)$ lies on the boundary ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceBundleConstruction · HalfSpaceReflection.corner_mem_boundarySet⟧ and has isotropy $\mathbb Z/2$ ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceBundleConstruction · HalfSpaceReflection.exists_ne_unit_corner⟧.
- The zero set is the half circle $\{a^2 + y^2 = 1,\ a \ge 0\}$ ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceWitnesses · HalfSpaceReflection.halfF_zeroSet⟧, and the zero $(0, 1)$ lies on the boundary ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceWitnesses · HalfSpaceReflection.topPoint_mem_zeroSet_boundarySet⟧.

At $(a, 0)$ the element $-1$ acts with determinant $-1$ on the tangent space $\mathbb R^2$ and trivially on the fibre ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceBundleConstruction · HalfSpaceReflection.neg_one_smul_of_snd_eq_zero⟧. So at these points the isotropy reverses both orientations of $\mathrm{DET}(f, (a, 0))$ for every sc-Fredholm section functor, and by the obstruction of §XVIII.21 the half-space bundle has no orientable section functor. This step is argued in the documentation of the witness files and is not stated as a Lean theorem. It is why the orientation clause (§XVIII.25) has no witness with $\partial C \ne \emptyset$.

### XVIII.28 Witnesses and the hypotheses

**Standing readings, with their witnesses.** These are not added hypotheses but the readings of the text the chapter rests on; each is witnessed.

- *$\bar F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$* ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.HasStrongBundleUniformizers⟧, where the text uses a strong bundle uniformizer at every object: Remark 18.1.2 (c) "⇒" (§XVIII.2), Definition 18.1.3 (§XVIII.3), Theorems 18.1.5, 18.1.12 (1), 18.2.2, 18.2.6, Remark 18.2.5 and the model-free forms of §18.6. Witnesses: the reflection bundle ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctorsExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflQ_hasStrongBundleUniformizers⟧ and the half-space bundle ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceWitnesses · HalfSpaceReflection.halfQ_hasStrongBundleUniformizers⟧.
- *Tame germ data* ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctors · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.IsScFredholmSectionFunctorTame⟧, the standing reading of Chapters 3–15, wherever a theorem of Chapter 15 or Theorem 6.6.17 is invoked: Theorem 18.1.5 (§XVIII.8), §18.5, Theorem 18.6.1 and Theorem 15.3.9 in the polyfold context (§§XVIII.24–XVIII.26). Witnesses: the diagonal section of the reflection bundle ⟦Chapter18/FredholmSectionFunctorsExamples · ReflectionBundle.isScFredholmSectionFunctorTame_reflDiag⟧, weak: $\partial C = \emptyset$; the section $a^2 + y^2 - 1$ of the half-space bundle ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceWitnesses · HalfSpaceReflection.isScFredholmSectionFunctorTame_halfF⟧, with boundary.
- *Sc-Fredholm in Theorem 18.6.1*, from the preamble of §18.6 (§XVIII.24); the witnesses are those of tame germ data.
- *Auxiliary norms with the continuity of Definition 12.2.1* (§XVIII.9). Witnesses: auxiliary norms exist on every strong polyfold bundle with a paracompact model (Theorem 18.1.12 (1), §XVIII.11); on the half-space bundle a concrete one ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceBoundaryData · HalfSpaceReflection.isAuxNormFunctor_halfNorm⟧.

**Hypotheses taken, by section.** Each is named, is not derived from the text unless said, and has a witness.

- *§XVIII.4.* The global form of Definition 18.1.4 takes transported sc⁺-sections ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationSet · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.HasTransportedScPlusSections⟧, needed only because the sections there are taken on all of $O$; the local form, the text's, takes none. Witness: the reflection bundle ⟦Chapter18/LinearizationSetExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflQ_hasTransportedScPlusSections⟧, where the transported section is the conjugate by the isotropy.
- *§§XVIII.10, XVIII.11, XVIII.13, XVIII.14.* Reflexive $(0,1)$-fibres and reflexive local compactness, where the text takes them (Definitions 18.1.9, 18.2.4). Witnesses: ⟦Chapter18/CompactnessExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflQ_hasReflexiveFibers⟧, ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceWitnesses · HalfSpaceReflection.halfQ_hasReflexiveFibers⟧, ⟦Chapter18/CompactnessExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflDiag_hasReflexiveLocalCompactness⟧, ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceWitnesses · HalfSpaceReflection.halfF_hasReflexiveLocalCompactness⟧; the reflexive pieces of a model, which the paragraph after Definition 18.1.9 asserts and which is derived from reflexive fibres in one model (§XVIII.10), on two copies of the reflection chart ⟦Chapter18/AuxiliaryNormsDirectExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflPair_hasReflexivePieces⟧. All fibres of the examples are finite-dimensional, so reflexivity is automatic there.
- *§§XVIII.20, XVIII.26.* Sc-smooth partitions of unity on the object M-polyfold of a model ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationModelIndependence · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.HasModelMPolyfoldPartitionsOfUnity⟧, Definition 18.4.1 in the sense in which Theorem 14.1.9 uses it. Witnesses: ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationModelIndependenceExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflQ_hasModelMPolyfoldPartitionsOfUnity⟧, weak: $\partial C = \emptyset$; ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceWitnesses · HalfSpaceReflection.halfQ_hasModelMPolyfoldPartitionsOfUnity⟧, with boundary. The boundary datum of Theorem 18.4.4 is witnessed with non-empty domain support, the closure of $|\partial C|$ through the corner with isotropy $\mathbb Z/2$ ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceBoundaryData · HalfSpaceReflection.theorem_18_4_4_modelFree_witness_constDatum⟧.
- *§§XVIII.21–XVIII.23.* A tame base with sc-smooth bump functions in the model ($hT$, $hbump$), as Theorem 6.6.17 takes them. Witness: the diagonal section of the reflection bundle, whose isotropy $-1$ acts non-trivially on the fibre ⟦Chapter18/OrientationsExamples · ReflectionBundle.reflDiag_orientation_hyp⟧, weak: $\partial C = \emptyset$. On the half-space bundle both hold ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceBundleConstruction · HalfSpaceReflection.halfS_isTame⟧ ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceWitnesses · HalfSpaceReflection.halfS_admitsModelBumpFunctions⟧, but it carries no orientable section functor (§XVIII.27). Remark 18.5.5 takes a topology on the orientation category of the reference model with the properties of Theorem 6.6.17 (§XVIII.23).
- *§XVIII.25.* The Fredholm index on the support ($hidx$), tacit in the text through Theorem 15.4.3. Witness: the single model of the reflection bundle ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationOrientedWitness · ReflectionBundle.isIndexAt_reflSingleModel⟧, weak: $\partial C = \emptyset$; the oriented conclusion with non-empty support $\{0\}$ and $\Theta(0) = 1$ ⟦Chapter18/PerturbationOrientedSupport · ReflectionBundle.theorem_18_6_1_oriented_witness_support⟧.

Theorem 18.1.5 has its hypotheses witnessed at an interior zero ⟦Chapter18/ImplicitFunctionTheoremExamples · ReflectionBundle.theorem_18_1_5_hyp_witness⟧, weak: $\partial C = \emptyset$, and at the boundary zero $(0, 1)$ of the half-space bundle ⟦Chapter18/GoodPositionIntrinsicExamples · HalfSpaceReflection.theorem_18_1_5_intrinsic_hyp_witness⟧. Every hypothesis of the theorems of §§18.1–18.4 and 18.6 is witnessed with $\partial C \ne \emptyset$ by the half-space bundle (Theorem 18.1.12 (3) ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceBoundaryData · HalfSpaceReflection.theorem_18_1_12_three_witness_boundary⟧, Theorem 18.2.6 ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceWitnesses · HalfSpaceReflection.theorem_18_2_6_witness_boundary⟧, Theorem 18.6.1 ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceWitnesses · HalfSpaceReflection.theorem_18_6_1_witness_boundary⟧, Theorem 15.3.9 in the polyfold context ⟦Chapter18/HalfSpaceWitnesses · HalfSpaceReflection.theorem_15_3_9_categorical_witness_boundary⟧), except the orientation clause of Theorem 18.6.1. No hypothesis of this Part quantifies over all charts or all uniformizers, and none is unsatisfiable: each has a witness above, weak where said.

**Readings, not hypotheses.** $N : C \to [0, \infty]$ and $|C_{0,1}|$ read on $E$ (§§XVIII.9, XVIII.13, XVIII.14); functors to $[0, \infty]$ and $\mathbb Q^+$ as invariant functions (§XVIII.1); "$f(\alpha) \in E_{m,m+1}$" in a strong bundle uniformizer (§XVIII.2); $s$ local near $q_0$ and "$f_{\bar\Psi} - s$" with $s(q_0) = f_{\bar\Psi}(q_0)$ in Definition 18.1.4 (§XVIII.4); good position to the boundary through the partial cone of Remark 17.2.18 (§XVIII.7); "tame orbifold-type ep⁺-subcategory" through local branching structures (§XVIII.8); the proper covering functor of Theorem 18.3.6 through models (§XVIII.17); bump functions and partitions of unity in the ep-groupoid sense in Definition 18.4.1, and on the object M-polyfold where Theorem 14.1.9 is used (§§XVIII.18, XVIII.20); $\bar\Gamma^* o$ as $\bar\Psi^* o$ and $\Psi(\bar o) = \alpha$ as $\alpha_z$ in Definition 18.5.2 (§XVIII.22); $|\sigma|$ in Remark 18.5.5 through the reference model (§XVIII.23).

**Every definition has a witness.** *Section functors and Definition 18.1.1*: the diagonal section of the reflection bundle and the section $a^2 + y^2 - 1$ of the half-space bundle (§§XVIII.2, XVIII.27). *$C_U$*: on the reflection construction (§XVIII.6). *The partial cone and good position*: at the corner of the half-space base (§XVIII.7). *Auxiliary norms*, in both forms, reflexive and over the boundary (§§XVIII.9–XVIII.11). *Compact moduli space, proper, proper$_{<\infty}$, controlling and strongly controlling compactness, reflexive local compactness* (§§XVIII.12–XVIII.14). *Sc⁺-multisection functors and structurability*, with sums, products, the pull-back by the identity covering and the atomic functors of Proposition 18.4.3 (§§XVIII.15–XVIII.19); *over the boundary*, the constant datum of the half-space bundle (§XVIII.20). *Orientations*: the two orientations of the diagonal section of the reflection bundle (§XVIII.23). *Transversal and general-position perturbations*: the witnesses of Theorem 18.6.1 (§XVIII.24).

### XVIII.29 What is not formalized

- **G.245, settled: a solution object is smooth** (p. 849); §XVIII.12 above, through Remark 18.1.2 (b) in the levels ⟦Chapter18/SolutionRegularity · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.zeroSet_subset_isSmoothObj⟧.
- **G.256, settled: the category $\widehat{Gr}_F(C)$** (p. 856) of oriented formal sums: the category of elements ⟦Chapter18/OrientationCategory · GCT.VectorBundle.GrFHatCat⟧ of the functor $\alpha \mapsto \widehat{Gr}_F(\alpha)$, $\varphi \mapsto \varphi_*$ termwise ⟦Chapter18/OrientationCategory · GCT.VectorBundle.grFHatFunctor⟧, with $\hat\pi$ ⟦Chapter18/OrientationCategory · GCT.VectorBundle.grFHatProj⟧ and the printed morphisms $(\varphi, \hat L)$ ⟦Chapter18/OrientationCategory · GCT.VectorBundle.GrFMor⟧; the elements of $\mathrm{Lin}(f, \alpha)$ with their orientations ⟦Chapter18/OrientationCategory · StrongPolyfoldBundleConstruction.orFredPush_linOrLift⟧; witness ⟦Chapter18/OrientationCategory · ReflectionBundle.reflection_grFHat⟧. The chapter's statements do not use it, and the chart-level orientations of $O_f$ (§XVIII.21) are not identified with the rays of the determinant on $\mathrm{Lin}(f, \alpha)$.
- **Nothing to formalize.** G.244, the invitation after Theorem 18.1.12 to transport other results on auxiliary norms (§XVIII.11); G.247, the invitation in the introduction of §18.3 to treat the remaining concepts (§XVIII.15); G.249, the comment after Proposition 18.4.3 on sums and push-forwards, whose content the chapter uses is the push-forward from a model, the sums of §XVIII.16 and Theorem 18.4.4 (§§XVIII.19, XVIII.20, XVIII.24); G.255, the exercises on Theorems 5.3.12 and 5.3.15 for ep-groupoids and polyfolds (§XVIII.26).
- *Settled points of this chapter.* G.235 (Remark 18.1.2 (a), §XVIII.2), G.236 (the independence of (18.1), §XVIII.3), G.237 (Definition 18.1.4, §XVIII.4), G.238 ((18.2) and $\varphi_*$, §XVIII.5), G.239 (the proof of Theorem 18.1.5, §§XVIII.6–XVIII.8), G.240 (Remark 18.1.6, §XVIII.8), G.241 (Lemma 18.1.8, §XVIII.9), G.242 (the paragraph after Definition 18.1.9, §XVIII.10), G.243 (the paragraph before Theorem 18.1.12, §XVIII.11), G.246 (the observation after Definition 18.2.3, §XVIII.13), G.248 (Proposition 18.4.3, §XVIII.19), G.250 ($\bar\Psi_*$ and the germ identity, §XVIII.22), G.251 (Remark 18.5.5, §XVIII.23), G.252 (Proposition 18.5.6, §XVIII.23), G.253 (the proof of Theorem 18.6.1 in a smooth model, §§XVIII.24, XVIII.25), G.254 (Theorem 15.3.9 in the polyfold context, without "moreover tame", §XVIII.26).
- *Not concluded.* The "moreover tame" of Theorem 15.3.9 in the polyfold context, as in Chapter 15 (§XVIII.26).
- *Reading points.* Partitions of unity on the object M-polyfold of a model are the hypothesis of Theorems 18.4.4 and 15.3.9 in the polyfold context; Remark 18.4.2 compares Definition 18.4.1 between models in the ep-groupoid sense, and this M-polyfold notion is not compared between two models (§§XVIII.18, XVIII.20). The index on the support in the orientation clause of Theorem 18.6.1 is a hypothesis, and no witness of that clause has $\partial C \ne \emptyset$, since the half-space bundle carries no orientable section functor; that last step is argued in the documentation of the witness files and is not a formal theorem (§§XVIII.25, XVIII.27). Remark 18.2.5 has no witness with an object off level $1$, since every example of this Part is finite-dimensional (§XVIII.13).

### XVIII.30 What the formalization taught us

**The model does not matter, and why.** The chapter's notions are all of one shape: a property of a strong polyfold bundle is the corresponding property of Chapters 12–15 in an sc-smooth model, and "the specific model does not matter". Formalized, this claim has one engine. Two covering families are compared through their union, along inclusions that are strong bundle equivalences, and through the comparison isomorphism $[\bar f_{c,c'}]$, whose orbit map is $|\bar\Gamma_{c'}|^{-1} \circ |\bar\Gamma_c|$. The equivalence invariance theorems of Chapters 10–15 then carry every notion along the inclusions, and the identification of the transported objects is made on orbits, since a section functor or an invariant function is determined by its values on orbits and $|\bar\Gamma|$ is injective. Remark 18.1.2 (a), the paragraphs after Definitions 18.1.9 and 18.3.1, Remarks 18.3.2, 18.3.4, 18.4.2 and 18.5.3, Proposition 18.5.6 and the independence of transversality and general position in Theorem 18.6.1 are instances.

**Lemma 18.1.8 needs the continuity of Definition 12.2.1.** Definition 18.1.7 translates Definition 12.2.1 clause by clause, but the continuity of $|N|$ on the $(0,1)$-level orbit space is not among the printed clauses, and the printed clauses do not imply it: multiplying an auxiliary norm by a discontinuous function $a \ge 1$ of the base keeps every printed clause and destroys the continuity in the model. So for the printed form, "⇒" of Lemma 18.1.8 does not hold as we read it, on the reflection bundle. With the continuity, which the text's phrase about importing the notion from the ep-groupoid context suggests, the lemma holds as an equivalence, and the model condition is exactly the printed form plus continuity. Definition 18.1.7 presumably includes this continuity.

**"Local" in Definition 18.1.4 is what makes $\mathrm{Lin}(f, \alpha)$ intrinsic.** With $s$ an sc⁺-section near $q_0$, as the text says, the set does not depend on $\bar\Psi$, all its elements have one index, and $\varphi_*$ preserves it, with no hypothesis: a local section near $q$ is transported to a local section near $q'$ along the transition sheets. With global sections the comparison of two uniformizers needs the transported sections to extend, which is a hypothesis; the local reading removes it.

**Good position to the boundary is intrinsic.** Remark 17.2.18 defines the partial cone in a model and pushes it forward; the cone $C_\alpha C$ and the reduced tangent space $T^R_\alpha C$ are independent of the uniformizer, of the chart and of the presentation, because the tangent of a transition is the identity in the transported chart. The codimension of $T^R_\alpha C$ is the degeneracy index, so the cone is proper exactly on $\partial C$, and good position of $\ker f'(\alpha)$ to $C_\alpha C$ is Chapter 3's good position of the germ of $f_{\bar\Psi}$ at $q_0$. On the half-space bundle the hypotheses of Theorem 18.1.5 hold at the boundary zero $(0, 1)$; at the corner, where the isotropy is $\mathbb Z/2$, no invariant section of that bundle can satisfy them, because the trivial action on the fibre forces the kernel of a surjective derivative to be $T^R_\alpha C$.

**Orientations need the model's tameness, and the isotropy decides orientability.** The determinant lines of Chapter 12 and their continuation (Theorem 6.6.17) need a tame base with sc-smooth bump functions; §18.5 inherits these hypotheses through its reference model. An isotropy element that reverses an orientation of the determinant line excludes section functors of $\sigma$: on the reflection bundle $-1$ acts by $-1$ both on the tangent line and on the fibre, so it preserves orientations and the diagonal section is orientable; on the half-space bundle $-1$ reverses the tangent space and acts trivially on the fibre, so no section functor there is orientable. This is a property of the example, and it is why the orientation clause of Theorem 18.6.1 is witnessed only with $\partial C = \emptyset$.

**Three readings of §18.6 come from the text itself.** Theorem 18.6.1 prints "sc-smooth section functor", and the preamble of §18.6 and the appeal to Theorem 15.3.7 make sc-Fredholm the standing assumption. The orientation clause leaves the dimension of the oriented $\Theta$ tacit; Theorem 15.4.3, which it rests on, states the index. And Theorem 18.4.4, "the categorical version of Theorem 14.1.9", uses partitions of unity in the sense of Theorem 14.1.9, on the object M-polyfold of a model, which implies the ep-groupoid sense of Definition 18.4.1 (Remark 18.4.2).

**Small readings.** Remark 18.2.5 needs only the lower semicontinuity of a reflexive auxiliary norm, not the local compactness of Definition 18.2.4. Remark 18.1.2 (c) in its direction "⇒" needs a strong bundle uniformizer at the object, the standing $\bar F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$. The sc-smooth strong bundle proper covering functor of Theorem 18.3.6 is read through models, a proper strong bundle covering of Definition 8.5.9 between models with the square of (17.106) commuting up to isomorphism.
